# Terrain & Biome Compendium (Full)

Each item: `Name: description`, one per line. Index line numbers in terrain-biome_index.md must match this file; regenerate or edit the index after any insertion.

## Sky & Aerial
### Atmospheric Layers
- Lower Sky (Troposphere): A turbulent, weather-filled band of air where clouds boil up, birds wheel, and low-altitude aircraft fight crosswinds and sudden storms. Rain, hail, and shifting gusts make every flight unpredictable. Players chart safe routes between fronts, hunt flocks, and gamble on shortcuts through brewing squalls.
- Stratosphere Calm: A thin, still, brilliantly bright upper air lying above all weather, where silence replaces the roar of storms. It suits high-altitude gliders and silently drifting balloons, though cold and thin air sap stamina. Players rest, scout from above, and discover rare high-flying creatures.
- Mesosphere Edge: A freezing near-space sky where the air is barely there and meteors streak and burn overhead against a curved blue-black horizon. Oxygen runs short, cold bites hard, and falling debris threatens craft. Players gather meteor fragments and push vehicles to their limits at the edge of the world.
- Above-the-Clouds Sunlit Expanse: An endless white cloud floor stretches beneath blazing, unfiltered sun, with distant peaks and towers poking through the haze. Cruising is safe and serene, but glare, sunburn, and hidden sky creatures keep travelers alert. Players find quiet trade routes, hidden landings, and secret mountaintop shrines.
### Cloud Terrain
- Cumulus Fields: Puffy white cloud mounds drift in soft rolling ranks, with gentle updrafts rising between them. Light creatures can bounce across the tops or hide inside the fluffy interiors. Players use them for stealth, shortcuts, and playful traversal, while sudden shifts in density can swallow careless flyers.
- Thunderhead Towers: Huge anvil-topped storm clouds rise like dark cathedrals, rumbling with violent updrafts, hail, and lightning that punish careless flyers. Rain curtains hang beneath, and the crackling interior hides electric creatures. Players risk the climb for storm-charged materials, or dodge them while racing across the sky.
- Cirrus Highlands: High, icy, wispy streaks of cloud form a shimmering, frost-lit landscape of thin air and strong steady winds. Footing is slippery, breath is short, and cold drains endurance quickly. Players ride the steady currents for fast travel and hunt rare ice crystals and wind spirits.
- Cloud Sea: A vast, flat layer of cloud spreads to the horizon like an ocean, with mountain peaks poking through as islands. Its smooth surface suits surf-like traversal and sudden hidden ambushes from creatures beneath. Players skim across it by board or sail and dive below to find secrets.
- Fog Banks: Dense, low clouds drift in slow walls of gray, cutting visibility to a few steps and muffling sound. They conceal cliffs, ships, and lurking predators until the last moment. Players navigate by sound, lantern, or instinct, and ambush encounters or hidden finds await within.
- Storm Wall: A towering, churning barrier of black cloud and constant lightning blocks the horizon and the routes beyond it. Winds shear and thunder hammers, testing hull durability and pilot nerve. Players must find weak points, upgrade their ships, or time a daring crossing to reach what lies on the far side.
- Eye of the Storm: An eerie, silent clearing opens in the heart of a hurricane, ringed by violent cloud walls that tower on every side. The calm is deceptive, hiding treasure, wrecked ships, or a guardian boss. Players break through the surrounding gauntlet for a rare moment of stillness and a rich reward.
### Wind Phenomena
- Jet Stream: A fast, roaring river of high-altitude wind flows in a single direction, speeding travel enormously for those who ride it. Weak or poorly built craft can be torn apart by its shear and turbulence. Players use it for long-distance jumps, racing, and daring escapes from pursuers.
- Updraft Column: A rising pillar of warm air shimmers over sun-baked ground or rock, lifting gliders, feathers, and creatures steadily upward. It can carry travelers to otherwise unreachable ledges and floating platforms. Players hunt for them to climb, circle with hawks, and reach high secrets.
- Tornado Funnel: A spinning, debris-filled vortex tears across the land, hurling objects, shredding structures, and dragging the unwary into the sky. The roar is deafening and the sky turns green. Players flee, shelter in cellars, or deliberately ride the funnel to reach otherwise inaccessible heights.
- Trade-Wind Corridor: A steady, predictable lane of breeze blows along a known route, rewarding sailing and flying with fast, safe travel. Its reliability makes it a magnet for merchants, convoys, and pirates waiting to ambush them. Players plan trade runs, defend shipments, and fight for control of the lane.
- Doldrums (dead air): A windless, stagnant zone where sails hang slack and craft sit motionless under a heavy, humid sky. Supplies dwindle, tempers fray, and strange things stir in the stillness. Players must scavenge, row, or bargain their way out while a stranded crew grows bored and desperate.
- Gale Canyon: A narrow channel of howling wind runs between rock or cloud walls, funneling gusts into a constant punishing roar. Travelers are pushed off course and toward jagged edges. Players brace, tether, or time their passage, finding hidden alcoves and nesting creatures in its sheltered pockets.
### Light & Electric Phenomena
- Aurora Curtain: Shimmering sheets of green and violet light ripple across the night sky, casting an eerie glow over everything beneath. The air hums with magical energy, and spirits are said to appear in the folds. Players use it to power enchantments, spot visions, and follow its glow to hidden places.
- Lightning Field: An open expanse crackles with constant strikes, studded with conductive spires, charged air, and scorched glass. Burst damage punishes the careless, and every metal item becomes a liability. Players harvest static energy, bait strikes onto enemies, and sprint between rods to cross safely.
- Rainbow Bridge: A shimmering arc of color spans a gap in the sky, solid enough to walk and bright enough to be seen from far away. It serves as a magical path or a hidden gateway to another realm. Players cross it for blessings, but guardians and fading light may test them.
- Sprite Storm: Silent red flashes leap upward from the tops of thunderclouds, glowing eerily in towering jellyfish-like shapes far above the normal lightning. They hint at strange energy creatures and untamed power. Players chase them for rare essences and risk strange effects on any craft that ventures near.
### Floating & Built Sky
- Floating Island: A chunk of earth hovers in open air, crowned with grass and trees, with waterfalls dripping into nothing below. Isolated wildlife has evolved in peculiar ways, free from outside influence. Players land to explore, forage rare plants, and uncover relics or hermits living apart from the world.
- Sky Archipelago: A cluster of floating islands is linked by ropes, bridges, and air currents, forming a scattered, airy kingdom. Each isle offers its own climate, creatures, and secrets. Players hop between them in exploration chains, trade supplies, and face rope-cutting raiders and sudden storms.
- Aerial Reef: A living or coral-like growth hangs suspended in the sky, drifting slowly among colorful flyers and gas-bladder creatures. Its tendrils sway in the breeze and its blooms glow at dusk. Players harvest rare materials and observe delicate ecosystems, while stinging polyps and predators lurk among the branches.
- Cloud City: A gleaming settlement built on or within great cloud banks, with pale spires, markets, docks, and winding stairs. Rival sky factions jostle for influence and trade. Players shop for exotic goods, take contracts, uncover intrigue, and sometimes fight for control of districts that float far above the ground.
- Airship Harbor: A bustling cluster of mooring towers and docks for dirigibles, thick with the smell of fuel and the clang of repair work. Cargo, traders, mechanics, and smugglers crowd every gantry. Players hire crews, upgrade ships, pick up jobs, and watch for pickpockets and rival captains.
- Sky Bridge: A long suspended span crosses a chasm or gap in the sky, swaying in the wind and creaking underfoot. Planks may be missing and railings frayed. Players cross cautiously while open to ambush from flyers or bandits, and may fight, cut, or defend the span to control passage.
- Sky Tree / Beanstalk: A colossal plant climbs up through the clouds, its trunk wide as a town and its leaves broad as fields. Branch platforms, giant fruit, and lofty hidden realms reward climbers. Players scale it in stages, fending off insects and birds, and find giants, nests, and treasures at the top.
- Aerial Ruins: Broken towers, bridges, and temples drift among the clouds, trailing torn banners and loose stone. They hold forgotten relics, nesting flyers, and crumbling platforms that give way underfoot. Players explore carefully for lost lore and loot, while ancient traps and wary guardians protect the secrets of a vanished sky civilization.
## Scale-Shifted
### Microscopic
- Cell Interior: A gelatinous chamber full of floating organelles, folded membranes, and humming energy factories, all lit with a soft inner glow. Defensive proteins guard against intruders and treat visitors as pathogens. Players navigate the cytoplasm, sabotage or repair structures, and hunt for the nucleus and its precious code.
- Bloodstream River: A red-lit, rushing current carries cells, pathogens, and debris past branching vessel walls and one-way valves. The pulse sets a steady rhythm to every movement. Players ride the flow to deliver cargo or reach distant organs, fending off white-cell patrols and clots that block the way.
- Petri Dish Jungle: Glowing colonies of bacteria and mold form bizarre landscapes of fuzzy domes, slimy ridges, and spreading rings under a sterile glass sky. Some patches are toxic, others rich with nutrients. Players forage, cultivate useful strains, and avoid fast-growing blooms that devour everything in their path.
- Dewdrop World: Lens-like water spheres cling to leaves, bending light into rainbows and hiding tiny swimmers inside. Sparkling refracted pathways appear and vanish as the sun moves. Players hop between drops, solve light puzzles, and avoid evaporation or drips that sweep them off the leaf.
- Moss Micro-Forest: Dense green filaments tower like trees over a damp, shadowed floor, dripping with moisture and swarming with life. Tardigrades, mites, and other tiny creatures roam its trunks and hollows. Players explore the canopy, forage spores, and survive hunting predators and sudden drying spells.
- Dust-Mite Plains: Soft fields of drifting fibers stretch across bedding or carpet, rippling with static and dusted with flakes of skin. Herds of mites graze lazily while larger predators prowl. Players cross the plains for hidden troves, avoiding sneezes, vacuum storms, and the sudden collapse of shifting fibers.
### Inside Bodies
- Giant's Stomach: An acid-pooled cavern of slimy folds, half-digested debris, and lurching walls that squeeze intruders. The air is hot and sour and the floor churns. Players hunt for swallowed treasures, climb to escape rising acid, and race the clock before the walls grind everything to paste.
- Ribcage Cavern: Curved bone arches rise around a thumping central chamber, echoing with heartbeats, breathing winds, and creaking sinew ropes. The whole space expands and contracts with each breath. Players climb the ribs and swing across ropes, timing movement to the rhythm while guarding against immune defenders.
- Neural Web: Branching glowing fibers carry electric pulses between bright nodes, forming a vast living network of thought. Signal hazards zap the unwary, and memory fragments drift like fireflies. Players ride signals, rewire connections, and collect or protect memories while avoiding corrupting surges.
- Vein Network: Pulsing tunnels with valve gates, steady flow currents, and cramped junctions twist through soft tissue. Defensive cells patrol the walls and attack foreign bodies. Players follow the current, open or close valves to reroute travel, and slip past guards to reach critical organs.
- Throat Tunnel: A wet, ridged passage with gusting breath, dangling flesh, and sudden swallowing surges that drag travelers downward. The walls glisten and flex with every swallow or cough. Players brace against the pulls, hide from violent gags, and time dashes to reach whatever lies beyond.
### Giant-World (household scale)
- Under the Table: A dim forest of chair legs and fallen crumbs, ruled by scavenging insects and looming giant feet. Shadows shift as people move above. Players sneak between legs, gather food scraps, and dodge stomps, sweeping brooms, and the sudden descent of a curious pet.
- Garden Grass Forest: Towering blades of grass rise overhead, holding dewdrop pools and ant trails in dappled sunlight. Spiders lurk in webs between stems, and birds swoop from above. Players forage nectar, ride beetles, and avoid mowers, sprinklers, and the shadow of a predator swooping down.
- Sink Basin Sea: A slick porcelain valley stretches around a drain whirlpool and beneath a faucet waterfall, with sudden floods of soapy water. The surface is slippery and gleaming. Players cross it carefully, fighting currents and bubbles, and risk being swept down the drain while chasing items and shortcuts.
- Attic Canyon: Dusty beams and stacked boxes form cliffs and ledges, strung with cobwebs and lit by thin shafts of light through cracked vents. Hidden heirlooms wait in forgotten trunks. Players climb and explore, finding relics and memories while rustling nesting creatures guard their territory.
- Toolbox Cavern: A metal-walled den of giant screws, wrenches, and pools of oil gleams dully in the dark. Sharp edges and rolling hazards make every step risky. Players scavenge useful parts, climb on handles and levers, and avoid being crushed by shifting tools when the box is moved.
- Drain Pipe Maze: Slimy, twisting tubes run with trickling water and rusty grates, echoing with drips and distant gurgles. Rats or worse wait inside, and sudden surges can flush everything away. Players navigate the dark junctions, hunt lost objects, and find hidden routes into the walls and underworld below.
### Toy & Miniature
- Toy Box Land: A cluttered playground of oversized blocks, wind-up soldiers, and plush hills sprawls across the floor, animated by childlike magic. Dolls and toys have wills of their own and sometimes shifting loyalties. Players join games, solve playful puzzles, and avoid the sudden return of a child.
- Model Railway Town: A tiny painted village boasts working tracks, tunnels, and trains that follow fixed, dangerous schedules. Little lamps glow and miniature citizens stand frozen in place. Players learn the timetable, ride or sabotage trains, and explore the hidden layout beneath the detailed surface.
- Diorama: A carefully staged scene features painted backdrops and frozen figures, hiding trick walls and glass boundaries that limit movement. Perspective tricks make distances deceptive. Players discover hidden mechanisms, break the illusion, and find how to step beyond the frame or help the figures finally move.
- Board-Game Board: A stylized, tiled landscape governs movement by dice, with card events and rules that bend the world. Spaces bring rewards, traps, or forced detours. Players gamble on rolls, exploit rule loopholes, and face rival pieces in a world where fate is literally decided by chance.
- Snow Globe: An enclosed glass dome holds swirling flakes and a tiny frozen town, glittering under a painted sky. A looming shake can trigger a sudden blizzard that rearranges everything. Players explore the small village, hunt for the way out, and brace when giant hands tilt the world.
- Terrarium: A sealed miniature ecosystem filled with humid air, moss, ferns, and tiny reptiles sits behind glass walls that limit escape. Light and heat are controlled from outside. Players survive the predators, tend the delicate balance, and look for a way past the lid or through the vents.
- Dollhouse: Multi-story rooms of miniature furniture and porcelain residents stand creepy, silent, and watchful, with hidden passages behind walls and mirrors. Dust and old tea sets fill the corners. Players explore floor by floor, uncover the family secrets, and find out whether the dolls are guardians or captors.
## Time & Dimensional
- Time-Frozen Zone: An area where all motion halts mid-action, leaving suspended people, hanging raindrops, and frozen projectiles in eerie silence. Players can walk freely among them, studying scenes and moving objects. They might steal, rescue, or rearrange the moment, while fearing the instant the stillness breaks.
- Time-Looped Valley: A region resets after a set period, trapping inhabitants in repeating days while only a few remember what happened. Knowledge carried between cycles is the real currency. Players learn schedules, prevent disasters, and slowly piece together the cause of the loop to finally break free.
- Accelerated Time Zone: An area where days pass in seconds, with ruined structures crumbling, plants growing at visible speed, and aging hazards ticking away. Everything changes rapidly. Players rush to finish tasks before time takes its toll, or use the speed to grow crops, rot enemies, and age useful things.
- Slowed Time Zone: A sluggish area where movement drags, projectiles crawl through the air, and sounds stretch into low moans. Tension builds as every action feels heavy. Players exploit the slowdown for stealth and sniping, dodging bullets by hand, while enemies inside gain the same advantage.
- Temporal Rift: A jagged tear in time spits out relics, creatures, and echoes from various eras, with unstable footing around its edges. Strange weather and flickering light mark its presence. Players collect anachronistic loot and fight displaced beings, and may try to seal the rift or enter it.
- Era Overlap Zone: A place where past and future structures and people coexist, ancient ruins standing beside neon towers and rusted machines. Mixed enemies and loot appear in odd combinations. Players trade across ages, meet confused locals, and exploit anachronistic weaknesses as eras fight for dominance.
- Parallel Mirror World: A reversed duplicate of the known land with altered rules, evil doubles, and inverted geography where left becomes right. Familiar places hold twisted versions of friends and foes. Players hunt for the differences, outwit their doubles, and seek the reflective doorway home.
- Pocket Dimension: A small, self-contained space is accessed through a door, book, or object, often serving as a private vault or hideout. Its size and nature reflect its maker. Players store treasures, rest in safety, or discover that an unwelcome occupant or a decaying boundary awaits within.
- Fold in Space: A shortcut where distance collapses, linking distant places through disorienting overlaps and sudden teleports. The scenery stutters and doubles at the seams. Players use it to cross continents in a step, but unstable folds may drop them somewhere dangerous, or split the party apart.
- Portal Hub: A central chamber of many gateways to different worlds, each shimmering with its own light and weather. Gatekeepers regulate passage and bustling travelers mill about. Players choose destinations, bargain for keys, and navigate politics and smuggling while mistakes can send them to unfriendly realms.
- Shifting Maze Dimension: A realm of ever-changing corridors that rearrange themselves, demanding adaptive navigation and punishing memorized routes. Walls slide, doors vanish, and stairs loop. Players track patterns, mark paths, and outwit the maze's will, while the creatures inside move through it with ease.
- Echo of the Past: A ghostly replay of a historical moment that players can witness, interact with, and sometimes alter. Figures repeat their actions in translucent light, unaware of visitors. Players learn secrets, change outcomes, and risk paradox or being trapped in the memory as it fades.
## Weather & Seasonal (modifier layer)
### Precipitation
- Rain: Steady falling water dampens sound, darkens the ground, slows fire, and swells streams into muddy torrents. The world takes on a quiet gray mood. Players find easier stealth and tracking issues, struggle with wet gear and slick footing, and may need to find shelter or harvest water and growth.
- Downpour: A heavy deluge sharply reduces visibility, floods low areas, muddies roads, and drowns out footsteps and shouts under constant roaring noise. Rivers rise quickly and paths wash out. Players take shelter or risk the storm for stealth, while ambushes and flash floods become real dangers.
- Drizzle: A light, persistent, mist-like rain gives a gloomy mood, slick surfaces, and mild visibility loss that never quite lifts. Everything feels damp and heavy. Players endure low morale and slow travel but enjoy softened sound, easy mushrooms, and subtle atmosphere for noir mysteries.
- Snowfall: Soft falling snow gathers underfoot, muffles noise, and blankets the land in quiet white. Fresh tracks are easy to follow and cold slowly saps warmth. Players track prey and enemies, slog through drifts, build fires, and enjoy a peaceful but potentially deadly season.
- Blizzard: Howling wind-driven snow creates whiteout visibility, bitter cold, and rapid exposure damage that can kill the unprepared. Landmarks vanish and sound is swallowed. Players hunker down in shelters, rope together to travel, and hope to survive until it passes, while predators and lost travelers emerge.
- Hailstorm: Pelting ice stones hammer the land, damaging exposed travelers, denting armor, and shredding crops and tents. The clatter is deafening and cover is vital. Players dash for shelter, use shields overhead, and salvage ice afterward, while farmers and herds suffer losses that drive story events.
- Ice Storm: Freezing rain coats everything in a glassy glaze, dropping branches, snapping power lines, and making footing treacherous. The world sparkles beautifully and dangerously. Players slide and fall, find passages blocked, and use the ice for new routes, while the cracking of trees signals ongoing danger.
- Freezing Fog: Supercooled mist coats surfaces in rime, hides landmarks, and slowly chills those within as frost builds on gear and hair. Sound carries oddly and shapes loom. Players navigate carefully, avoid frostbite, and meet lurking creatures, with spooky atmosphere and hidden encounters adding tension.
### Wind Events
- Sandstorm: A wall of blowing grit that blinds travelers, scours exposed skin, and buries camps and tracks beneath shifting drifts. Visibility drops to arm's length and the roar drowns out all signals. Players must find shelter, lash down gear, and navigate by rope or compass, or risk losing caravans and landmarks entirely.
- Dust Storm: A dry brown cloud of lifted topsoil that chokes lungs, reduces sight to a few paces, and fouls machinery with fine grit. It often follows drought or plowed, barren fields. Players cover their faces, protect engines and filters, and may find buried ruins or lost objects exposed once the air clears.
- Haboob: A towering, churning wall of dust rolling across arid land, arriving suddenly and engulfing caravans before they can react. The air turns dark and electric, then thick with choking sand. Scouts can spot the advancing front hours away, giving prepared players a tense race to find cover.
- Hurricane: A massive rotating coastal storm bringing storm surge, flying debris, and flooded streets that last many hours. Winds tear roofs apart while the eye offers a deceptive, eerie calm. Players board up shelters, evacuate or ride it out, then face rescue missions, looting dangers, and broken settlements afterward.
- Typhoon: A powerful ocean cyclone battering ports and ships with huge waves, torrential rain, and fierce gusts that snap masts. Harbors become death traps as vessels grind together. Sailors must run for open water or safe anchorage, and survivors may wash ashore with salvage, secrets, or desperate stories.
- Cyclone: A broad, spiraling storm system that tears roofs from buildings, uproots trees, and drives surging seawater across low-lying coasts and plains. Warning signs include falling pressure and strange skies. Players race to reinforce shelters or flee inland, then rebuild amid wreckage, stranded survivors, and opportunistic raiders.
- Tornado Alley: Open plains regularly hit by twisting funnels that appear out of towering storm clouds with little warning. The sky turns green and the air goes still before devastation strikes. Players can go storm chasing for rare lore or resources, hunt cellars for shelter, and pick through flattened farmsteads afterward.
- Squall Line: A narrow band of sudden, gusty storms sweeping across land or sea, dropping the temperature and whipping up waves in minutes. Dark cloud banks and a cold wind give only brief warning. Sailors and riders caught unprepared lose cargo or boats, while alert players can time crossings between fronts.
### Temperature Events
- Heat Wave: An oppressive stretch of scorching days that drains stamina, spoils food, and cracks dry ground, driving people toward shade and water. Nights bring little relief. Players ration water, travel at dawn and dusk, and may find tempers fraying in settlements, with exhausted animals and tempting but dangerous mirages.
- Cold Snap: An abrupt plunge in temperature that freezes water, cracks pipes, and threatens unprepared travelers and livestock with frostbite and exposure. Breath hangs white and metal burns bare skin. Players scramble for fuel, furs, and warm shelter, and may rescue stranded neighbors or find frozen routes suddenly open.
- Hard Frost: A killing freeze that blackens tender crops, glazes the ground in white crystals, and makes surfaces brittle and dangerously slippery. Pale mornings sparkle but hide hazards. Farmers lose harvests or race to cover fields, while travelers risk falls, and frozen ponds and streams briefly become walkable.
- Drought: A long dry spell of cracked earth, failing wells, shriveled crops, and mounting wildfire risk that drives desperate migration toward remaining water. Rivers shrink to muddy trickles and old secrets surface. Players ration supplies, bargain for water rights, and may uncover sunken ruins or face conflict over scarce springs.
- Flood Season: A period of swollen rivers breaching banks, drowning fields, isolating villages, and washing away bridges and roads. Brown water carries debris, livestock, and sometimes stranded survivors. Players ferry people and goods by boat, rebuild crossings, and later harvest rich silt, while predators and disease spread through the mud.
### Visibility
- Thick Fog: Heavy white murk that limits sight to a few steps, muffles sound, and distorts direction. Familiar landmarks vanish and shapes loom suddenly. It is ideal for ambushes, smuggling, and getting thoroughly lost, so players rely on ropes, bells, lanterns, or careful tracking to move safely.
- Mist: A light, low haze that softens distances, hides small details, and adds mood without actually blocking movement. It clings to rivers, meadows, and forest floors at dawn. Players can use it for gentle stealth and atmosphere, glimpse vague silhouettes, and stumble onto quiet encounters or secluded, eerie places.
- Smog: Foul yellow-gray air from industry, fires, or volcanic vents that causes coughing, dim light, and steady health penalties. It stains buildings and hangs low in valleys. Players wear masks or filters, hunt for cleaner air, and may investigate the polluting sources, black markets for respirators, and sickened communities.
- Ash Fall: Gray flakes drifting from volcanoes or great fires that coat the land, clog lungs and machinery, and darken the sky at midday. Roofs collapse under the weight and streams run murky. Players cover their faces, shovel shelters clear, and flee or scavenge settlements buried beneath the soft, silent gray.
- Whiteout: Blinding snow or glare that removes all contrast and depth, making orientation nearly impossible and sky and ground blend together. Travelers walk in circles and drift off cliffs unseen. Players must anchor to guide lines, dig in and wait, or gamble on a blind march toward distant shelter.
### Electrical
- Thunderstorm: A dark, rumbling storm with lightning strikes, heavy rain, and gusty wind that threatens tall objects, lone trees, and open ground. Flash floods and hail can follow. Players seek low, sheltered cover, avoid metal, and may harvest lightning-struck materials or witness sudden, spectacular strikes on landmarks.
- Dry Lightning: A rainless electrical storm that ignites tinder-dry forests and grasslands, spreading wildfire across the landscape before anyone notices. Distant flashes and a sulfurous smell warn of danger. Players race to dig firebreaks, evacuate wildlife and villages, or exploit the chaos while smoke fills the horizon.
- Static Storm: Crackling charged air that disrupts electronics, raises hair, sparks off metal, and zaps unprepared travelers with sudden shocks. Blue glows dance along masts and weapons. Players protect delicate gear, ground their vehicles, and may harness the energy, or discover how machines and magic misbehave under the surge.
### Seasons
- Spring Thaw: Melting snow swells streams, softens the ground into mud, and awakens animals and blossoms after a harsh winter. Meltwater brings floods and uncovers long-buried items. Players find roads reopening, new forage and young creatures appearing, and trade resuming, while hungry predators emerge and ice cracks underfoot.
- Summer Bloom: Long warm days of lush growth, abundant forage, buzzing insects, and thriving trade and travel across open roads. Fields, forests, and meadows teem with life. Players gather food, hunt, and journey far, though heat, biting insects, festivals, and bustling crowds bring their own complications and opportunities.
- Autumn Fall: Golden leaves, harvest bounty, migrating birds, and cooling nights as communities prepare for winter. Markets overflow and fairs fill the roads. Players stock supplies, hunt fattened game, and finish journeys before the weather turns, while shortening days and falling leaves cover tracks and hidden hazards.
- Deep Winter: Harsh frozen months of short days, scarce food, impassable roads, and hungry predators drawn toward settlements. Snow muffles the world and rivers freeze solid. Players survive on stored supplies, hunt desperately, huddle by fires, and may cross ice shortcuts or face isolated hamlets with dark secrets.
- Wet Season: Extended rains swell rivers, flood plains, and spread disease, while unlocking lush growth and opening new waterways. Skies stay gray and everything smells of damp earth. Players ferry across drowned lowlands, fight fevers and rot, and find rare plants, fish, and routes unavailable in drier months.
- Dry Season: Parched months of low water, rising dust, and easy overland travel, with high wildfire risk and animals clustering at shrinking waterholes. Rivers become crossable and old tracks appear. Players trek far and fast, ambush game at water sources, and compete or bargain over every dwindling spring.
- Monsoon Season: A seasonal wind shift bringing torrential rains, landslides, flooded fields, and steamy warm air that never quite dries. Roads wash out and rivers turn violent. Players endure leaking shelters and isolation, ride flooded valleys by boat, and watch crops flourish while rescue and repair become constant duties.
- Mud Season: A sloppy thaw or rain period where roads bog down, wagons stick, boots sink, and travel crawls to a frustrating halt. Everything is smeared brown and smells of wet earth. Players face delays, stuck caravans, and easy pursuit tracks, while traders profit from scarcity and hardy locals offer shortcuts.
### Day & Sky Cycle
- Dawn: Soft rising light, dewy ground, waking birds, and a cool, quiet stillness that makes a short window favoring early raids and travel. Shadows are long and mist often lingers. Players can catch sleeping guards or prey off guard, start journeys with a fresh advantage, and see distant landmarks emerge.
- Midday: Bright overhead sun with short shadows, intense heat, and peak activity and visibility across the land. Little can hide and crowds fill markets and roads. Players travel and trade freely but suffer glare and exhaustion in hot climates, and stealth becomes difficult without careful cover or clever distractions.
- Dusk: Fading orange light with lengthening shadows, bats emerging, and creeping danger as night approaches and temperatures drop. Fires and lanterns flicker on in the distance. Players hurry to find shelter or camp, face the first nocturnal hunters, and may find the best moment for quiet meetings.
- Midnight: The deepest dark hours, favoring stealth, nocturnal predators, spirits, and an eerie silence broken by distant, unidentified sounds. Few people walk abroad and most doors are barred. Players sneak, steal, or meet secret contacts, while ghosts, beasts, and rituals grow bolder and sleepers are easily vulnerable.
- Solar Eclipse: The moon blocks the sun, bringing an eerie twilight, confused animals, falling temperatures, and omens or magical power surges. Birds fall silent and strange shadows appear. Players may witness rituals, exploit sudden darkness for ambushes, or race to complete ancient ceremonies timed to the rare event.
- Polar Night: Weeks of continuous darkness in far latitudes, lit only by stars, aurora, and fires, with brutal cold and isolation. Time loses meaning and tempers fray. Players rely on lamps and stores, hunt by moonlight, endure madness and cabin fever, and uncover things that thrive or hide in the long dark.
- Midnight Sun: Weeks of continuous daylight in far latitudes, disrupting sleep and exposing hidden things at all hours with a low golden glare. Nights offer no cover for stealth. Players enjoy long journeys and extended work, yet face exhaustion, strange moods, and the difficulty of hiding or resting anywhere.
- Blood Moon: A rare crimson lunar eclipse inspiring dread, empowering monsters and rituals, and warping tides and tempers across the land. Red light stains everything and animals howl. Players might face stronger beasts, interrupted cults, or unique prizes available only beneath that ominous, glowing sky.
## Space & Celestial
### Orbital
- Low Orbit: The swift fringe of space above a planet, with a glowing curved horizon, faint atmospheric drag, crowded satellites, and fast reentry dangers. Sunrises flash by every hour or so. Players dock at stations, dodge junk, repair satellites, and plan risky descents or launches from the surface below.
- Geostationary Belt: A ring of fixed-position satellites and stations far above the equator, hovering over one spot below and ideal for relays and secret outposts. Traffic is sparse and orderly. Players service communications hubs, hide in quiet slots, or intercept signals and cargo moving between world and space.
- Debris Field: A swirling cloud of wrecked hulls, broken satellites, and shrapnel that shreds ships and provides scavenging opportunities for the bold. Fragments move at lethal speeds and chain reactions are possible. Players pick through wrecks for salvage and secrets while avoiding collisions and hidden salvage pirates.
- Lagrange Point: A gravitationally balanced pocket in space where objects stay put with little fuel, perfect for stable stations, hidden bases, and drifting wrecks. Debris and dust gather quietly there. Players build outposts, smuggle goods, or find forgotten ships and ancient probes parked in the calm.
- Orbital Ring: A gigantic habitat or structure encircling a planet, with docking spokes, space elevators, and layered districts of industry, housing, and trade. Spinning sections create artificial gravity. Players navigate bustling markets, rival factions, and engineering marvels, while sabotage, smuggling, and politics thrive in its corridors.
### Planetary Surfaces
- Lunar Maria: Smooth, dark basalt plains pocked with small craters and covered in fine gray dust that clings to everything. Silence, harsh sun, and black skies dominate. These flats are ideal for landing bases and rovers, offering mining and construction sites, though dust abrades suits and long exposed trips are dangerous.
- Lunar Crater Field: Rim-ringed pits and rubble fields that provide cover, shadowed ice deposits, and treacherous low-gravity walking. Sharp shadows hide drops and loose slopes. Players hunt water ice in permanently dark floors, ambush from rims, and risk falls, fading light, and long, cold nights.
- Lunar Highlands: Bright, rugged uplands of ancient crust with jagged ridges, steep slopes, and abundant rock for mining. Light reflects harshly off pale peaks. Players establish observatories and quarries, scout long vistas of Earth or stars, and navigate rough terrain where low gravity makes both climbing and falling dangerous.
- Lunar Lava Tubes: Huge underground tunnels left by old volcanism, sheltering colonies from radiation and meteorites and holding hidden resources. Their vast dark chambers stay stable in temperature. Players build secure bases, explore unmapped caverns, and risk collapses, lost routes, or discovering unexpected ancient or alien traces.
- Martian Red Plains: Rust-colored dusty flats with scattered rocks, thin air, and dust devils sweeping the horizon under a butterscotch sky. Fine grit gets into everything. Players drive rovers, hunt for water and minerals, and endure seasonal dust storms while scanning for ruins, probes, or hidden entrances.
- Martian Canyon: A colossal rift valley with layered cliffs, a windswept floor, and possible ice, caves, or ancient traces of old water. The scale dwarfs any earthly canyon. Players rappel the walls, explore side caves for resources or secrets, and face landslides, thin air, and long, exposed descents.
- Martian Giant Volcano: An enormous shield mountain with gentle slopes, sheer cliffs, vast calderas, and cold thin air that makes climbing exhausting. The summit rises above most dust. Players trek or fly to its rim, explore lava tubes and vents, and may find rare minerals or hidden installations.
- Martian Polar Cap: A frozen ice dome layered with dust and carbon dioxide frost, carved by spiral troughs and bitter cold that seasonally shifts. Pale ice glitters under a weak sun. Players drill cores for water and ancient climate secrets, battle brutal storms, and risk crevasses, whiteouts, and vanishing seasonal frost.
- Venus Hellscape: A crushing, acid-clouded furnace of lava plains and volcanoes where heat and pressure destroy gear within hours. The sky glows sickly orange and rain evaporates before landing. Players need specialized survival tech to descend, grab precious samples, and flee before everything melts or implodes.
- Gas Giant Atmosphere: Banded storms, crushing pressure, and lightning in endless cloud layers with no surface to stand on, home to floating exotic life. Winds scream at impossible speeds. Players pilot sturdy airships or probes, harvest rare gases, and risk being swept down, struck, or hunted by drifting sky creatures.
- Ice Giant Depths: A dark blue slush of methane and water under immense pressure, with diamond rain and strange currents far below the hazy clouds. Cold and crushing forces threaten any craft. Players send hardened probes to mine exotic materials, chase mysterious signals, and encounter odd phenomena nobody has charted before.
- Ice Moon Crust: A fractured frozen shell with ridges, geysers, and glittering plains, hiding oceans below and subject to harsh radiation from its parent planet. Plumes erupt into vacuum. Players drill through ice, harvest water, investigate cracks and vents, and risk collapse, cold, and radiation exposure.
- Subsurface Ocean Moon: A dark, warm sea beneath an ice shell, with hydrothermal vents and strange alien biology thriving in perpetual night. Pressure and isolation are constant dangers. Players descend in submersibles through the ice, study unfamiliar ecosystems, and face monstrous predators, hidden cities, or fragile first contact.
- Tidally Locked Terminator Zone: A narrow twilight strip between a scorched day side and a frozen night side, ideal for habitation but with a permanently low sun. Winds howl between the extremes. Players build settlements, farm the dim light, and mount expeditions into either deadly half for resources and secrets.
- Rogue Planet Dark: A starless wandering world in permanent night, lit only by volcanic heat, glowing life, or faint cosmic light. Cold is relentless and shadows are absolute. Players explore by lamp and thermal vision, find unusual ecosystems or hidden civilizations, and risk getting lost far from any sun.
- Dwarf Planet Plain: A distant, frozen expanse of nitrogen ice and dim sunlight, with cliffs, craters, and an eerie silence under a black sky. The sun is only a bright star. Players scout slow surface routes, drill ancient ice for rare volatiles, and find remote outposts or lonely wrecks.
- Comet Surface: Crumbly dirty ice with jets of gas, weak gravity, and a long glowing tail that grows as it nears the sun. The ground is dark and fragile. Players anchor themselves, mine volatiles and organics, and ride the comet through erupting vents, sudden cracking, and shifting, treacherous terrain.
- Asteroid Surface: Rocky low-gravity terrain of craters and boulders, ideal for mining, hiding, and hazardous jumps that can fling the unwary into space. Tiny worlds spin quickly and horizons are close. Players tunnel for metals, hide bases or pirates, and carefully tether themselves while hopping between outcrops.
### Deep Space
- Asteroid Belt: A ring of drifting rocks of all sizes, offering mining, ambushes, and tricky navigation between tumbling hazards. Most space between them is empty but collisions threaten. Players stake claims, hide from pursuers among the rubble, and fight pirates or rivals over rich metal and ice deposits.
- Kuiper Ring: A dim, icy frontier beyond the outer planets, scattered with frozen worlds, comets, and lonely outposts under a distant, tiny sun. Travel takes months and communication lags. Players stake remote claims, search for dwarf planets and ancient wrecks, and endure isolation, scarcity, and mysterious signals.
- Oort Cloud: A vast, sparse shell of icy bodies at the edge of the system, remote, cold, and almost empty, with objects separated by enormous distances. The sun is just another star. Players undertake epic voyages, find hidden stations or dormant ships, and brave solitude, dark silence, and fragile life support.
- Nebula: A glowing, colorful gas cloud where stars are born, dimming sensors and hiding ships in shimmering mist. Pillars and filaments glow in impossible hues. Players navigate by instinct, ambush or evade foes in the haze, harvest rare gases, and risk radiation, static interference, and lurking creatures.
- Dark Nebula: A thick dust cloud blocking starlight, creating inky voids ideal for stealth, lurking creatures, and navigation trouble. Instruments struggle and stars vanish. Players hide fleets or bases within the gloom, hunt hidden prey, and risk collision or getting lost without beacons, lamps, or trustworthy charts.
- Star Nursery: A dense, glowing cluster where new stars ignite, with radiation, bright gas pillars, and protostar hazards in a restless cosmic landscape. Young stars blaze fiercely and flares are frequent. Players study birth events, harvest exotic gases, and dodge deadly bursts while rival expeditions compete for scientific glory.
- Pulsar Field: A region swept by rapid beams of lethal radiation from spinning neutron stars, creating a timing-based hazard to navigate. Beams strike like lighthouse sweeps through the void. Players time their crossings, use shielded hideouts, and exploit the precise rhythm for navigation, communication, or deadly traps.
- Accretion Disk: A blazing spiral of superheated matter orbiting a black hole or star, gravitationally treacherous and intensely radioactive. Light and heat blaze in glowing bands. Players skirt its edge for rare energy and resources, flee tidal forces, and risk being torn apart or cooked by radiation.
- Event Horizon: The point of no return around a black hole, where time warps, light bends, and escape fails for anything that crosses. The view stretches into strange distortions. Players risk extreme experiments, daring slingshots, and desperate rescues, or uncover mysteries that appear to vanish beyond the edge.
- Neutron Star Surface: An ultra-dense, glowing crust with crushing gravity, extreme magnetism, and mountains only millimeters tall that shape tiny, brutal landscapes. Radiation and spin are extreme. Players, probably exotic beings or machines, study impossible physics, mine unimaginable matter, and risk instant destruction by the slightest error.
- Binary Star Gap: The unstable space between two orbiting suns, with double shadows, tidal stresses, and shifting light that changes color and warmth. Orbits are chaotic and hard to predict. Players find unusual planets and stations, trade between the stars, and navigate delicate gravitational currents or sudden flares.
- Supernova Remnant: An expanding shell of glowing wreckage from a dead star, rich in heavy elements and radiation, glittering in vivid colors. Shockwaves still ripple through the debris. Players mine rare metals, study the aftermath, and brave harsh radiation and drifting wrecks of civilizations destroyed by the explosion.
- Cosmic Void: A vast, almost empty gulf between galaxies, cold and silent, with distant lights and isolated drifters. Nothing lies within a long travel time of any ship. Players undertake desperate crossings, find lone relics and stations, and confront deep solitude, supply shortages, and sparse, eerie signals.
- Galactic Core: The dense, blazing, star-packed center of a galaxy, with intense radiation and a supermassive black hole at its heart. The sky is bright with countless suns. Players seek ancient artifacts and rare resources, endure constant danger, and encounter powerful factions and cosmic mysteries.
- Galactic Halo: A sparse outer sphere of ancient stars and globular clusters surrounding a galaxy, quiet and mysterious, with old, metal-poor suns. Travel is slow and traffic is rare. Players discover forgotten colonies, ancient ruins, and hidden refuges, and face the loneliness of the galaxy's distant, dim fringe.
- Wormhole Throat: A shimmering tunnel entrance bending space, offering instant long-range travel with an unstable, hazardous passage. Light twists and warps around the opening. Players seek safe passage, defend or exploit the crossing, and risk being lost, torn apart, or delivered to unknown regions or alternate realities.
- Solar Corona: A searing plasma halo around a star, whipping with flares and magnetic loops that destroy unshielded craft. The glow is blinding and heat is extreme. Players brave daring skims with heavy shielding to harvest energy or study the sun, racing flares and risking fatal exposure.
- Star Surface: A roiling, seething sea of plasma, sunspots, and prominences, survivable only by exotic or fiery beings. Everything is blinding light and unimaginable heat, with fountains of burning gas. Players may play as or meet star-dwelling creatures, enter spiritual realms, or perform legendary feats that mortals can scarcely imagine.
## Planes & Otherworldly (abstract planes)
### Dream & Mind
- Dreamscape: shifting surreal landscape shaped by thoughts, where logic bends, symbols walk, and gravity obeys feelings. Skies melt into staircases and doors open onto childhood bedrooms without warning. Players navigate by emotion and symbol, bargaining with dream-figures, and risk losing their sense of self if they linger too long.
- Nightmare Realm: twisted version of dreams full of fears made flesh, distorted geometry, and pursuing horrors. Corridors stretch and fold behind you while the thing you dread most always seems one step closer. Players must confront or outwit personal terrors, gaining hard-won resolve while sanity drains with every chase.
- Memory Palace: grand mental building of rooms holding stored memories, guarded by forgetfulness and reachable through recall puzzles. Each hall preserves a scene, scent, or voice in perfect detail, though some doors have faded or been walled off. Players hunt lost knowledge, unlock sealed rooms, and fend off the creeping decay of oblivion.
- Collective Unconscious: shared deep mind-space of archetypes, myths, and symbolic beings that influence everyone's dreams. Towering figures like the Hero, the Mother, and the Trickster drift through a twilight of half-remembered stories. Players can petition archetypes for insight or power, but each bargain subtly reshapes how mortals dream and believe.
- Mindscape: inner landscape of a single mind reflecting emotions, with personality aspects as inhabitants and landmarks. Anger may smolder as a volcano while grief pools as a gray lake, and old beliefs stand as crumbling monuments. Players enter to heal, interrogate, or manipulate a mind, facing its defenses and hidden traumas.
- Fever Dream: hot, delirious, warped landscape with melting walls, looping events, and unreliable perception. Sweat-soaked colors pulse, voices repeat themselves, and distances change each time you look away. Players struggle to tell real clues from hallucinations, escaping loops by spotting the one detail that never quite repeats.
### Spirit
- Astral Plane: silvery drifting realm beyond the body, where thought travels, cords connect, and strange entities roam. Travelers glide through pale light, tethered by a thin silver cord to their sleeping flesh. Players explore, spy across vast distances, and meet curious or predatory beings, while a severed cord means never returning home.
- Ethereal Mist: pale veil between worlds, hiding half-seen spirits, dampening sounds, and allowing ghostly passage. Shapes loom and vanish within the fog, and footsteps echo from nowhere. Players slip between realms to bypass obstacles or ambush foes, but lingering risks losing their way and becoming one more whisper in the haze.
- Spirit World: parallel realm of ancestors, nature spirits, and lost souls, mirroring the living world in eerie tones. Familiar forests and villages appear washed in muted color, populated by wise elders and wandering ghosts. Players seek guidance, strike bargains with spirits, and resolve unfinished business, while offending the wrong entity brings lasting curses.
- Shadow Realm: dim inverted echo of the material world, ruled by shades, secrets, and stealthy predators. Light sources gutter and fade, and every building is a darker, twisted copy of its counterpart. Players use it for stealthy shortcuts and hidden knowledge, but shade-lords hunt intruders and may claim their shadows.
- Ghost Layer: overlapping faint dimension where the dead linger, visible only through special sight and lingering emotions. Ruined rooms replay old tragedies as translucent figures repeat their final moments. Players can listen to the dead, uncover buried crimes, and ease restless spirits, but strong grief and rage can lash out dangerously.
### Elemental
- Plane of Fire: realm of molten rivers, burning skies, and flame-bodied beings where heat damages constantly. Obsidian cliffs rise above lakes of lava, and ash storms glow orange overhead. Players need protection or cunning to survive, seeking rare fire-forged materials and negotiating with proud efreet-like lords who respect strength.
- Plane of Water: boundless ocean realm with floating currents, drowning pressure, and fluid creatures with no solid ground. Spheres of water drift past shimmering shoals and slow, vast shapes in the depths. Players must breathe, swim, and navigate without landmarks, hunting pearls of power while tide-beings test every visitor's endurance.
- Plane of Earth: dense world of tunnels, crystal caverns, and living stone inhabited by slow, mighty beings. Veins of gems glitter in endless dark rock, and the ground itself shifts and rumbles. Players mine rare minerals and treat with ancient stone-folk, but cave-ins and crushing guardians punish carelessness and impatience.
- Plane of Air: endless storm-swept sky with floating shards, roaring winds, and fast whirling elemental beings. Islands of rock and cloud tumble through gale-force currents under a shifting horizon. Players leap between drifting platforms, ride updrafts, and race swift wind-beings, with every fall promising a very long drop.
- Plane of Lightning: crackling realm of arcing bolts, static fields, and charged beings that surge with speed and damage. Purple-white storm clouds flash constantly and metal hums with stored energy. Players harness lightning for power and speed, but exposed travelers face stunning shocks, and conductive gear can become a deadly liability.
- Plane of Ice: frozen glittering expanse of glaciers, frost spires, and crystalline beings with numbing cold. Pale blue light refracts through towering ice, and silence broken only by cracking glaciers. Players battle frostbite while mining rare frozen relics and negotiating with rigid, patient ice-lords who value endurance above all.
- Plane of Light: radiant dazzling realm of pure brightness, blinding purity, and radiant beings that scour darkness. Gleaming halls and golden plains offer no shadow to hide in. Players may find healing, revelation, and powerful allies here, but corrupt or secretive travelers are burned, exposed, or driven out by the unyielding glare.
- Plane of Shadow: murky realm of living darkness and whispering silhouettes, draining light and rewarding stealth. Shapes slide along the edges of vision, and torches shrink to feeble embers. Players sneak, ambush, and bargain with silent shade-beings for secrets, but prolonged exposure leaches warmth, memory, and color from the unwary.
### Void
- The Void: absolute emptiness beyond reality, devoid of light, sound, and matter, unsettling and hungry. Travelers feel their senses fade and their identity thin out as the nothingness presses in. Players brave it to reach forbidden places or seal rifts, risking being unmade, with only strange fragments of lost things drifting nearby.
- Limbo Between Worlds: gray in-between space of drifting fragments, lost travelers, and unstable portals to everywhere and nowhere. Broken pieces of cities and landscapes float in endless twilight, and time barely moves. Players hunt for a stable gateway, trade with stranded wanderers, and avoid portals that lead somewhere far worse.
- Abyssal Dark: bottomless lightless depth teeming with ancient horrors, crushing silence, and creeping madness. Pressure squeezes the lungs while unseen things stir far below. Players descend with scarce light and fragile sanity to recover sunken relics, knowing every glimpse of what lives down there leaves a lasting mental scar.
- Static Between Channels: buzzing glitchy noise-space of broken signals, flickering shapes, and half-heard voices. Fragments of old broadcasts and corrupted images flash by in a sea of hissing interference. Players tune into hidden messages, dodge signal-eating entities, and risk their minds being overwritten by the noise.
## Living & Creature-Based Terrain
- Giant Skeleton Valley: vast ribs and vertebrae of a dead colossus forming arches, caves, and bone-white canyon walls. Wind whistles through hollow bones, and ancient marrow caves shelter strange nests. Players explore this natural cathedral, scavenge relics from the remains, and wonder what could have killed something so enormous.
- Titan's Corpse Mountain: mountain range formed by a fallen giant, with veins of ore, rotting caves, and stirring remnants. Slopes of hardened flesh and stone hide rich metal veins and foul pockets of ancient decay. Players mine its riches and delve its caverns, but rumbles suggest something inside may not be entirely dead.
- World Turtle Back: enormous shelled creature carrying a continent on its slow-moving carapace, with shifting coastlines and tides. Settlements cling to ridges of shell while seasons follow the creature's ponderous journey. Players trade between regions, uncover myths about the beast, and face crises when it turns, dives, or finally stirs.
- Beast-Back Continent: land riding on a gigantic wandering animal, with fur forests, parasite villages, and tremor-prone terrain. Hairy woodlands sway with each step and rivers drain into creases of hide. Players cope with quakes and migrations, hunt monstrous parasites, and decide whether to guide, soothe, or exploit their living world.
- Sleeping Giant Hills: hills shaped like a slumbering colossus, with hidden breath vents and risk of waking. Gentle green slopes trace a vast chest and face, and warm steam hisses from secret fissures. Players explore its caves and ruins carefully, since noise, mining, or ritual could rouse the giant with catastrophic results.
- Dragon-Bone Wastes: barren plains strewn with huge skeletal remains, scavengers, treasure hunters, and lingering draconic magic. Sun-bleached ribs jut from dust like broken ships, and strange energy crackles in the old bones. Players harvest valuable scales and fangs, compete with rival hunters, and risk the curses and hoard-guarding things that remain.
- Bone Forest: stands of upright bones like trees, rattling in wind and sheltering ghouls, scavengers, and grim shrines. Pale trunks click and clatter overhead while fine bone dust coats everything. Players follow hidden trails between macabre shrines, recover grim relics, and defend against ghouls and cultists who consider the grove sacred.
- Living Forest: sentient woodland that shifts paths, whispers, and defends itself with grasping roots and watchful trees. Trails rearrange behind travelers and leaves murmur judgments in rustling voices. Players earn passage by showing respect or proving useful, while loggers, hunters, and fire-bringers face an angry, patient, and relentless wilderness.
- Flesh Cavern: pulsing organic tunnels of muscle and membrane, dripping fluids and guarded by immune-like monsters. Walls throb with a slow heartbeat, and warm acidic liquid pools in the folds. Players push deeper toward a vital organ or hidden prize while fighting off defenders that treat intruders as an infection.
- Hive Interior: waxy or resinous chambers of insectoid colonies, with swarming workers, royal chambers, and sticky traps. Hexagonal cells glow amber, and the air buzzes with constant movement and chemical signals. Players sneak past drones or fight through soldiers to reach a queen, seizing royal jelly and fragile eggs along the way.
- Mycelium Network: sprawling underground fungal web linking life, carrying signals and spores, and hosting glowing mushroom growths. Pale threads run through soil and stone, pulsing with faint light and quiet communication. Players tap the network for scouting and healing, or navigate it to far places, while spores and rival fungal minds may seek to control them.
- Carnivorous Garden: lush blooms and snapping plants luring prey with scent and color, hiding digestive pits and vines. The air is thick with sweet perfume, and bright petals conceal deadly jaws. Players harvest rare reagents and nectar while avoiding traps, rescuing swallowed victims, or striking deals with the garden's cunning keeper.
- Web-Filled Canopy: treetops draped in giant spider silk, with sticky walkways, wrapped victims, and lurking weavers. Thick strands span the branches like bridges, vibrating at the slightest touch. Players climb carefully, cutting through snares and rescuing cocooned captives, while huge spiders respond swiftly to any struggle on their web.
- Egg Chamber Nest: warm cavern of clustered eggs guarded by fierce parents, with nursery hazards and valuable loot. Humid heat and the smell of brooding creatures fill the air as eggs glow faintly in straw and bone. Players may sneak, steal, or fight for rare eggs and treasure, but returning parents are furious and deadly.
- Kraken-Back Island: deceptive island that is really a sleeping sea monster, erupting when travelers camp on it. Sandy shores, scrub, and tidepools hide a vast, slow-breathing body just beneath. Players may notice subtle warning signs like odd tremors or barnacled stone, and must flee for their ships when the island awakens and dives.
- Tendril Wilds: dense jungle of grasping vines and writhing feelers that snare, drag, and strangle unwary explorers. Moist green growth twitches in the corner of the eye, and the ground shifts with hidden roots. Players cut, burn, or sneak through while rescuing trapped companions, and may find the tendrils guard something worth the danger.
- Symbiote Grove: cooperative woodland of linked creatures and plants, trading resources and bonding with visitors in strange ways. Glowing vines, nesting animals, and flowering trees share nutrients and warnings in harmony. Players can forge bonds that grant unusual abilities, but each partnership has a price, and leaving a symbiote behind may prove painful.
## Confinement
- Dungeon: dark stone cells and torch-lit corridors beneath a castle, full of captives, traps, and sadistic guards. Damp walls weep, chains clink in the dark, and distant screams echo through barred doors. Players break out prisoners, bribe or evade jailers, and uncover buried secrets, or struggle to escape after being locked inside themselves.
- Oubliette: narrow pit prison with a single hatch above, designed to make prisoners vanish and be forgotten. Smooth cold walls offer no handholds, and light arrives only when the hatch opens for scraps. Players plan clever escapes using scraps and sounds, or rescue a forgotten figure whose knowledge could topple powerful people.
- Gulag Camp: remote frozen labor camp of barbed wire, watchtowers, and starvation, where inmates scheme to survive. Biting winds, thin rations, and relentless work grind prisoners down, while informants lurk in every barracks. Players trade favors, organize resistance, and plot an escape across a deadly white wilderness.
- Island Prison: isolated fortress on a rocky sea island, guarded by waves, patrols, and no easy escape. Cold surf pounds the cliffs, searchlights sweep the walls, and boats rarely arrive. Players work out guard routines and smuggle tools to flee, or infiltrate to free a prisoner, with the sea as a final obstacle.
- Asylum Ward: dim echoing hospital wing of padded rooms and restraints, haunted by restless patients and sinister experiments. Flickering lights, scratching sounds, and locked files hint at cruel treatments. Players investigate disturbing records, calm or evade troubled inmates, and discover whether the real horror lies in the staff or in the ghosts left behind.
- Panopticon: circular prison with a central watchtower seeing every cell, enforcing paranoia and demanding clever stealth. Inmates can never tell when they are watched, so even guards grow tense. Players exploit blind spots, dim lights, and distractions to move unseen, or sabotage the tower itself to break the system of surveillance.
- Labyrinth: winding maze of dead ends and false paths, guarded by a lurking monster at its heart. Walls shift, echoes mislead, and old bones mark the unlucky. Players map routes, solve riddles, and follow clues like thread or chalk marks, ultimately facing the creature or secret waiting at the center.
- Vault: reinforced secure chamber of locks, lasers, and guards, protecting treasure and tempting thieves. Thick steel doors, pressure plates, and layered alarms stand between intruders and the prize. Players assemble a crew, study the layout, and time every step of a heist, while a single mistake can seal them inside.
- Cell Block: rows of barred cells along walkways, with rowdy inmates, shakedowns, riots, and tense guard rotations. The air is loud with shouting and slamming doors, and favors are traded in whispers. Players navigate prison politics, hide contraband, and decide whether to lead a riot or keep their head down until release.
- Penal Colony: remote settlement of convict laborers under harsh rule, with brutal overseers, smuggling, and desperate escape plots. Shacks, fences, and work gangs spread across a harsh landscape with few supplies. Players barter for survival, expose corrupt officials, and risk wilderness or sea to flee, or may come to rule the colony themselves.
- Quarry Chain Gang: dusty pit where shackled prisoners break stone under armed watch, with rockfalls and rebellion. Hammers ring against rock as overseers patrol the terraces and sun beats down. Players endure punishing labor, sabotage equipment, and rally fellow prisoners for an uprising or escape, with cliffs and crumbling walls adding danger.
- Prison Ship: rotting hulk of crowded holds and barred decks anchored offshore, plagued by disease and mutiny. Dripping timbers, rats, and stale air make the dark holds miserable. Players tend the sick, trade secrets among prisoners, and plot a mutiny or swim for shore, while guards ruthlessly protect their floating fortress.
- Quarantine Zone: sealed-off area of checkpoints and hazmat gear, holding the sick and infected under armed enforcement. Barricades, spray stations, and watchful soldiers cordon off empty streets and crowded wards. Players sneak supplies in, search for a cure, or smuggle people out, while the line between protection and cruelty grows increasingly thin.
## Knowledge & Institutions
- Library Stacks: towering shelves and narrow aisles of dusty books, hiding lore, secret doors, and cranky guardians. Ladders climb into shadow, and the smell of old paper hangs over quiet reading nooks. Players search for crucial volumes, decode cataloging mysteries, and avoid stern librarians or enchanted books that resent being disturbed.
- Forbidden Archive: locked restricted collection of dangerous tomes, protected by wards, curses, and watchful keepers. Iron-bound cabinets hum with suppressed power and some books whisper from behind their locks. Players seek forbidden knowledge by cracking wards or deceiving keepers, and every discovery risks unleashing something that should stay buried.
- Scriptorium: quiet hall of scribes copying manuscripts by candlelight, with inks, parchment, and illuminated secrets. Desks line the hall under soft lamplight, and scratching quills fill the silence. Players commission or forge documents, decipher hidden marginalia, and uncover plots concealed in the careful work of the copyists.
- University Quad: green campus courtyard ringed by lecture halls, bustling with students, rivalries, and academic intrigue. Scholars debate on benches while clubs, societies, and secret fraternities recruit newcomers. Players pursue research, make allies, and navigate professor politics, uncovering scandals or hidden mysteries beneath the ivy-covered tradition.
- Wizard Academy: magical school of spire towers and enchanted classrooms, with apprentices, duels, and hazardous experiments. Staircases move, portraits comment, and spell practice occasionally sets something on fire. Players attend lessons, join rival houses, and investigate secrets in restricted wings, while rising from novice to master brings both status and danger.
- Observatory: domed hilltop building with telescopes and star charts, for stargazing, omens, and cosmic discoveries. A slit in the dome opens to the night sky, and brass instruments gleam in lamplight. Players track celestial events, interpret portents, and uncover signs of approaching disaster or distant worlds hidden among the constellations.
- Laboratory: benches of bubbling glassware, instruments, and dangerous specimens, with experiments gone wrong and secret research. Chemical fumes, humming machines, and caged subjects fill the cramped space. Players investigate failed projects, steal prototypes, or conduct their own experiments, with each breakthrough carrying the chance of explosion, contamination, or escape.
- Courthouse: formal hall of judges, juries, and records, driving trials, bribery, and legal drama. Polished benches and heavy oak doors frame arguments before stern magistrates. Players act as advocates, investigators, or defendants, gathering evidence, swaying juries, and exposing corruption, while a single verdict can reshape fortunes or send someone to the gallows.
- Parliament Hall: grand debating chamber of tiered benches and ornate speeches, where factions maneuver for power. Gavels pound and heckling rises as rival blocs trade votes and favors. Players lobby, blackmail, or deliver rousing speeches to pass laws, topple ministers, and expose conspiracies behind the scenes.
- Museum Halls: quiet galleries of relics, exhibits, and glass cases, with guards, hidden vaults, and tempting artifacts. Soft lighting and echoing footsteps fill rooms of preserved history. Players plan thefts, investigate curses clinging to exhibits, or protect priceless collections from rival thieves, with some displays holding more power than curators realize.
- Alchemist's Workshop: cluttered room of cauldrons, vials, and strange fumes, producing potions, poisons, and volatile experiments. Shelves sag under jars of odd ingredients while something green simmers in the corner. Players gather reagents, brew remedies and weapons, and trade secrets, but a wrong mixture can poison or explode the whole room.
- Research Station: isolated outpost of labs and sensors in a harsh environment, with scientists, secrets, and creeping dangers. Humming generators and flickering monitors keep the cold or heat at bay while storms isolate the crew. Players investigate strange readings, missing colleagues, and hidden projects as supplies dwindle and something outside draws closer.
- School Grounds: classrooms, playing fields, and hallways populated by students and teachers, with cliques, rules, and hidden mischief. Bells ring, lockers slam, and rumors race through the corridors. Players balance classes, friendships, and rivalries while investigating pranks, secrets, or darker mysteries lurking in the basement or after-hours halls.
- Hospital Ward: rows of beds, antiseptic corridors, and beeping machines, where healers work and patients hide secrets. Night shifts bring dim lights, squeaking carts, and strained whispers among exhausted staff. Players treat the wounded, guard vulnerable patients, or uncover suspicious deaths and illicit experiments, while contagion and shortages constantly threaten order.
## Entertainment & Recreation
- Gladiator Arena: sand-floored fighting pit with roaring crowds, trapdoors, and beasts, built for combat spectacle. Blood-stained sand and iron gates frame battles staged for cheering spectators. Players fight for glory and freedom, exploit trapdoors and rival weaknesses, and win the crowd's favor, while rigged matches and sponsors' schemes lurk behind the scenes.
- Colosseum: monumental tiered stone stadium hosting grand battles, executions, and political theater before cheering throngs. Towering arches, imperial boxes, and vast stands dwarf the combatants below. Players compete in mass spectacles, court powerful patrons, and survive intrigue, since a single thumb gesture from the crowd or ruler can decide fate.
- Amphitheater: open semicircular stage with tiered seating, perfect for performances, speeches, and acoustic tricks. Stone seats curve upward from a central stage where a whisper carries to the back row. Players deliver rousing speeches, stage plays, or ambush speakers, using the unusual acoustics and open sightlines to impress, eavesdrop, or hide messages.
- Circus Tent: striped big top with trapezes, animal acts, and clowns, hiding sinister secrets backstage. Sawdust rings, bright lights, and cheerful music cover cramped wagons and caged beasts behind the curtains. Players perform, investigate strange disappearances, or join the troupe, discovering that the greatest act may be hiding something truly unsettling.
- Carnival Midway: bright lane of rigged games, food stalls, and rides, with barkers, pickpockets, and creepy attractions. Neon bulbs, calliope music, and sugary smells draw crowds past dubious sideshows. Players win prizes, spot cons, and chase thieves, and a particular funhouse or fortune teller may involve them in something far stranger.
- Fairground: open field of stalls, contests, livestock, and music during festivals, bustling with traders and rowdy crowds. Banners flutter above pens of animals, craft booths, and contest rings. Players trade goods, enter competitions, gather rumors, and meet colorful characters, while pickpockets, rivalries, and brawls simmer beneath the cheerful commotion.
- Theme Park: sprawling attraction of rides, mascots, and queue mazes, ripe for malfunctions and abandoned-park horror. Giant coasters, cartoon characters, and winding queues stretch across themed zones. Players might enjoy lighthearted adventures or explore a derelict version where rusting rides, creepy mascots, and failed machinery threaten anyone who wanders in.
- Casino Floor: glittering hall of tables, slot machines, and watchful security, where fortunes turn and cheaters scheme. Chiming machines, smoky air, and endless free drinks keep patrons playing. Players gamble, count cards, or plan a heist, while watchful pit bosses and shady owners ensure that luck is rarely the only factor.
- Theater Stage: curtained platform with props, trapdoors, and lighting rigs, offering backstage intrigue and performative tricks. Painted backdrops and hanging ropes hide narrow catwalks and secret passages. Players act, sabotage, or investigate mishaps, using rehearsals and costumes to disguise themselves while jealous rivals and ghostly rumors complicate the show.
- Opera House: ornate auditorium of velvet boxes and chandeliers, with dramatic performances, hidden passages, and phantom rumors. Gilded balconies overlook a grand stage, while cellars and flies hide forgotten corridors. Players attend glittering galas, chase a mysterious figure, or uncover plots among the patrons, and the music itself may hold a secret.
- Racetrack: looping course with spectators, bets, and fast competitors, mixing speed, sabotage, and betting-ring intrigue. Dust or exhaust rises as racers thunder past screaming crowds and nervous gamblers. Players race, fix races, or place wagers, dodging crooked bookies and saboteurs while fighting for a championship prize.
- Sports Stadium: vast bowl of seating around a playing field, loud with fans, vendors, and rivalry. Floodlights glare over chanting crowds and the roar of home and away supporters. Players compete or scheme around the big game, dealing with hooligans, rigged matches, and star athletes whose fame creates both opportunity and trouble.
- Festival Grounds: colorful temporary village of tents, music, food, and games, packed with revelers and opportunists. Lanterns, drums, and savory smoke fill the air as visitors drift between stages and stalls. Players join contests, make deals, and chase rumors, while festival chaos offers cover for thieves, lovers, and conspirators.
- Tournament Lists: fenced jousting field with pavilions, heralds, and banners, hosting knightly duels and noble intrigue. Colorful tents flank the barrier where armored riders thunder together before cheering nobles. Players joust, spar, or serve as squires, winning renown and favors, while rivals poison horses, tamper with lances, and plot behind the pavilions.
- Hall of Mirrors: reflective maze of warped glass, disorienting illusions, and hidden doors that confuse and deceive. Countless reflections multiply every movement, and the true path is rarely where it seems. Players puzzle through corridors, spot subtle tells, and confront deceptive doubles, while enchanted mirrors might show truths, fears, or other worlds.
## Burial & Death
- Catacombs: Underground tunnels lined with niches of bones and tombs, dark, damp, and haunted by the restless dead. Dripping water echoes through branching passages where it is easy to get lost, and collapsing walls and ghouls threaten explorers. Players hunt for forgotten relics, map the maze, and uncover which cult still tends the bones.
- Crypt: Cold stone vault beneath a church or manor, holding coffins, sealed doors, and lingering undead. The air is stale and still, candles gutter without wind, and inscriptions hint at family secrets. Players pry open sarcophagi for heirlooms, solve lock puzzles, and risk waking the long-dead occupants who guard them.
- Mausoleum: Freestanding stone house of the dead with ornate sarcophagi, iron gates, and quiet, ghostly dread. Marble statues seem to watch visitors, locks and wards guard the wealthiest remains, and cold spots mark where spirits linger. Players break in for treasure, lay a restless noble to rest, or hide from pursuers.
- Pyramid Tomb: Massive stepped monument with trapped corridors, burial chambers, and cursed treasures for the king. Sand-choked passages hide pressure plates, collapsing ceilings, and swarming scarabs, while painted walls tell the dead ruler's story. Players decipher hieroglyphs, dodge deadly traps, and weigh the gold against the curse that guards it.
- Barrow Mound: Grassy ancient burial hill with narrow stone passages, grave goods, and restless warrior spirits. From outside it looks like a peaceful green swell, but inside crouching tunnels lead to a dark chamber of weapons and bones. Players slip past draugr guardians to claim a legendary blade and risk the hill's vengeance.
- Cemetery: Rows of headstones, mausoleums, and mourners under dim skies, with grave robbers, ghouls, and fog. Crooked iron fences and wilted flowers frame a place that feels hushed by day and menacing at night. Players follow clues carved on gravestones, confront body snatchers, and decide whether to disturb the freshly turned earth.
- Ossuary: Chamber decorated with stacked skulls and bones, serving as chilling shrine and storage for the dead. Every wall and arch is built from human remains arranged in patterns, creating a silent, oppressive beauty. Players seek a particular skull or relic, face the monks or spirits who tend it, and may be tempted by dark rites.
- Necropolis: Sprawling city of the dead with streets of tombs, temples, and gravekeepers, silent and ominous. Avenues of mausoleums stretch toward distant crypt towers, and strange lights drift between the monuments. Players navigate factions of keepers, ghouls, and thieves, search for a buried noble's tomb, and avoid wandering patrols after dark.
- Royal Tomb Valley: Hidden gorge of carved cliff tombs for kings, packed with traps, hieroglyphs, and looters. Sun-baked rock faces hide sealed doorways, and every entrance is guarded by curses, false passages, and mummified protectors. Players compete with tomb raiders to find a pharaoh's resting place, decode its wards, and escape with the burial riches.
- Funeral Pyre Ground: Ritual site of burning platforms, ash, and mourners, thick with smoke and spirits departing. Flames crackle over wood stacks while chanting priests tend the fires, and the air is choking and solemn. Players attend or disrupt a ceremony, gather ashes for rituals, and may glimpse souls drifting upward in the smoke.
