- Triassic Desert: A hot, dry supercontinent interior of red rock and scattered oases, shimmering under a relentless sun. Early dinosaurs and reptiles gather at scarce water, so travelers face thirst, flash floods, and wary predators, while fossil-bearing cliffs and hidden springs offer rewards for patient, resourceful explorers.
- Primordial Sea: A warm ancient ocean of primitive life, stromatolites, and strange early creatures beneath a hazy sky. Volcanic vents and shallow reefs teem with simple organisms, and the barren shores lie nearby, making it a place of discovery and wonder, where players witness the earliest stirrings of complex life.
- Cambrian Seafloor: A shallow ancient sea bottom crawling with armored trilobites, spiny worms, and bizarre early predators. Strange shapes with too many eyes or limbs scuttle through the sediment, so divers observe, collect, and avoid hunters like giant anomalocaridids, uncovering the alien beginnings of animal life.
- Tar Pit: Sticky black pools disguised by water or leaves that trap large animals, drawing scavengers and preserving bones. The stench of asphalt and rot hangs in the air, and a single misstep can mire a traveler, so hunters, scavengers, and later fossil seekers all find opportunity and peril.
### Ancient
- Bronze Age Citadel: A walled hilltop stronghold of massive stone ramparts enclosing a palace, granary, and chariot yard, ruled by warlords and priest-kings. Smoke from forges and sacrificial fires hangs over the cramped streets. Players can defend against sea raiders, plot palace coups, or smuggle grain past suspicious stewards.
- Ancient River Kingdom: Irrigated farmland and temple cities stretch along a great river, ruled by god-kings who raise monumental tombs. Annual floods bring fertile silt but also famine when they fail, and crocodiles lurk in the reeds. Players might serve as scribes, tomb-builders, or robbers braving priestly curses.
- Hanging Gardens: A terraced palace of lush plants and cascading water rises like a green mountain above a dry plain, a legendary wonder of luxury and engineering. Hidden pumps and slaves keep it alive, and the air is cool and fragrant. Explorers can find secret grottos, court intrigue, and engineering sabotage.
- Greek Polis: A city-state of marble temples, a busy agora, open theatres, and hoplite militias, known for philosophy and fierce independence. Debates echo in the colonnades while rival cities scheme across the hills. Players can join assemblies, march in phalanxes, consult oracles, or stir up faction politics.
- Roman Forum: A bustling civic square of temples, speeches, markets, and politicians at the center of imperial power. Crowds jostle beneath marble statues while senators, lawyers, and pickpockets work the same ground. Players can campaign for votes, uncover conspiracies, or witness triumphs and assassinations.
- Roman Road: A straight paved highway lined with milestones, inns, and patrols, linking far cities and moving legions quickly. Traffic ranges from couriers and merchants to marching columns, while bandits and ambushers lurk in the wooded stretches. Players can escort caravans, race messages, or investigate troubles at remote waystations.
- Roman Villa Countryside: Rolling estates of vineyards, olive groves, and mosaic-floored farmhouses worked by slaves and tenants. The pace is slow and sunlit, but harvest labor, debts, and wealthy owners' feuds create tension. Players can manage a farm, uncover buried secrets, shelter fugitives, or face a slave uprising.
- Persian Palace: A grand columned complex of gardens, painted reliefs, and treasuries, serving a king of kings and layers of satraps. Guards in glittering armor watch every hall, and informants whisper in every corridor. Players can navigate court protocol, carry royal decrees, steal tribute gold, or survive a poisoned banquet.
- Ziggurat City: A Mesopotamian metropolis dominated by a stepped temple tower, surrounded by canals, mud-brick houses, and busy scribes. Priests tally grain and omens on clay tablets while floods and rival cities threaten stability. Players can climb the tower for rites, trade across canals, or decode forbidden texts.
### Medieval
- Feudal Manor: A lord's fortified house stands amid surrounding fields, a mill, and peasant huts, run by obligations and seasonal labor. Life follows planting, harvest, and feast days, with the reeve and priest watching everyone. Players can settle disputes, fend off poachers, endure a bad winter, or lead a peasant revolt.
- Motte-and-Bailey: An early timber castle perches on an earthen mound beside a fenced courtyard, quick to build and easy to burn. Wooden palisades creak in the wind and the keep smells of smoke and animals. Players can besiege it with fire, defend it against raiders, or sneak in through the unguarded ditch.
- Village Green: The central common of a rural hamlet gathers a well, church, tavern, and constant gossip. Children play, geese wander, and travelers bring news from the road. It is ideal for quiet quests, where players trade rumors, solve small mysteries, and uncover darker secrets behind friendly neighbors.
- Crusader Fort: A stout stone stronghold in a distant land, garrisoned by knights and mercenaries amid hostile territory. Thirst, heat, and dwindling supplies wear down morale while scouts report enemy columns on the horizon. Players can hold the walls through a siege, negotiate with locals, or search for relics.
- Monastery Grounds: A cloistered stone complex of chapel, scriptorium, gardens, and cellars, home to monks, manuscripts, and secrets. Bells mark the hours and silence hangs over the cloister. Players can copy forbidden books, investigate a murder in the cellars, shelter fugitives, or hunt for hidden relics.
- Walled Town: A busy medieval settlement behind defensive ramparts, full of guilds, markets, gates, and cramped alleys. Guards check carts at the gates while merchants haggle and thieves slip through crowds. Players can join a guild, smuggle goods past tax collectors, fight in a street brawl, or defend the walls in a siege.
- Plague Village: A sickly hamlet of shuttered doors, plague carts, and desperate prayers, avoided by travelers and rife with fear. Bells toll for the dead, red crosses mark houses, and rumors of witches and cures spread. Players can deliver medicine, search for the source, quarantine the sick, or flee hysterical mobs.
### Early Modern
- Renaissance City: An elegant city of palaces, art workshops, banks, and intrigue, ruled by wealthy families and ambitious patrons. Canals and piazzas hum with painters, bankers, and poisoners. Players can commission masterpieces, forge alliances, spy on rival houses, or steal inventions from guarded workshops.
- Colonial Port: A bustling trade harbor of warehouses, forts, and mixed peoples, blending commerce, corruption, and distant empire. Ships unload spices and silver while officials take bribes and smugglers work the docks. Players can run cargo, expose graft, navigate colonial politics, or sail inland on risky expeditions.
- Plantation: A sprawling estate of cash crops and a grand house stands over fields worked by forced labor, rife with cruelty and tension. The contrast between the manor and the quarters is stark, and rebellion simmers beneath the surface. Stories here confront moral weight through escape, resistance, and the owners' fall.
- Pirate Haven: A lawless harbor town of taverns, hidden coves, and plundered goods, run by captains and rogues. Rum flows, treasure maps change hands, and loyalty is for sale. Players can recruit a crew, fence loot, dodge naval patrols, duel in the streets, or chase legendary treasure from secret anchorages.
- Frontier Fort: A lonely wooden stockade on contested wilderness, held by soldiers and traders amid raids and rough weather. Supplies are thin, the forest presses close, and distant allies are slow to arrive. Players can trade with nearby tribes, hold the walls through a raid, scout the woods, or mediate fragile treaties.
- Age of Sail Harbor: A crowded dock of tall ships, rope, cannon, and merchants, bustling with sailors, press gangs, and cargo. The air smells of tar and fish while shouts and creaking timbers fill the quay. Players can sign aboard a ship, dodge impressment, smuggle contraband, or investigate a mysterious cargo.
- Baroque Palace Gardens: Formal geometric gardens of fountains, hedges, and statues spread in front of an ornate royal residence. Gravel paths, hidden grottos, and maze hedges offer privacy for courtiers' secrets. Players can attend elaborate fetes, arrange secret meetings, chase an assassin through the maze, or sabotage a royal pageant.
### Industrial
- Victorian Slum: Soot-stained, crowded tenements and foggy alleys filled with street urchins, factory workers, thieves, and disease. Gutters run foul, gas lamps barely cut the gloom, and every doorway hides desperate lives. Players can survive as pickpockets, aid reformers, track a killer, or escape gangs and landlords.
- Coal Town: A grimy mining settlement of cottage rows, pit heads, and slag heaps, bound together by dangerous labor. Coal dust coats everything and the whistle sends families to the pits at dawn. Hazards include cave-ins and gas explosions, while players can organize strikes, rescue trapped miners, or confront owners.
- Mill Town: A factory district of smoking chimneys, looms, canals, and worker housing, ruled by owners and clanging shifts. Machinery thunders day and night, and children and women labor in dangerous conditions. Players can sabotage looms, organize unions, investigate a mill fire, or climb the social ladder.
- Railway Junction: A busy rail hub of steam engines, signal boxes, freight yards, and travelers, alive with whistles and clouds of steam. Schedules, switches, and coal stores matter, and cargo can hide anything. It is ideal for chases and heists, with players leaping between trains, hijacking freight, or averting a collision.
- Gaslit Streets: Foggy cobbled lanes lit by flickering lamps, home to cabs, pickpockets, constables, and shadowy killers. Footsteps echo, shapes loom in the mist, and every corner might hide a secret. Players can stalk a murderer, evade police, chase a hansom cab, or follow a mysterious stranger.
- Workhouse: A grim institution of forced labor, thin gruel, and strict rules, housing the destitute under harsh supervisors. Cold dormitories and bare stone yards leave inmates exhausted and hungry. Players can plan an escape, expose corrupt masters, protect fellow inmates, or uncover what happens to those who vanish.
- Canal District: An industrial waterway lined with warehouses, barges, locks, and mills, used for hauling goods and smuggling. Water is dark and oily, towpaths are slippery, and lock-keepers know all the secrets. Players can chase smugglers on barges, hide contraband, sabotage locks to flood rival mills, or find bodies in the water.
### Modern
- WWI Trench Lines: Muddy zigzag trenches, barbed wire, and shell craters stretch across no-man's-land, ruled by artillery and gas. Rats, mud, and exhaustion wear down soldiers who live in constant fear of the whistle. Players can lead night raids, repair wire under fire, survive barrages, or tunnel beneath enemy lines.
- WWII Bunker Coast: A fortified shoreline of concrete bunkers, barbed beaches, and searchlights, contested by invading forces. Machine guns cover every approach while mines and obstacles line the sand. Players can storm the beach, sabotage emplacements, defend a pillbox, or infiltrate with resistance fighters.
- Cold War Bunker: A sealed underground shelter of blast doors, supplies, and radios, built for survival of nuclear catastrophe. Fluorescent lights hum, air is recycled, and tension builds as the world above is silent. Players can manage dwindling rations, decode transmissions, face paranoia and sabotage, or finally emerge into the aftermath.
- Suburbia: Orderly neighborhoods of identical houses, lawns, cul-de-sacs, and minivans, hiding quiet secrets and small-town mysteries. Sprinklers tick and neighbors wave, yet curtains twitch and basements hold surprises. Players can investigate disappearances, uncover conspiracies behind picket fences, survive block-party horrors, or flee a monstrous cul-de-sac.
- 1950s Diner Strip: A neon-lit roadside of chrome diners, jukeboxes, and big cars, bustling with teens and traveling salesmen. Milkshakes, hot rods, and rock and roll fill the evening, while strangers pass through at night. Players can race drag strips, solve a local crime, meet shady drifters, or find alien visitors.
- Interstate Highway: A wide multi-lane asphalt corridor with overpasses, rest stops, and trucks, endless under headlights and roadside signs. Fuel, fatigue, and cops are constant hazards, and the shoulder hides strange sights. It is ideal for chases and roadside encounters, with players dodging pursuers, hitchhiking, or surviving breakdowns.
- Skyscraper Canyon: A dense downtown of glass towers, wind tunnels, and crowded streets, humming with traffic and sirens. Reflections confuse the eye and ledges and cranes invite risky climbs. Players enjoy vertical combat and rooftop routes, from window-washer rigs and elevator shafts to helicopter pads and corporate heists.
- Airport Tarmac: An open expanse of runways, hangars, and idling planes, loud with engines and busy with fuel trucks and security. Jet blast, spinning props, and tight schedules create danger and little cover. Players can stow away on cargo flights, chase a plane down the runway, smuggle contraband, or foil a hijacking.
- Rural Backroads: Lonely two-lane country roads past farms, gas stations, and woods, empty under big skies or heavy fog. Cell service fails, locals watch outsiders, and a broken-down car can mean trouble. It is ideal for road trips and horror, with players meeting strange diners, abandoned barns, and hitchhikers.
## Cultural & Civilizational Regions
### Africa & Middle East
- Nile Valley: A narrow fertile floodplain between desert cliffs, dotted with reed boats, irrigation canals, crocodiles, and ancient farming towns. The river's flood sets the rhythm of life, and temples and tombs line the banks. Players can sail upriver, fight crocodiles, trade grain, or seek lost tombs in the cliffs.
- Pyramid Plateau: A windswept desert tableland crowned with monumental stone tombs, buried chambers, trapdoor traps, and restless mummified guardians. Sand shifts across ancient causeways and sunlight blazes by day. Players can map hidden passages, avoid curses and collapsing corridors, race rival looters, and unseal a pharaoh's sealed treasure.
- Temple Complex: Colossal columned halls and sphinx-lined avenues carved with hieroglyphs shelter priests, hidden vaults, and curses. Torchlight flickers across painted walls and incense drifts through shadowed sanctuaries. Players can decode inscriptions, sneak past guardians, take part in festivals, or steal sacred relics at great risk.
- Delta Marshes: Sprawling papyrus wetlands where the great river splits into channels, hiding hippos, fowlers, and skiff-borne reed fishers. Mosquitoes, shifting waterways, and thick reeds make navigation difficult. Players can hunt waterfowl, ambush from reed boats, escape pursuers through hidden channels, or find a forgotten shrine.
- Tigris-Euphrates Plain: Flat irrigated farmland between two rivers, studded with mud-brick ziggurats, canal networks, and rival city-states. Floods and droughts decide fortunes, and canals are both lifelines and battle lines. Players can negotiate water rights, join armies, trade grain and tablets, or sabotage a rival's irrigation.
- Caravanserai: A fortified desert roadside inn with a central courtyard, stables, and wells, serving traders, spies, and bandit informants. Camels groan, languages mix, and rumors travel faster than goods. Players can hire guards, sell wares, overhear secrets, defend against a night raid, or join a caravan heading across the dunes.
- Bazaar City: A dense maze of covered market lanes, spice stalls, and haggling merchants, thick with scents, noise, and colored cloth. Every alley hides a rug seller, a thief, or a secret door. It is ideal for shopping, pickpockets, and chases, with players bargaining, tailing suspects, and slipping through crowds across rooftops.
- Desert Citadel: A sandstone fortress rising from dunes around a guarded oasis, with sun-baked ramparts, hidden cisterns, and sandstorm defenses. Heat and thirst protect it as much as the walls. Players can lay siege, smuggle water, scale ramparts at night, or negotiate with the lord for passage across the sands.
- Minaret City: A skyline of slender towers and domes above whitewashed streets, with scholars, markets, courtyards, and calls echoing at dawn. Libraries, bathhouses, and fountains offer refuge from the sun. Players can study with scholars, chase thieves across rooftops, deliver messages through winding lanes, or uncover a plot within the mosque.
- Savanna Kingdom: A grassland realm of thatched royal compounds and cattle herds, patrolled by lions, elephants, and proud mounted warriors. Seasonal rains transform the plains, and the king's court gathers beneath great trees. Players can join hunts, escort cattle drives, mediate disputes between clans, or defend villages from raiders.
- Sahel: A semi-arid belt of thorn scrub and cracked earth between desert and savanna, crossed by caravans and drought-hardened herders. Dust winds and scarce wells shape every journey. Players can lead trade caravans toward gold-rich cities, survive drought, resolve water disputes, or follow rumors of a vanished well.
- Great Zimbabwe Stone City: Mortarless granite walls and conical towers crown a hilltop capital, rich with gold trade and ritual enclosures. The curved walls form narrow passages and sacred precincts. Players can trade gold and ivory with coastal merchants, take part in royal rituals, uncover the secret of the great enclosure, or defend against rivals.
- Rainforest Village: A clearing of thatched huts sits under a towering canopy, with river canoes, medicinal plants, drumming, and lurking leopards. Humid air and constant sound surround the villagers. Players can gather rare herbs, learn healers' lore, guide expeditions through the dense forest, or face spirits and predators at night.
- Mud-Brick City: Sun-dried clay towers and flat-roofed houses with protruding timber beams crowd narrow alleys under desert sun, controlled by merchant guilds. The buildings glow orange at dusk and must be replastered after rains. Players can join caravans, bargain for salt and gold, scale towers for views, or uncover guild conspiracies.
- Swahili Coast: Palm-fringed trading ports with coral-stone houses, dhow fleets, monsoon spice routes, and a blend of cultures. Sailors from Arabia, India, and inland Africa mingle in the markets. Players can sail with the monsoon winds, trade ivory and spices, navigate rival sultans, or hunt pirates in the warm waters.
### Europe
- Aegean Isles: A sun-bleached island chain of white villages, olive terraces, rocky coves, and sailing fleets, with sea raiders lurking. Ferries and fishing boats link tiny harbors, while ancient ruins crown the hills. Players can sail between islands, trade wine, search sea caves for relics, or fend off pirates.
- Acropolis: A fortified marble hilltop crowned with columned temples, steep approach stairs, panoramic views, and sacred precincts watched by priests. The city sprawls below while processions climb the sacred way. Players can join festivals, defend the heights from invaders, seek oracles, or steal offerings from guarded treasuries.
- Roman Province: An orderly frontier land of straight stone roads, aqueducts, forts, bathhouses, and marching legions watching restless tribes. Roman law and local custom clash in every town. Players can serve as soldiers or officials, trade at the forts, quell rebellions, protect supply lines, or mediate between settlers and natives.
- Fjord Longhouse: A steep-walled sea inlet shelters timber halls, drying fish racks, and longship moorings among pine-covered cliffs. Winters are long and dark, and the hall's fire is the heart of the community. Players can launch raiding voyages, settle feuds at the thing, hunt in the mountains, or survive a harsh season.
- Viking Longship Coast: A rugged shoreline with beached dragon-prowed vessels, raider camps, burning monasteries, and cold surf crashing on shingle. Sudden landings strike terror into coastal villages. Players can lead raids, defend a settlement, negotiate tribute, navigate treacherous seas, or hunt a rival's fleet along the fog-bound shore.
- Celtic Hillfort: Ringed earthwork ramparts top a hill, enclosing roundhouses, smiths, and chieftains ready to repel invaders. Banners flap, dogs bark, and bonfires burn at festivals. Players can forge weapons, join cattle raids, parley with druids, defend against Roman or rival attackers, or uncover treachery within the chieftain's hall.
- Druid Grove: An ancient oak glade with mossy altars, mistletoe, and standing stones, tended by robed seers and haunted by woodland spirits. Mist hangs between the trunks and the air hums with old magic. Players can take part in rituals, seek prophecies, protect the grove from axes, or break a dark curse.
- Highland Glen: A misty valley of heather, loch shores, and stone ruins, with clan skirmishes, wild deer, and sudden fog. Weather changes in minutes and old castles brood above the water. Players can hunt stags, join clan feuds, track cattle thieves, explore ruins, or follow tales of a lake monster.
- Black Forest Village: A timbered hamlet beneath dense dark pines, known for woodcutters, clockmakers, folklore wolves, and witches in hidden hollows. Sunlight barely reaches the forest floor. Players can chop timber, hunt wolves, trade clocks, follow strange lights between trees, or bargain with a witch and face the price.
- Alpine Village: Wooden chalets cluster below snowy peaks, with cowbells, cheese cellars, avalanche risks, and winter mountain passes. Spring brings herds to high pastures and winter locks the valley in snow. Players can guide travelers over passes, rescue climbers from avalanches, trade cheese, or investigate disappearances on the mountain.
- Tuscan Hills: Sunlit rolling terrain of vineyards, cypress lines, and stone villas, perfect for peaceful travel and noble intrigue. Dusty roads wind between hilltop towns and olive groves. Players can attend harvest festivals, arrange marriages, uncover feuds between estates, paint frescoes, or hide from soldiers in wine cellars.
- Castilian Plateau: A high dry tableland of wheat fields and windmills, crowned by fortified towns and knights' castles. Hot summers and bitter winters shape hardy people and long wars. Players can ride with knights, defend a border castle, shepherd flocks along drove roads, or tilt at windmills in a tale of chivalry.
- Highland Croft: A remote stone cottage and sheep pasture on windy moors offers simple shelter, peat fires, and hardy folk tales. Weather is harsh and neighbors are miles away. Players can rest by the fire, herd sheep, shelter from a storm, hear legends from elders, or confront things that wander the moor at night.
- Cotswold Village: Honey-stone cottages, wool markets, and hedged lanes in gentle green hills, suited to cozy quests and rural mysteries. Church bells, pubs, and gardens set a calm pace. Players can solve village puzzles, trade wool, attend fairs, investigate a manor's secrets, or discover that peaceful lanes hide old crimes.
### Steppe & Slavic
- Yurt Steppe: An endless grass sea dotted with felt tents, horse herds, and mounted archers, with wind, wolves, and raiding clans. The horizon is vast and weather shifts quickly. Players can ride with nomads, herd horses, join raids, hunt with eagles, or race across the plains to deliver a message before a storm.
- Cossack Plains: An open frontier grassland patrolled by hard-riding horsemen, with fortified camps, river fords, and skirmishes against raiders. Freedom and discipline mix in tight-knit brotherhoods. Players can scout the steppe, guard river crossings, charge in cavalry raids, feast at camp fires, or deal with rival hosts and distant rulers.
- Taiga Village: Log cabins sit in a frozen coniferous forest, with smoking chimneys, trapper trails, bears, and long deadly winters. Snow muffles sound and wolves roam at night. Players can trap furs, hunt for food, survive blizzards, trade with passing sledges, or face folkloric horrors that stalk the dark trees.
- Onion-Dome City: A snowy walled capital of colorful bulbous cupolas, wooden palisades, fur traders, and bell towers ringing over icy streets. Frost glitters on gilded domes and market stalls sell furs, honey, and amber. Players can navigate boyar politics, trade at winter fairs, uncover plots in the cathedral, or defend against invaders.
### Asia
- Himalayan Monastery Valley: A remote high-altitude vale of prayer-flag strung gompas, yak trails, and thin air, where meditating monks sit beneath icy peaks and bells echo off the cliffs. Avalanches, altitude sickness, and bitter cold punish the unprepared. Players trade for supplies, seek teachers, or guide pilgrims across passes.
- Ganges Ghats: Stepped riverbank stairways thick with pilgrims, floating lamps, funeral pyres, boatmen, and sacred rituals at sunrise. Smoke, marigolds, and chanting fill the air, while crowds and river currents create both cover and danger. Players can ferry mourners, chase thieves through the throng, or witness rites that hide secrets.
- Mughal Gardens: A symmetrical walled paradise of reflecting pools, fountains, and marble pavilions, calm and perfumed with roses and jasmine. Its geometry makes sightlines clear and hiding places scarce. Ideal for courtly intrigue and ambush, players might broker alliances, poison a rival's wine, or stage a duel by moonlit water.
- Rajput Fortress: A sandstone hill fort with heavy spiked gates, elephant ramps, and ornate palaces that glow amber at dusk. Proud warrior clans defend it with archers on every rampart. Players may besiege its switchback approaches, court a clan's loyalty, or uncover the royal treasury and the old honor codes behind it.
- Pagoda Mountain: Misty cliffs strung with tiered temple towers, stone stairways, and hermit shrines, with clouds drifting through bamboo groves below. Slick steps and sudden fog make climbing treacherous. Players ascend toward enlightenment or relics, meeting reclusive masters, testing trials of skill, and finding hidden shrines along the route.
- Imperial City: A vast walled capital of red gates, courtyards, and golden-roofed halls, ruled by ceremony, eunuchs, and guarded secrets. Every gate has a protocol and every corridor has watchers. Players navigate court politics, bribe officials, slip into forbidden quarters, or serve a throne whose whims shape the whole empire.
- Bamboo Valley: Swaying green stalks hide winding paths, wandering pandas, and creaking rope bridges, while wind makes the whole grove whisper and clatter. Limited sightlines favor ambush and stealth. Swordsmen duel on treetop-high stalks, hunters track elusive animals, and travelers might stumble onto a hidden hermitage or bandit camp.
- Samurai Castle: A tiered white keep on stone ramparts, ringed by moats, maze-like gates, and trap-laced corridors with squeaking nightingale floors. Loyal retainers patrol with strict discipline. Players may infiltrate as shinobi, defend against a siege, or navigate the clan politics and honor debts that decide who lives in the keep.
- Zen Garden: Raked gravel, mossy stones, and pruned pines frame quiet tea houses in a stillness that sharpens the mind. Nothing here is accidental, so a disturbed pattern reveals intruders. It is perfect for puzzles, meditation, and sudden duels, with masters offering riddles, rare teas, and lessons in patience and discipline.
- Great Wall Ridge: A long crenellated barrier snaking across mountain crests, with signal towers, patrol walkways, and cold winds howling over the stones. Garrisons are thin and supplies are scarce. Nomad raiders probe weak spots, so players man beacon fires, repair breaches, smuggle goods through gates, or ride out to meet the incursion.
- Karst Pinnacle River: A jade river winding between tall limestone spires wrapped in mist, where cormorant fishers drift on bamboo rafts at dawn. Hidden caves pierce the cliffs, and rapids lurk around the bends. Players can explore sinkholes and grottos, hunt spirits, trade with fishing hamlets, or search for a lost hermit's retreat.
- Floating Village: Houses and shops built on rafts and stilts over a lake, linked by boats, nets, and swaying gangplanks. Life rises and falls with the water, and storms or floods threaten everything. Players barter fish and tools, escort traders between platforms, or defend the community from pirates arriving by night.
- Khmer Temple Jungle: Strangler-fig roots crush carved sandstone towers, enormous faces gaze from moss-covered stone, and humid air hangs heavy beneath the canopy. Snakes, collapsing galleries, and rival treasure hunters threaten explorers. Players decipher bas-reliefs, cross forgotten moats, and uncover sealed sanctums guarded by traps and ancient curses.
- Stilt Village: Bamboo homes raised above tidal flats or floods, joined by walkways, canoes, and drying fishing nets. The settlement is lively and close-knit but vulnerable to storms, rising water, and raiders. Players help repair damaged platforms, trade for catch, learn tide lore, or shelter here while a typhoon rages.
- Spice Islands: Humid volcanic isles of clove and nutmeg groves, rich trade ports, and rival sultans, with fragrant breezes drifting across the harbors. Pirate-infested lagoons and monsoon squalls threaten shipping. Players run valuable cargoes, play factions against each other, defend plantations, or hunt rumored treasure galleons wrecked on reefs.
### Oceania
- Polynesian Atoll: A low coral ring of palms and white sand around a shallow lagoon, with outrigger canoes, shell tools, and little shelter from storm surges. Fresh water and land are scarce, so survival depends on fishing and navigation. Players sail between atolls by stars and swells, gather resources, and weather cyclones.
- Outrigger Lagoon: Calm turquoise shallows ringed by reef, crowded with fishing canoes, coral gardens, and drifting tide pools that glitter in the sun. Sharks and sharp coral lurk beneath the surface. Players fish, dive for pearls, race canoes across the water, and slip through reef passes to reach hidden coves.
- Dreamtime Outback: Red desert plains dotted with rock formations and songlines, where scarce water and shimmering heat test every traveler. Strange spirit encounters and ancestral stories carved in stone reward respect and punish arrogance. Players follow songlines between waterholes, learn sacred lore, and avoid desecrating places that watch back.
- Maori Hill Fortress: Carved timber palisades crown a terraced hill, with war canoes drawn up on the shore below and tattooed warriors posted at the gates. Geothermal steam rises nearby, offering warmth and hazard. Players may defend the pa from raiders, join a ritual challenge, or trade carved treasures and tribal knowledge.
### Americas
- Mayan Step Pyramid Jungle: Stepped limestone temples poke through thick canopy, with ball courts, sinkholes, and sacrificial altars hidden in vines. Jaguars prowl, heat and humidity sap the traveler, and astronomy shapes every ritual. Players climb pyramids for vistas, explore cenotes and tombs, decode glyphs, and uncover calendar-driven secrets.
- Aztec Lake City: An island capital on a shallow lake, connected by causeways and canals, with floating gardens, bustling markets, and blood-soaked temple summits. Canoes crowd the waterways and warriors patrol the bridges. Players trade in the great market, join political plots among nobles, or infiltrate temple precincts to disrupt ritual sacrifices.
- Inca Mountain Citadel: Precisely fitted stone terraces and swaying rope bridges perch on a cloud-wrapped Andean ridge, guarded by llama herders and watchful sentries. The thin air saps endurance, and landslides and mist can strand travelers. Players cross suspension bridges, follow messenger runners, or search sacred stone shrines.
- Nazca Plain: An arid highland desert etched with giant ground drawings, buried aqueducts, and mummies, scorched by day and cold by night. Sudden dust storms erase tracks and hide ancient secrets. Players decipher the meaning of the lines, trace underground water channels, loot or protect burial sites, and survive punishing weather.
- Terraced Andes: Steep green steps carved into mountainsides grow potatoes and maize, linked by stone stairs and llama trails in thin air. Farming here is a feat of engineering and community labor. Players herd llamas, haul harvests down slopes, negotiate with villagers over water rights, and escape rockfalls and cold nights.
- Plains Tipi Camp: A circle of hide tents on open prairie, busy with horse herds, buffalo hunts, drying racks, and sweeping horizon scouts. The camp moves with the seasons and the herds. Players ride out to hunt, trade with neighboring bands, defend against raiders, or join storytelling and ceremony around evening fires.
- Pueblo Cliff Dwelling: Adobe rooms stacked in canyon alcoves are reached by ladders pulled up at night, with kivas, sun-baked mesas, and hidden granaries nearby. Water and food stores determine survival, and the canyon walls make a natural fortress. Players explore ceremonial chambers, trade pottery, or protect stored harvests.
- Woodland Longhouse: Bark-covered communal homes stand in a forest clearing, surrounded by cornfields, birchbark canoes, ambush trails, and strong council fires. Clan rules and consensus govern daily life, and rivals may strike from the trees. Players join councils, scout the forest paths, paddle rivers, and negotiate treaties.
- Totem Pole Coast: A misty temperate shore of cedar plank houses and carved poles, backed by dark rain forest and fed by salmon runs. Sea canoes carry traders and warriors among the inlets. Players fish the runs, attend potlatch feasts, learn clan stories carved in wood, and encounter orca spirits and fog-shrouded dangers.
- Arctic Ice Camp: Windswept sea ice with snow shelters, sled dogs, seal holes, and aurora skies, brutally cold and nearly featureless. Deadly polar bears, thin ice, and blizzards threaten every outing. Players hunt seals at breathing holes, navigate by wind and stars, drive dog teams between camps, and endure long dark winters.
## Religious & Sacred Sites
- Stone Temple: A solid masonry sanctuary with carved altars and echoing halls that stay cool and hushed even in summer. It offers healing shrines, hidden crypts, and ritual guardians who test visitors' intentions. Players seek blessings, uncover relics sealed in lower vaults, or shelter within thick walls during raids and storms.
- Pagoda: A multi-tiered tower with upturned eaves and wind-chimed bells, climbable for sweeping vistas across the surrounding land. Each floor houses relics, monks, or an ascending challenge, from riddles to guardian duels. Players climb for rewards and views, protect scrolls from thieves, or follow clues up to the summit.
- Step Pyramid Temple: A terraced stone mountain with a summit shrine, steep stairways, and sacrificial platforms rising above the surrounding landscape. Chambers sealed deep within hold tombs, treasure, and traps. Players climb during festivals, infiltrate ceremonies, crawl into hidden passages, and uncover the astronomical secrets and bloody rites it was built for.
- Cathedral: A soaring vaulted nave with stained glass, flying buttresses, crypts below, and bell towers overhead, filled with candlelight and echoing chant. Clergy shelter fugitives under the right of sanctuary and guard hidden secrets. Players seek absolution, search crypts for relics, climb towers for vantage points, or become entangled in church intrigue.
- Basilica: A long columned hall ending in an apse and altar, glowing with mosaics, side chapels, and relic vaults along its flanks. Processions, pilgrims, and clergy fill it on feast days. Players can attend rites, steal or protect sacred treasures, meet informants among the crowd, and explore forgotten catacombs beneath the floor.
- Mosque: A domed prayer hall with minarets, ablution courtyards, geometric tilework, and quiet libraries, calm and carefully ordered. It serves as community refuge and gathering place, offering hospitality to travelers. Players can study in its libraries, seek mediation of disputes, find shelter, or hear news and rumors from merchants.
- Synagogue: A modest prayer house with an ark, hanging lamps, study benches, and scrolls, anchoring community life and scholarly discussion. Its quiet intimacy hides a deep tradition of debate and memory. Players may consult scholars for lost knowledge, protect the congregation from persecution, or help recover stolen scrolls and hidden records.
- Shinto Shrine: A wooden sanctuary among sacred trees, with purification basins, rope charms, fox statues, and quiet festival grounds that wake with lanterns and drums. Kami are felt rather than seen, and disrespect has consequences. Players leave offerings, receive omens, join seasonal festivals, or soothe a restless spirit.
- Torii Gate Path: A lane of vermilion gates winding up a wooded slope, marking passage into spirit realms and watched by stone foxes. The gates grow denser and the light stranger the higher one climbs. Players follow the path to hidden shrines, meet trickster spirits, and risk wandering somewhere that is no longer the mortal world.
- Stupa: A rounded relic mound topped with a spire, circled clockwise by pilgrims and ringed with prayer wheels, lamps, and offerings. The air hums with murmured mantras and butter-lamp smoke. Players join the circumambulation, deliver offerings, guard a saint's relic from thieves, or uncover chambers beneath the dome.
- Gurdwara: An open-doored Sikh temple with a golden-domed hall, a sacred pool, and a communal kitchen where all are fed regardless of status. Music and recitation drift through the courtyards. Players find free meals, sanctuary, and honest counsel here, or serve as defenders of the community when oppressors threaten the faithful.
- Hindu Temple Complex: Towering carved gateways and shrine halls ringed by water tanks and courtyards, bright with flowers, incense, bells, and priests. Every surface teems with sculpted deities and stories. Players join processions, seek blessings, explore sanctums and treasuries, or resolve disputes involving the temple's wealth and devotees.
- Buddhist Monastery: A quiet walled compound of prayer halls, dormitories, gardens, and bells, where daily rhythms of chanting and chores never change. It is home to monks, trainees, and martial secrets passed down through generations. Players seek training and wisdom, shelter from storms, or attempt to unearth forbidden texts.
- Cave Temple: A hollowed cavern shrine lit by lamps and carved with statues and murals that flicker in the dim light. Bats roost overhead while hidden relics and secret passages lie beyond the main hall. Players explore side tunnels, interpret ancient paintings, and uncover treasures guarded by traps or spirits.
- Rock-Cut Temple: A shrine chiseled directly into a cliff face, with pillars and statues carved from the living stone, remote, ancient, and full of echoing dark halls. Pilgrims and monks keep faint traditions alive. Players trace carved stories, search for hidden chambers, and face guardians or collapses in unlit passages.
- Sun Temple: A sunlit shrine aligned to the solstice, gilded and carved, where light puzzles guide visitors through its chambers. Golden relics gleam when the sunbeam strikes the right spot, and fire priests tend sacred flames. Players solve alignment riddles at the right hour, claim solar treasures, and risk the wrath of priests.
- Moon Shrine: A silver-lit sanctuary of pale stone and still water, quiet and hushed, and most active at night. Reflections shimmer on the pool as moon rituals unfold under the stars. Players might seek dream omens, commune with lunar spirits, join secret rites, or defend the shrine against those who would steal its radiance.
- Druid Circle: A ring of tall oaks or megaliths used for seasonal rites, charged with nature magic, mist, and the sense of being watched. Robed figures tend sacred groves, and trespass is met with swift reprisals. Players may attend solstice ceremonies, learn herbal and animal lore, or defend the grove from axes and invaders.
- Oracle Cave: A vapor-filled grotto where a seer delivers cryptic prophecies amid strange fumes and flickering torchlight. Visions reveal quest hints but rarely in plain language. Offering strange visions and dangerous bargains, it draws desperate seekers, kings, and schemers. Players must interpret riddles, pay the price, and live with the consequences.
- Pilgrim Route: A long devotional road with waystations, shrines, beggars, and weary travelers moving toward a holy destination. Footsore crowds bring trade, rumors, and sometimes bandits. It is ideal for caravan escorts and roadside encounters, with players guiding pilgrims, resting at hostels, or investigating mysteries at forgotten shrines.
- Holy Mountain: A revered peak with steep trails, hermit huts, and summit shrines, where thin air and sudden weather test every climber. Rules of reverence govern who may ascend and how. Trials and visions reward those who reach the top, and players may seek enlightenment, relics, or the guidance of mountain hermits.
- Sacred River Bank: A shoreline of ritual bathing, floating offerings, and prayer fires, alive with chanting at dawn and dusk. Ferrymen, pilgrims, and priests work the banks while the purifying waters hide currents and eddies. Players take part in cleansing rites, ferry travelers, recover offerings lost downstream, or witness mystic events on the water.
- Holy Spring: A bubbling healing pool ringed with votive offerings, its clear water restoring health to visitors who show proper respect. The spring attracts guardians, pilgrims, and covetous seekers, and its power may be tainted or fading. Players may defend the site, collect restorative water, or investigate why the spring has changed.
- Standing Stone Ring: A prehistoric circle of weathered monoliths aligned to the stars, humming with old magic, portals, and eerie moonlit rituals. Wind moans through the gaps, and compasses or senses may fail within. Players can activate the stones, cross into other realms, interrupt dark ceremonies, or study alignments for lost knowledge.
- Sacrifice Altar: A blood-stained stone platform in a ritual precinct, surrounded by cultists, ominous drums, and offerings that attract dark powers. The air feels heavy and wrong, and the ground may be littered with bones. Players might rescue captives, disrupt a ceremony, steal cursed relics, or face what answers the blood.
- Hermit Cave: A lonely hillside shelter holding a wise recluse, meager supplies, and cryptic advice offered grudgingly to those who persist. Perhaps something sinister lurks behind the stillness, since solitude can breed madness as readily as wisdom. Players may seek guidance, trade for herbs, or discover the hermit's dark secret.
- Desert Monastery: An isolated walled retreat among dunes with deep cisterns, ascetic monks, and scriptoriums full of copied manuscripts. Travelers seek refuge from heat and sandstorms, though hospitality comes with expectations. Players can rest, buy water, study ancient texts, or protect the monastery from raiders after its precious wells.
- Cliffside Monastery: A precarious complex clinging to a sheer rock face, reached by ladders, rope lifts, or hidden stairs, remote and defensible. Wind howls around its narrow ledges and dizzying drops punish carelessness. Players must climb in, earn the monks' trust, hunt for rare manuscripts, or hold the heights against attackers.
- Cloister Garden: A peaceful arcaded courtyard of herbs, fountain, and silent walkways, where bees hum and sunlight dapples old stone. It offers healing plants, secret meetings, and contemplative rest from the world outside. Players gather medicinal herbs, hold covert conversations, recover from wounds, or uncover a secret buried beneath the flagstones.
- Wayside Shrine: A small roadside altar with candles, charms, and offerings left by passing travelers, weathered by years of rain and sun. It grants minor blessings, travel hints, or ambush opportunities when bandits watch for the devout. Players leave tokens for luck, receive omens, shelter briefly, or find clues left behind.
- Prayer Flag Pass: A windy mountain saddle strung with fluttering colored flags, cairns, and thin air, marking a sacred and perilous crossing. Each flag carries prayers to the wind, and each cairn marks someone's passing. Players brave storms and altitude, leave offerings for safe travel, and may meet traders, bandits, or restless spirits.
- Sacred Island: An isolated holy islet reached by boat or tidal causeway, hosting shrines, hermits, and ritual forbidden zones. Storm-lashed shores and treacherous tides guard it from casual visitors. Players must time crossings, seek permission to land, uncover island mysteries, or find themselves stranded overnight with whatever dwells there.
- Burning Ghat: Riverside cremation steps with smoking pyres, mourning families, and ash-laden air, solemn and spiritually charged with haunted undertones. Priests tend the fires and boatmen scatter remains upon the water. Players may attend rites, bargain with restless spirits, search for a lost soul, or mind the etiquette of grief.
- Tabernacle Tent: A portable sacred pavilion of woven cloth and gilded poles, central to wandering tribes, guarded by priests and relics. It is raised at each camp and folded away before each march, carrying the people's holiest object. Players may defend it on the move, seek counsel inside, or steal or recover its relics.
- Pilgrim Plain: Wide open ground where devotees camp in great numbers, with tents, processions, hawkers, and festival crowds spreading to the horizon. Smoke, song, and haggling fill the air, while disease and theft spread quickly. Players trade among the stalls, escort travelers, hunt pickpockets, or search the throng for someone in disguise.
- Ritual Circle: Chalked or stone-marked ground for ceremonies, summonings, and oaths, glowing with symbols, candles, and tense chanting participants. Breaking the circle can unleash chaos, while completing it can bind spirits or seal pacts. Players may perform or disrupt rites, bargain with summoned entities, or draw hidden lines for protection.
- Dark Cult Altar: A hidden shrine of black stone and candle smoke, tended by masked cultists, surrounded by traps, sacrifices, and eldritch whispers. The air is cold and the shadows seem to move. Players may infiltrate to rescue victims, steal forbidden tomes, sabotage a summoning, or become tempted by the bargains offered.
## Mythological Realms
### Greek
- Olympus: A cloud-wreathed mountain palace of marble halls and golden thrones, where capricious gods feast, scheme, and hurl thunderbolts at mortals. Nectar flows, music plays, and divine moods shift in an instant. Players may beg favors, run errands for patrons, compete in contests of glory, or suffer the consequences of angering a god.
- Elysian Fields: Sunlit meadows of eternal peace for heroes, with gentle breezes, singing, games, and feasting, free of pain. Fallen champions rest and reminisce here, though stillness can feel like its own trial. Players may visit to seek counsel from legendary shades, recover a lost friend, or test whether paradise is truly free of sorrow.
- Tartarus: A bottomless pit of bronze gates and chained Titans, tormenting the damned with endless punishments and monstrous jailers. The air is dark, hot, and filled with screams and the clank of chains. Players may descend to free a prisoner, steal from the abyss, or escape the guardians that stalk the walls.
- Asphodel Meadows: Gray, pale-flowered plains where unremarkable souls drift aimlessly, wispy and forgetful, under dim twilight. Nothing grows vividly here, and memory fades with every step. Players crossing it risk losing their names and purpose, but may find lost friends, bargain with forgotten ghosts, or follow whispers toward the judgment halls.
- River Styx Shore: A murky underworld bank where ferrymen demand coins, ghosts crowd the fog, and oaths bind even gods. The water is deathly cold and corrosive to the living. Players must pay the toll or find another way across, swear unbreakable vows, evade drowning shades, and negotiate with the gaunt boatman.
- Halls of Hades: A shadowy palace of black stone, guarded by a three-headed hound, where the dead are judged by the pale king. Torchlight flickers on cold columns and every echo seems to listen. Players might plead for a soul's return, sneak past Cerberus, strike a bargain with the lord of the dead, or face judgment.
- Atlantis: A drowned island metropolis of concentric canals, crystal towers, and lost technology, ruled by hubris and haunted by sea creatures. Strange light and silence fill the flooded avenues. Players dive for relics and powerful devices, evade guardians and predators, uncover the cause of its fall, or confront survivors still ruling the ruins.
- Garden of the Hesperides: A golden-apple orchard at the world's edge, tended by singing nymphs and guarded by a sleepless serpent. The fruit glows with immortal power and the air hums with sweet, drowsy music. Players may attempt to steal an apple, trick or fight the dragon, or bargain with the nymphs and their secrets.
### Norse
- Asgard: gleaming fortress realm of the war gods, with golden halls, rainbow-guarded gates, and mighty walls built by giants. Banners snap above feasting halls while sentries watch the horizon for any hint of invasion. Players can seek audiences with proud deities, take on divine errands, or defend the ramparts when frost and fire giants test the walls.
- Valhalla: vast hall of shields and spears where fallen warriors feast and battle daily, awaiting the final war. Roof beams of spears and tiled shields ring with song, boasting, and clashing steel, and the dead rise each dawn to fight again. Heroes can earn a seat by glorious deeds, join the endless brawls, or recruit champions for the coming doom.
- Midgard: the mortal world of villages, forests, and seas, ringed by a monstrous serpent and threatened by giants. Smoke curls from thatched halls, longships crowd the fjords, and the ocean itself seems to coil around the horizon. Players farm, trade, raid, and answer the calls of gods and monsters that spill into the human realm.
- Niflheim: frozen misty realm of primordial ice and fog, with poisonous rivers, wandering shades, and gnawing dragons. Visibility drops to a few paces, the cold saps strength, and the rivers carry venom that rots flesh. Explorers hunt for ancient springs and buried secrets while avoiding drifting shades and the dragon gnawing at the roots below.
- Muspelheim: scorched land of flame and lava ruled by fire giants, glowing with embers, searing winds, and burning swords. Rivers of molten rock cut through black basalt, and the air shimmers hot enough to ignite clothing and wood. Adventurers must bargain with or fight fiery lords, seeking flame-forged weapons while guarding against heat that drains health quickly.
- Jotunheim: harsh mountainous realm of frost and stone giants, with enormous halls, treacherous fjords, and brutal tests of strength. Everything is built at giant scale, so doorways loom, tables tower, and footsteps shake the ground. Players sneak through oversized halls, win wagers through contests of strength and wit, or steal treasures from hosts who despise intruders.
- Helheim: gloomy underworld of those who died of age or illness, with high walls, a hollow-eyed queen, and cold feasts. Pale mist drifts through silent halls where the dead sit in dim, joyless comfort, and the great gate is watched without pause. Visitors may bargain with the half-living ruler to retrieve a lost soul, at a price that always proves steep.
- Alfheim: radiant forest realm of light elves, with luminous glades, shimmering streams, and graceful, proud, magic-rich inhabitants. Sunlight seems to linger in every clearing, and music drifts between white-barked trees. Players can learn elegant magic, trade for enchanted gifts, or stumble into elven politics where a careless insult can close every door.
- Yggdrasil Roots: gnarled cosmic tree roots spanning realms, with wells of wisdom, gnawing serpents, hidden passages, and unseen watchers. Tunnels twist between huge woody coils that glow faintly with sap and rumor, linking the nine worlds. Travelers use the roots as shortcuts, drink from sacred wells at a cost, and dodge the creatures that gnaw the foundation of existence.
- Bifrost: shimmering rainbow bridge between realms, guarded by a vigilant sentinel, burning at the touch of enemies. Colors ripple beneath each step, and the span hums with power as it arcs across the void between worlds. Players must win passage past the keen-eyed watchman, race invaders across the burning span, or defend it when the final army marches.
### Egyptian & Mesopotamian
- Duat: perilous underworld river passage of gates, serpents, and lakes of fire, which the dead must navigate nightly. Dark water carries a solar barque past guardians who demand secret names and spoken spells. Players can sail with the sun god, solve riddles at each gate, and fight the great serpent that tries to swallow dawn itself.
- Field of Reeds: lush, golden afterlife marsh resembling an idealized homeland, with bountiful harvests, rivers, and peaceful, eternal labor for the worthy. Wheat grows taller than a man, canals shimmer, and the souls who farm here never tire. Visitors find a gentle sanctuary, though they must prove they are worthy and may be drawn to stay forever.
- Hall of Judgment: grand chamber where hearts are weighed against a feather, watched by jackal-headed judges and a devouring beast. Towering columns, gleaming scales, and forty-two stern assessors fill the vaulted space as souls recite their innocence. Players face a tense trial of confession and conscience, where a heavy heart means being devoured by the waiting monster.
- Kur: dusty, dark underworld of clay and silence, from which none return, ruled by a stern queen of the dead. The dead eat dust and wear feathers like birds, shuffling through gloomy gates that close behind them. Heroes descend only for desperate rescues or bargains, and escaping requires clever tricks, since the ruler never freely releases a soul.
- Dilmun: pure, radiant paradise island of fresh water and abundant life, free of sickness, hunger, and death. Clear springs feed orchards and meadows, and the air is clean and gentle. Players can seek healing or immortality here, meet the few legendary survivors who live in it, and confront a place so perfect that it tempts them to forget their quests.
### Abrahamic
- Eden: lush primordial garden of fruit trees, rivers, and gentle beasts, guarded by flaming sentinels after the fall. Four rivers water a world that once knew no fear, now sealed behind a gate and a whirling blade of fire. Explorers can seek forbidden fruit and lost innocence, but angelic wardens turn back all who try to return.
- Heaven: radiant celestial realm of golden light, pearly gates, choirs, and winged hosts, serving as peaceful reward and divine court. Clouds form marble terraces under endless dawn, and music fills every corridor. Players may petition angels, stand before judgment, or join the celestial armies when rebellion threatens the shining walls.
- Hell: fiery infernal pit of torment, demons, brimstone, and endless suffering, ruled by a fallen adversary and his legions. Scorched stone, screaming winds, and rivers of fire fill a hierarchy of cruel officials who love contracts. Players can descend to rescue souls, trade bargains with devils, or fight through legions where every deal hides a harsher price.
- Purgatory: mountainous place of purification where souls climb terraces, bearing burdens and cleansing sins before ascending to paradise. Each ledge assigns penance, such as heavy stones, blinded eyes, or burning fire, while hymns echo overhead. Players climb tier by tier, confronting their own flaws, and guiding souls who must finish their atonement before the summit.
- Limbo: gray borderland of quiet, unfulfilled souls, neither punished nor rewarded, filled with melancholy, mist, and idle longing. Pale castles and meadows sit under a colorless sky, populated by wise, sorrowful shades who can never move on. Visitors can learn forgotten lore from them, or lose themselves in a listless fog that slowly erodes ambition and will.
- Gehenna: smoldering valley of fire and refuse, symbolizing judgment and destruction, with burning pits, wailing shades, and charred ground. Smoke from endless trash fires chokes the air, and cinders crunch underfoot beside glowing chasms. Players scavenge cursed relics, hunt punished spirits, and struggle to cross a valley where the very ground sometimes gives way into flame.
- Paradise Gardens: terraced celestial orchards of flowing rivers, shade, fruit, and fountains, offering rest, abundance, and serenity for the blessed. Cool streams run beneath trees heavy with fruit, and every breeze carries perfume. Players restore health and morale here, meet peaceful souls with wisdom to share, and guard a sanctuary that darker forces long to corrupt.
- Circles of the Inferno: nine descending rings of escalating torment, each with themed punishments, monstrous wardens, and gloomy landscapes. Storms, frozen lakes, burning tombs, and rivers of blood mark each level, with sinners suffering in fitting ways. Players descend ring by ring, face a unique guardian and moral puzzle at every depth, and confront deeper horrors the farther they go.
### Hindu & Buddhist
- Mount Meru: golden world-axis mountain, tiered with heavens and palaces, circled by sun and moon, home to deities. Its terraced slopes rise through layers of shining realms, each ruled by different gods and spirits. Players climb through celestial courts, bargain with divine guardians, and seek the summit where the cosmos turns around a single sacred peak.
- Naraka: layered hellish realms of purification, with torture pits, boiling oceans, flame trees, and judging servants of the death lord. Souls are sorted by their deeds and endure a fitting sentence in cauldrons, thorn forests, and frozen wastes. Players can attempt daring rescues, bargain with stern judges, or endure ordeals meant to burn away karma before rebirth.
- Svarga: luminous heavenly paradise of celestial gardens, music, and nectar, ruled by thunder-wielding king and graced by dancers. Pavilions glitter among wishing trees, and divine musicians play beside cool pools. Players can attend lavish courts, earn favors from the storm-god monarch, and discover that even paradise has intrigue, temptation, and rivals jealous of mortal merit.
- Pure Land: serene lotus-filled paradise of jeweled trees and golden ponds, where seekers gain enlightenment free of suffering. Soft light falls through fragrant air, and lotus blossoms hold newly born souls. Players can study with luminous teachers, find peace between battles, and face a gentle trial of letting go of desire, rather than a physical enemy.
- Hungry Ghost Realm: barren, shadowy wasteland of gaunt spirits with swollen bellies and needle throats, cursed with insatiable craving. Dry plains, dim twilight, and the groan of spirits who can never be satisfied fill the landscape. Players may offer food and prayers to ease suffering, or fall prey to grasping hordes who devour anything they can reach.
- Vaikuntha: radiant celestial abode of the preserver deity, with jeweled palaces, endless milk ocean, and perfected, blissful servants. Gates of gold open onto lotus lakes and crystal halls where nothing decays and nobody suffers. Players may serve in the divine court, test their devotion against guardians at the threshold, or seek a boon from the sleeping lord on his serpent couch.
### East Asian
- Celestial Court: bureaucratic heavenly palace of jade, clouds, and golden roofs, ruled by emperor gods, ministers, and scheming immortals. Officials in elaborate robes stamp decrees and file petitions, while rank and ritual decide who is heard. Players can navigate court intrigue, earn promotions, or start trouble when a petition, a stolen peach, or a rebellious spirit disrupts the ordered heavens.
- Peach Garden of Immortals: sacred orchard whose fruit grants eternal life, jealously guarded by a queen mother and heavenly soldiers. Pink blossoms scent the air above trees that ripen only once in thousands of years. Thieves and heroes alike try to steal a peach, but celestial sentries, cunning traps, and divine wrath make every attempt dangerous.
- Diyu: multi-tiered underworld of judgment halls and torture chambers, administered by ox-headed and horse-faced guards under hellish magistrates. Each court reviews a soul's deeds, assigning punishments that fit the crimes. Players can bribe clerks, appeal verdicts, or sneak out lost souls, while hunting down escaped ghosts that haunt the world above.
- Mount Penglai: mist-shrouded island of immortals, rumored to hold elixirs of life, invisible to those who search unworthily. Gleaming palaces of gold and jade float in the fog while white cranes wheel overhead. Players sail through shifting mists, resist illusions, and prove themselves pure of heart to glimpse an island that vanishes from greed.
- Mount Kunlun: sacred western peak of jade palaces, divine springs, and mythical beasts, abode of the queen mother and immortals. Towering cliffs, glittering terraces, and nine-headed guardians protect the path to the summit. Players trek through snow and spirit-haunted passes, seek rare herbs and elixirs, and risk the wrath of beasts that guard the divine mountain.
- Yomi: dark underworld of decay and shadow, entered through a hidden cave, where the dead mutate and hunt the living. Rotting halls and sour mist surround corpses that shamble through gloom, and a boulder-sealed gate marks the exit. Players creep through the dark, avoid pursuing hags and thunder spirits, and race to escape before the bonds of the dead take hold.
- Takamagahara: high plain of heaven where sun and storm gods reside, with sacred halls, mirrors, rice fields, and shining rivers. Bright meadows lie above the clouds, and shrines hold relics like a polished mirror and a legendary sword. Players can mediate quarrels between gods, join festivals, or face the chaos of a storm-god's tantrum disrupting the radiant heavens.
- Ryugu Palace: coral-and-pearl underwater castle of the dragon king, with tide jewels, shifting time, fishy courtiers, and sea magic. Glowing halls of shell and pearl sit beneath the waves, where banquets seem to last days while years pass above. Players can win the king's gifts, borrow or steal tide jewels that command the sea, and return to a world that has moved on.
### Celtic & Slavic
- Tir na nOg: eternal-youth island across the western sea, golden-lit and timeless, where visitors forget age but risk lost years. Meadows stay green, feasts never end, and nobody grows old or weary. Players can enjoy ageless bliss and fairy gifts, but returning to the mortal world may cause centuries to catch up in a heartbeat.
- Avalon: misty apple-orchard isle shrouded in enchantment, where wounded kings heal under priestesses' care and legendary blades are forged. Fog hides the shore from all but the chosen, and the orchards bloom through every season. Players can seek healing, claim fabled weapons, and learn old magic from the island's guardians, who value loyalty above all.
- Annwn: shadowy otherworld of hunts, cauldrons, and shifting castles, ruled by a dark lord, where bargains carry heavy costs. Spectral hounds bay across misty hills, and magical cauldrons restore life or refuse the unworthy. Players can raid for treasures, join the wild hunt, or make deals with the lord that always demand something dear in return.
- Fairy Mound: grassy hill concealing a glittering court of fae, with feasts, dance, trickery, and time passing strangely inside. A door in the turf opens on music, gold lamps, and beautiful strangers who smile too widely. Players can win fairy favors and magical gifts, but eating their food or breaking their rules risks being trapped for years.
- Nav: Slavic realm of the dead, a misty twilight beyond the world tree, where ancestors wander and spirits roam. Pale fields stretch under a dim sky, and echoes of old homes fade into fog. Players can seek counsel from departed kin, cross the dividing river, or face restless souls and spirits that slip back into the living world.
- Baba Yaga's Hut Grounds: bone-fenced forest clearing around a chicken-legged cottage, haunted by riddles, mortars, hungry crones, and strange guardians. Skull lanterns glow on the fence, and the hut turns and stomps to face visitors. Players must speak politely, answer riddles, and complete impossible chores to win the witch's aid, or risk becoming her next meal.
### Mesoamerican & Other
- Mictlan: nine-layered underworld of rivers, mountains, and obsidian winds, through which souls journey, aided by faithful dog companions. Each level poses a trial, from crushing hills to blades of wind to dark rivers that need a guide. Players travel the long road of the dead, negotiate with the lords of death, and rely on loyal companions to cross.
- Xibalba: dark Maya underworld of trial houses, bat caves, and lord demons, testing heroes with riddles, blades, and lethal games. Each house holds a different torment, such as darkness, razors, cold, jaguars, or fire, set by lords who delight in deceit. Players must outwit the demons through cleverness and teamwork, and may even win a ball game against death itself.
- Tlalocan: lush rain paradise of rain god, with endless green gardens, water-lilies, and abundance for those taken by water. Clouds drift over fields of maize, flowers, and bright ponds where the blessed dead live without want. Players can seek favor of the storm deity, bring rain to withered lands, and enjoy a peaceful haven that rewards those who died in water.
- Happy Hunting Ground: peaceful afterlife of rolling prairies and teeming game, where ancestors ride and hunt without hunger or hardship. Golden grass moves in endless wind beneath wide skies, and herds graze untroubled. Players can reunite with fallen kin, receive wisdom from elder spirits, and track great beasts in a place where every hunt is honorable and nothing is wasted.
- Shamballa: hidden Himalayan kingdom of enlightened rulers, guarded by snow peaks, mystic lotus walls, and secret doors for the pure. Its lotus-shaped valley lies beyond blizzards that turn back the unworthy. Players follow cryptic maps and spiritual trials to reach it, then seek teachings and aid for the prophesied final battle between harmony and darkness.
- Hyperborea: sunlit northern utopia beyond the cold wind, where long-lived sages sing and feast in endless daylight and warmth. Temperate groves and white temples sit under a perpetual sun, home to a people free of toil and disease. Players may sail the northern edge of the map to find it, learn secrets from the blessed inhabitants, and guard against those who would exploit it.
- Lemuria: sunken Pacific continent of lost civilization, with overgrown ruins, mystic crystals, and ancient psychic knowledge. Coral-crusted towers and glowing crystal vaults lie half drowned beneath the waves or hidden in jungle. Players dive, explore, and decode relics left by a mind-powered people, while rival treasure hunters and awakened guardians contest every artifact.
- El Dorado: legendary city of gold hidden in jungle highlands, luring explorers with riches, deadly traps, and treacherous guides. Rumors speak of golden streets, shining temples, and a ruler dusted in gold. Players mount expeditions through fever-ridden jungle, uncover false trails, and risk death, mutiny, and rival conquistadors for treasure that may be a trap or a myth.
- Shangri-La: secluded mountain valley paradise of calm, longevity, and learning, hidden by blizzards and reached only by lucky wanderers. Terraced gardens, quiet monasteries, and a gentle climate shelter residents who age slowly. Players arrive weary and battered, find rest and ancient libraries, and must decide whether to stay in peace or carry the secret back to a troubled world.
## Military & Battlefield
- Castle: stone stronghold with curtain walls, towers, gatehouse, and keep, providing lordly residence, defensive chokepoints, and siege objectives. Banners flutter over courtyards crowded with smiths, stables, and servants, while archers patrol the battlements. Players can defend it against assault, siege it with engines and tunnels, or intrigue within its halls for a lord's favor.
- Keep: massive fortified central tower, last refuge of a castle, with thick walls, narrow windows, dungeons, and armories. Cold stone corridors wind upward past guard rooms and storerooms, with a deep cellar below for prisoners and supplies. Players make a desperate last stand here, hunt for hidden passages, or storm the final stronghold to capture the lord within.
- Citadel: heavily fortified inner stronghold dominating a city, with layered walls, garrison barracks, and commanding views over approaches. Built on the highest ground, it watches both the streets below and every road outside. Players may command its garrison, quell uprisings from its gates, or fight tier by tier through defenses meant to protect rulers from their own people.
- Fortress: bastioned, sprawling defensive installation with ramparts, moats, and cannons, designed to hold borders against long sieges. Angled walls deflect shot, and cross-fire covers every approach, while storehouses hold months of supplies. Players can defend against wave after wave, plan costly assaults with sappers and artillery, or starve out the garrison in a grinding siege.
- Siege Camp: sprawling encampment of tents, catapults, and campfires ringing an enemy stronghold, vulnerable to sorties, disease, and raiders. Mud, smoke, and the rhythmic thud of engines hang over rows of supply wagons and idle soldiers. Players manage morale and supplies, repel night raids from the walls, and decide when to storm the gates or wait for hunger to win.
- Siege Tower Field: churned ground below walls with rolling towers, rams, ladders, and burning pitch, bloody and chaotic during assaults. Mud sucks at boots while arrows fall and boiling oil spills from the battlements. Players push towers forward under cover, fight on narrow platforms atop the wall, and race to breach the defenses before the attackers are cut down.
- Trench Line: muddy zig-zag earthworks reinforced with sandbags and barbed wire, under shellfire, with rats, dugouts, and terrified soldiers. Water pools in the duckboards, and the air smells of smoke, rot, and wet wool. Players endure bombardment, repel night raids, hold a short stretch of ground, and plan perilous charges over the parapet into open fire.
- No-Man's-Land: cratered, barbed-wire wasteland between enemy lines, swept by machine guns and shells, with corpses and craters offering cover. Nothing grows, flares light the dark, and every movement draws fire. Players crawl between shell holes, cut wire, rescue wounded comrades, and scout enemy positions, knowing that a single exposed step may be the last.
- Bunker Complex: reinforced concrete shelter network with corridors, armories, and command rooms, damp, dim, and hard to assault. Heavy doors, buzzing lamps, and echoing halls hide stores, generators, and communications gear. Players clear it room by room, hunt for plans or prisoners, or hold out inside while the enemy hammers the outer defenses.
- Watchtower: tall lookout post with signal fire or lamp, offering vision over surrounding land, archer positions, and early warning against raiders. Wind whistles through the open top where a sentry scans the horizon day and night. Players can man it as a lookout, light the beacon to rally allies, or capture it to blind the enemy and control the surrounding terrain.
- Border Wall: long fortified barrier with patrols, checkpoints, and watchtowers, controlling crossings and testing smugglers, spies, and infiltrators. Stone or concrete stretches to the horizon, broken by gates and searchlights where guards inspect papers. Players may bribe officials, tunnel beneath, scale at night, or defend the line against refugees, raiders, and rival armies.
- Barricade Street: urban lane blocked with carts, furniture, and rubble, defended by rebels, with rooftop snipers and tight street fighting. Overturned wagons and pried-up cobblestones form a ragged wall as flags hang from windows. Players hold the line against advancing troops, rally citizens, and fight from rooftops and doorways in a doomed or glorious stand.
- Naval Fleet Harbor: crowded military port of warships, docks, cranes, and barracks, with cannons, patrol boats, and tight security. Gray hulls line the quays amid shouting crews, hissing steam, and the clang of repair work. Players can infiltrate to sabotage ships, steal plans, or defend the base against raids, while commanding fleets heading out to battle.
- Battlefield Meadow: open grassy field of clashing armies, scattered banners, fallen soldiers, and trampled flowers, ideal for large-scale engagements. Dust and drumbeats rise as lines of infantry and cavalry meet in thunderous charges. Players command formations, exploit terrain and flanks, and later wander the same ground among the dead, searching for survivors, loot, and the fallen standard.
- Ambush Pass: narrow canyon or forest road with high ground on both sides, perfect for traps, rockfalls, archers, and bloody surprises. The silence is thick, and every shadow could hide an enemy waiting for the signal. Players either spring the trap with careful planning or must detect the danger in time to avoid being slaughtered in a column.
- Fortified Bridge: stone crossing with gatehouse towers, portcullis, and defenders, controlling river passage and creating a deadly chokepoint for armies. Arrow slits overlook the narrow roadway, and the water below is deep and swift. Players hold the span against overwhelming numbers, fight through a gauntlet of defenders, or sabotage the supports to deny the enemy a crossing.
- Missile Silo: underground launch complex with a hardened hatch, long shafts, blast doors, and control rooms, grim, sterile, and dangerous. Fluorescent corridors and cold steel stairs descend toward a looming warhead and hum with machinery. Players infiltrate to stop or seize a launch, race against countdown timers, and confront automated defenses and soldiers sworn to guard it.
- Training Ground: dusty drill yard with obstacle courses, dummies, archery butts, and sparring rings, where recruits learn skills and instructors test them. Sergeants shout orders as sweating trainees practice swings, shots, and formations. Players can hone abilities, take on challenge courses for rewards, make rivals and friends among recruits, and prove themselves before real combat.
- Barracks Yard: open yard before soldier dormitories, with drills, inspections, gambling, and gossip, bustling at dawn and tense before deployment. Soldiers polish gear, trade rumors, and play dice in the shade of long, plain buildings. Players gather news, recruit allies, settle grudges, and sense the mood of the troops before orders come to march.
- War Camp: rough tent city of an army on campaign, with cookfires, banners, supply wagons, war councils, and nervous sentries. Smoke drifts over rows of canvas, horses, and forges while officers argue over maps. Players can recruit soldiers, trade for supplies, listen in on council plans, and defend the camp from raids and treacherous spies at night.
- Palisade Fort: log-walled frontier stockade with watchtowers and a single gate, defended by settlers or soldiers against raids. Sharpened timber walls enclose a muddy yard, a well, and a few crowded buildings. Players hold out against attackers who may burn it, fight for supplies, and weigh whether to stand firm or break out into the wilderness.
- Armory: weapon storehouse of racks, forges, and crates, guarded by quartermasters, offering gear upgrades and tempting thieves. The room smells of oil and iron, with swords, shields, and armor sorted in tidy rows. Players can repair, upgrade, and purchase equipment, bribe the keeper, or plan a daring heist to arm a rebellion.
- Dry Dock: drained basin holding a ship under repair, with scaffolding, cranes, hammers, and tar, tense during sabotage or siege. The hull towers overhead, propped on timber while workers swarm over planks and rigging. Players can repair or refit vessels, sneak aboard to plant charges, or fight among the cranes when attackers strike the vulnerable ship.
- Command Bunker: fortified war room with maps, radios, and officers, buried below ground, central to strategy and prime target for infiltrators. Pins and markers cover map tables as messengers rush in with reports and radios crackle. Players issue orders that shape the battle, uncover traitors, or infiltrate to steal plans and cripple enemy command.
- Airbase: flat strip of runways, hangars, fuel dumps, and control towers, with parked aircraft, patrols, and vulnerable ground crews. Engines roar as planes launch and land while mechanics swarm around fuel trucks and ordnance. Players can raid it to destroy aircraft on the ground, defend against bombers, or fly missions that depend on its supplies and runways.
- Scorched Earth Front: ravaged land of burned fields, ruined villages, and smoking wells, stripped of resources and littered with abandoned equipment. Blackened stumps and ash cover everything, and survivors trudge through the haze. Players search for scarce food and water, scavenge wreckage, and face the moral cost of strategies that leave nothing behind for the enemy or civilians.
- Refugee Camp: crowded tent settlement of displaced families, with scarce food, disease, aid workers, and desperate traders under constant threat. Smoke and chatter rise from rows of patched shelters, and children and elders wait in line for rations. Players can deliver aid, broker deals, uncover thieves and informants, and protect people from raids and sickness.
- Field Hospital: makeshift medical tents near the front, full of wounded, surgeons, and bloody cots, offering healing but constant danger. Lantern light shows exhausted medics working through the night while shells thud in the distance. Players can recover health, ferry wounded from the line, scavenge medicine, and defend the tents from raiders and sudden attack.
- Sea Fort: stone or iron fortress on a rock or artificial island, with cannon batteries guarding harbors and difficult approach by boat. Waves slam against thick walls while gunners track every ship that passes. Players may attack by night with small boats, run past its guns, or hold it against fleets, supplied only by sea and weather.
- Tank Graveyard: field of rusting wrecked armor and twisted metal, offering salvage, cover, scavengers, and unexploded ordnance among the hulks. Turrets point at nothing while wind whistles through rusted hatches and shell holes.
