Players hunt for parts and fuel, hide from patrols among the wrecks, and risk traps, mines, and rival scavengers who claim the field.
## Settlements & Dwellings
### Wilderness Dwellings
- Nomad Camp: Portable tents and campfires are pitched near water, surrounded by grazing herds, loaded wagons, and wary scouts watching the horizon. The camp feels lively but provisional, ready to move at a moment's notice. Players can trade for mounts and rumors, join a migration, or defend the herd from raiders.
- Hunter-Gatherer Camp: A small band's temporary shelters sit near game trails and berry patches, with drying racks, stone tools, and wary watchers posted at the edges. Life here is tied to seasons and tracking, and food is never certain. Players might join a hunt, learn foraging lore, or be mistaken for rival trespassers.
- Tent Camp: Temporary canvas shelters cluster around a fire pit, used by travelers, soldiers, or explorers who plan to stay only briefly. The camp is vulnerable to weather, mud, and raiders, and smells of smoke and wet cloth. Players can rest, resupply, overhear orders, or defend it against a night attack.
- Hermit Hut: A tiny remote cabin is hidden in woods or hills, with a thin trail of smoke and herbs hung to dry by the door. Its reclusive owner may be a sage, a healer, or an outlaw with secrets. Players seek cures, forbidden knowledge, or shelter, and must earn trust before the door opens.
- Treehouse Village: Platforms, huts, and rope bridges hang high in the canopy, safe from ground predators and swaying gently in the wind. Forest folk and archers live here, watching every approaching traveler from above. Players can negotiate for passage, seek guides, or fight to save the village when fire or invaders threaten the trees.
- Cave Dwelling: A natural rock shelter serves as a home, with smoky hearths, hanging furs, and a cool interior that stays dry only near the mouth. It is easily defended but damp and cramped further inside. Players may find hospitable clans, old wall paintings, or deeper tunnels that someone sealed for a reason.
- Cliff Dwelling: Houses are built into canyon walls and reached only by ladders or ropes, secure from attackers and hidden from casual view. Winds whistle through the stone rooms, and granaries are tucked into high ledges. Players can climb into a hidden stronghold, trade with wary inhabitants, or find it abandoned and haunted.
- Igloo Village: A cluster of snow-block domes sits on the ice, joined by low tunnels and lit by seal oil lamps, with sled dogs yapping in the cold. Brutal wind rages outside while the interiors stay surprisingly warm. Players can shelter from blizzards, trade for furs and fish, or join an ice-hole hunt.
- Houseboat Harbor: A sheltered anchorage of moored homes rocks on the water, linked by planks and dinghies, with laundry lines and lanterns overhead. Lively floating markets share the water with smugglers and quiet informants. Players can trade, hide among the boats, chase a thief across the planks, or hunt for a missing vessel.
- Lighthouse Rock: A solitary tower stands on a wave-battered crag, with a beacon, keeper's quarters, and shipwreck salvage piled in the lower rooms. Storms lash the rock, and ghostly signals sometimes answer the light. Players can weather a gale, scavenge wrecks, or uncover why the keeper still tends a lamp for ships that never arrive.
### Rural
- Hamlet: A tiny cluster of cottages gathers around a well, home to a few farmers and a small shrine, with no real defenses to speak of. Everyone knows everyone, and strangers are noticed at once. Players can find a bed and a hot meal, hear local worries, and stumble into small mysteries.
- Village: A modest farming community built around a green, with an inn, chapel, and mill, offers supplies, rumors, and simple quests. Smoke rises from chimneys and market days draw neighbors together. Players can restock, rest, settle disputes, and pick up first adventures such as missing livestock, wolves, or a suspicious stranger.
- Farmstead: An isolated homestead of farmhouse, barn, and fields supplies food, shelter, and honest work to passing travelers. Distance from neighbors makes it vulnerable to raiders and wolves. Players can trade labor for lodging, defend the family through a night siege, or discover why the farmer's dogs will not stop barking.
- Manor House: The fortified country residence of a local lord sits among gardens, stables, and busy servants, with politics humming behind every door. It anchors the surrounding countryside and its quarrels over land and taxes. Players can seek audience, spy on rivals, investigate a scandal, or serve as guards at a tense feast.
- Fishing Village: Seaside huts cluster beside drying nets, beached boats, and smokehouses, and the whole place stinks of brine and fish. Gossip travels quickly between the docks, smugglers use quiet coves, and sea monsters lurk offshore. Players can hire a boat, buy rumors, join a risky catch, or investigate vanished crews.
- Mountain Village: Stone houses cling to steep slopes with terraced plots, goats, and narrow paths, isolated by snow and avalanches for much of the year. Residents are hardy and wary of outsiders. Players can find shelter during storms, hire guides for high passes, or help dig out neighbors buried by a slide.
- Mining Camp: A rough settlement of tents, shacks, and tunnels springs up beside a dig, bustling with prospectors, claim disputes, and gold fever. Cave-ins and cheap whiskey make life dangerous and short. Players can stake claims, guard shipments, break up fights, or follow rumors of a rich vein leading into something worse.
- Logging Camp: A forest clearing holds bunkhouses, sawpits, and piles of felled timber, noisy with axes and shouting crews. Rivers jammed with floating logs make the work deadly. Players can take on labor, clear a log jam, deal with saboteurs, or learn what is making the woodcutters afraid to go deeper into the trees.
- Trading Post: A remote outpost of storehouse, hitching posts, and traders swaps furs, tools, and news with travelers and locals. Shelves hold odd goods, and the owner knows every route in the region. Players can barter, take freight jobs, meet rival merchants, and learn which roads are safe and which are not.
- Frontier Town: A dusty boomtown of saloons, stables, and false-front shops sits on the edge of settlement, loud with piano music and gunshots. Lawmen, outlaws, and speculators jostle for control of the street. Players can take bounties, join a posse, win a poker pot, or survive a noon standoff.
- Ranch: A sprawling livestock property of corrals, bunkhouse, and open range is run by hardy cowhands under a stubborn owner. Rustlers, drought, and predators constantly threaten the herd. Players can ride on a cattle drive, track thieves across the range, break wild horses, or defend the homestead from a rival's hired guns.
### Urban
- Town: A bustling market settlement of houses, shops, taverns, and a guard post offers services, jobs, rumors, and mild danger. Carts crowd the main street and the watch keeps a nervous eye on newcomers. Players can resupply, hire help, gather leads, and get tangled in local feuds.
- Walled City: A dense settlement is enclosed by stout ramparts and gates, with tight streets, vigilant guards, and curfew bells that mark the day's end. Safe from outside threats, it can feel crowded and controlling. Players can face gate inspections, hunt for smuggling routes over the wall, or weather a siege from within.
- City: A large urban center of crowded districts, markets, temples, and guilds offers endless shopping, crime, politics, and adventure hooks. Every quarter has its own character, from glittering plazas to shadowed backstreets. Players can join factions, chase leads across districts, run errands for patrons, or vanish into the anonymous crowd.
- Capital: The seat of government rises with palaces, courts, grand avenues, and embassies, brimming with nobles, spies, bureaucrats, and grand ceremonies. Power concentrates here and every conversation has a hidden agenda. Players can petition officials, attend royal festivals, uncover conspiracies, or navigate rivalries between ministries and foreign envoys.
- Port City: A harbor metropolis of docks, warehouses, taverns, and shipyards mixes foreign sailors, merchants, pirates, press gangs, and smuggling. The air smells of tar and spice and the night is never quiet. Players can book passage, load contraband, hire crews, evade the press gang, or follow a mysterious cargo.
- Metropolis: A sprawling modern city of towers, traffic, and neon is thick with crowds, corporations, crime, and layered districts that never sleep. Rain-slick streets reflect endless signs and sirens. Players can chase leads between rooftops and basements, work for rival companies, hunt fugitives, or get lost in the density.
- Megacity: A gigantic overbuilt urban mass of stacked districts, endless suburbs, and megastructures is almost its own world, ruled by gangs and corporations. Daylight barely reaches the lower levels. Players can climb social tiers, navigate gang territories, find hidden sanctuaries, or discover that entire neighborhoods have vanished from official maps.
- Slum: A crowded shantytown of shacks and open sewers is rich in street life, crime, disease, informants, and desperate resilience. Narrow lanes echo with hawkers and children. Players can find unexpected allies, trade in black markets, outrun enforcers, or help a community under threat from landlords and plague.
- Suburb: An orderly residential ring of houses, lawns, and quiet streets hides secrets, local rivalries, and strange neighbors behind neat hedges. Everything seems calm until the curtains twitch at night. Players can investigate odd disappearances, canvass for clues, attend tense block parties, or uncover what lies beneath a perfect cul-de-sac.
- Alleyway Maze: A tangled network of narrow passages and dead ends winds between tall buildings, dripping with laundry and shadow. It is ideal for chases, ambushes, shady deals, and hidden shops. Players can lose pursuers, find secret doors, meet fences and informants, or get hopelessly lost with a threat close behind.
- Rooftop Network: A skyline of connected roofs, ledges, and ladders sits above the streets, windy and exposed, and favored by thieves, parkour runners, and snipers. Gaps and slick tiles make every move risky. Players can bypass guarded entrances, stage ambushes, spy on the city below, or flee a pursuer across a daring jump.
- Sewer System: Dank tunnels run beneath the city, with filthy water, rats, smugglers, and hidden lairs in the dark. Gratings and side channels offer bypasses around guarded locations. Players can sneak between districts, hunt strange creatures, discover forgotten shrines or vaults, and risk foul gas, floods, and disease.
- Subway Tunnels: Dark underground rail corridors and abandoned stations are lit by sparking cables and echoing with distant trains. Scavengers and lurking horrors stalk the platforms. Players can use the tracks as hidden routes, loot derelict stations, flee an oncoming train, or investigate why a whole line was quietly shut down.
- Underground City: A subterranean settlement of carved halls, lit tunnels, and fungus farms is sealed from the sun and sheltered from surface threats. The air is damp and the society is tightly ordered. Players can trade for rare goods, navigate rigid laws, uncover a rumored breach to the surface, or fight to stop a collapse.
- Skyscraper District: A forest of glass towers, plazas, and sky bridges is packed with offices, security, and elevators that never stop. The wealthy work above clouds while the street is a blur of commuters. Players can plan heists, infiltrate boardrooms, scale facades, hack lobbies, or flee across sky bridges as alarms sound.
- Tower Block: A tall residential slab holds cramped apartments, graffiti-covered corridors, broken lifts, and tight-knit, rough communities. Every floor has its own rules and rivals. Players can climb stairwell by stairwell, earn trust from residents, investigate a crime behind a locked door, or defend neighbors from an eviction crew.
- Gated Estate: A wealthy walled enclave of mansions and manicured lawns is watched by guards and cameras, and is quietly full of secrets. The calm exterior hides jealous households and hidden vices. Players can tempt burglars with rich targets, investigate a suspicious death, pose as staff, or evade patrols on a moonless night.
- Palace: An opulent royal residence of gilded halls, gardens, and guards is packed with courtiers, treasure, assassins, and ceremony. Every corridor hides a whispering faction. Players can seek audience with the ruler, attend a lavish banquet, uncover a poison plot, sneak through servants' passages, or steal a crown jewel.
- Mansion Grounds: A sprawling estate of gardens, hedge maze, outbuildings, and a big house is favored for parties, mysteries, and heists. Gravel paths, statues, and shuttered greenhouses offer plenty of cover. Players can search for a missing heir, mingle with guests, sneak past hounds, or solve a murder before the gates open.
- Castle Town: A bustling settlement clusters around a lord's fortress, filled with craftsmen, soldiers, and markets that serve the garrison. Walls and gatehouses loom over its roofs. Players can buy arms and supplies, take commissions from the keep, gather news of the lord's moods, and seek refuge within the walls during sieges.
- Garden District: A leafy upscale quarter of villas, parks, fountains, and quiet cafes feels tranquil, but gossip and discreet crime thrive beneath the trees. Residents guard their reputations carefully. Players can eavesdrop on social calls, investigate a quiet scandal, meet well-connected patrons, or trail a suspect through manicured walks.
- Canal City: A waterway-laced town of bridges, gondolas, and quays replaces carts with boats, and intrigue floods narrow lanes. Mist drifts over the water at dawn, and masked figures vanish around corners. Players can chase through boats, duel on bridges, meet contacts at waterside cafes, or follow a mysterious barge.
- Floating City: A sprawling settlement on rafts, pontoons, or sky platforms drifts or stays anchored, with docks, markets, and precarious foundations that creak in rough weather. Its streets can move, break apart, or collide with others. Players can trade among drifting districts, repair failing supports, race storms, or discover who steers the city.
### Interiors
- Tavern: A smoky drinking hall with roaring fires, lively music, and frequent brawls is the classic spot for rumors, hirelings, quest givers, and trouble. Regulars guard their corners and the barkeep hears everything. Players can gather leads, recruit companions, win a wager, or be pulled into a fight they did not start.
- Inn: A roadside lodging with beds, stable, and common room offers rest, meals, gossip, and secret visitors in the night. Travelers of every stripe share a roof and a fire. Players can trade stories, overhear a hushed conversation, defend their room from intruders, or find that a fellow guest is not who they claim.
- Great Hall: The grand feasting chamber of a lord has long tables, hanging banners, a huge hearth, and minstrels in the corner. It hosts court, council, and betrayal in equal measure. Players can attend a tense feast, present a petition, spot a poisoned cup, or defend the hall when a rival's men burst in.
- Throne Room: A ceremonial chamber with a raised seat, watchful guards, and heavy tapestries is where rulers judge, petitioners plead, and plots unfold. Every footstep echoes and every gesture is measured. Players can plead a case, expose a traitor, face a trial, or slip through a side door during an audience.
- Kitchen: A busy cooking space of ovens, pots, and pantries bustles with cooks, scullions, knives, and stolen scraps, and it crosses the path of every servant. Steam, noise, and heat provide cover. Players can sneak food, eavesdrop on staff, poison a dish, escape through a service door, or hide in a cold pantry.
- Cellar: A cool underground storeroom of barrels, crates, and cobwebs hides vermin, wine, secret doors, and forgotten horrors. Lamps flicker and the dark presses in at the edges. Players can search for hidden passages, find old supplies, escape pursuers through a trapdoor, or discover what was walled up behind the oldest casks.
- Attic: A dusty top-floor storage space under the rafters is full of trunks, relics, cobwebs, and ghostly keepsakes of past residents. Floorboards creak and light filters in through grimy windows. Players can uncover family secrets, find forgotten letters or weapons, hide from pursuers, or meet a presence that still lingers among the old things.
- Bedchamber: A private sleeping chamber with canopy bed, wardrobe, and hearth offers rest, secrets, hidden compartments, and nighttime intruders. Perfumed linen and half-hidden diaries tell stories of the occupant. Players can search for clues, hide behind curtains, slip out a window, sleep safely, or defend themselves against an assassin at midnight.
- Courtyard: An open inner yard of a castle or manor holds a well, stables, and gathering crowds, and is central to movement and ambush. Archers watch from walls above while servants hurry across the cobbles. Players can stage a duel, watch a muster, stage a distraction, or fight their way through the surrounding gates.
- Ballroom: A sweeping dance hall with chandeliers, polished floors, and musicians is ideal for masked intrigue, romance, assassinations, and grand entrances. Whispered alliances shift with each dance. Players can charm nobles, pass secret messages, spot a hidden blade, steal away to a balcony, or cause a scandal that changes the evening.
- Guild Hall: The headquarters of a trade or adventuring guild has meeting rooms, archives, banners, and job boards, and acts as a hub for quests. Members debate rules, rank, and dues in loud voices. Players can accept contracts, research old records, earn standing, challenge a rival, or uncover corruption in the guild's leadership.
- Smithy: A hot workshop of anvil, forge, and bellows is where smiths craft and repair weapons and tools amid sparks and hammering. The air shimmers with heat and smells of coal and hot metal. Players can commission arms, repair gear, learn crafting secrets, hide among the noise, or deal with a customer whose order is cursed.
- Chapel: A small consecrated room of pews, candles, and altar offers healing, sanctuary, confession, and quiet places for secret meetings. Soft light and old stone make voices carry in whispers. Players can seek blessings, shelter from pursuers, overhear a confessional secret, meet a contact, or discover a crypt hidden beneath the altar.
## Trade & Transport
### Roads & Crossings
- Dirt Road: A rutted, dusty track runs between villages, turning to mud in rain, and is traveled by carts and peddlers. Lonely stretches pass fields and forests where little help is within shouting distance. Players can meet merchants, camp by the roadside, repair a broken wheel, or face bandits waiting at a bend.
- Cobblestone Street: A paved city lane of rounded stones is noisy with hooves and lined with shops, awnings, and gutters. Wet slippery patches make fast movement treacherous, especially at night. Players can chase a thief through traffic, browse stalls, meet a contact on a corner, or stage an ambush from a doorway.
- Highway: A broad modern road of lanes, signs, and rest stops is built for speed, with heavy traffic, checkpoints, and roadside diners. Headlights streak by after dark and long stretches feel empty. Players can chase vehicles, hitch rides, stop at a seedy diner, evade a roadblock, or break down far from help.
- Mountain Pass Road: A narrow winding route crosses a high saddle, prone to rockfall, snow, bandits, and dizzying drops beside a thin guardrail or none at all. Thin air and sharp wind wear down travelers. Players can escort a caravan, outrun an avalanche, hold a choke point, or find a shelter before nightfall.
- Switchback Trail: A zigzag path climbs a steep slope, slow and exposed, with ambush points at each turn and fine overlooks at the corners. Loose gravel and sharp drops punish carelessness. Players can fight from higher ground, spot distant dangers, race a rival to the top, or push a heavy cart uphill.
- Silk Road Route: An ancient caravan trail links distant empires, thick with merchants, camels, oases, bandits, and exotic goods from far lands. Dust, heat, and languages mix along the way. Players can join or guard a caravan, trade rare wares, seek water at a half-forgotten oasis, or follow rumors of a lost city.
- Royal Road: A well-maintained imperial highway comes with waystations, relay riders, patrols, and tolls, enabling fast official travel across vast territory. Banners mark the route and soldiers watch every crossing. Players can carry urgent messages, bribe a toll collector, ride with a convoy, or ambush a courier whose pouch holds secrets.
- Game Trail: A faint animal path winds through the wilds, used by hunters and trackers, and leads to water, prey, and unexpected predators. Broken branches and tracks tell stories to those who can read them. Players can stalk quarry, find hidden clearings, follow signs of something large, or become the hunted themselves.
- Rope Bridge: A swaying span of planks and cord stretches across a chasm, creaking underfoot, easily cut, and perfect for tense crossings. Wind tugs at it and the drop below fades into mist. Players can balance past hazards, cut it to stop pursuers, fight on the planks, or find that it has been tampered with.
- Stone Bridge: A sturdy arched crossing spans a river, durable and wide, offering a chokepoint for toll collectors, battles, and ambushing trolls. Moss covers its piers and the water churns below. Players can hold it against an army, pay or dodge tolls, search for something hidden beneath the arch, or confront a monstrous guardian.
- Drawbridge: A raised or lowered span before a castle gate controls access across a moat, vulnerable to sabotage and timing puzzles. Chains rattle and the heavy planks thud into place. Players can sneak across while it is down, cut the chains, race a closing gate, or lure guards into opening it at the wrong moment.
- Toll Gate: A barrier on a road or bridge is where guards collect fees, inspect goods, and tempt bribery, smuggling, and disputes. Lines of wagons and travelers grumble in the queue. Players can pay, bribe, disguise contraband, argue with officials, run the barrier, or discover that the toll collectors are not what they seem.
- Mountain Tunnel: A dark bored passage through rock is echoing and damp, used by trains or caravans, and prone to collapses and lurking creatures. Lamps barely reach the walls, and sound travels strangely. Players can race an oncoming train, repair a failing support, ambush travelers, or find something living in the old side shafts.
### Water Transport
- Harbor: A sheltered coastal anchorage is ringed by quays, warehouses, and breakwaters, and busy with ships, sailors, customs officials, and rumors of distant lands. Gulls wheel overhead and cranes swing cargo. Players can hire passage, join a crew, smuggle goods past inspectors, or learn of a ship that never came home.
- Dock: A wooden or stone landing for loading cargo is stacked with crates and ropes, and crowded with stevedores, thieves, and rats. Lanterns swing in the dark and the water slaps against the pilings. Players can steal a shipment, follow a suspicious crate, fight on the planks, or find work as a laborer.
- Pier: A long platform juts over water for fishing, mooring, or amusements, with creaking planks, noisy gulls, and the occasional lurking smuggler. Fog can swallow the far end completely. Players can meet a contact at the end of the boards, fish for supper, chase a thief, or discover a body below.
- Canal: A man-made waterway for barges and boats is lined with towpaths and bridges, slow, calm, and perfect for chases. Reflected lamplight shimmers on the water at night. Players can hijack a barge, stalk a target along the towpath, drop from a bridge, or follow hidden passages that open from the banks.
- Canal Lock: A gated chamber raises or lowers boats between water levels, operated with winches by watchful keepers. Water roars through the sluices and the timing matters. Players can solve lock puzzles, delay pursuers by flooding or draining the chamber, bribe the keeper, or fight on slick gates as the water rises.
- Ferry Crossing: A raft or boat service across a river is run by a ferryman, offering a slow break, fares, and sneaky encounters. The current pulls at the rope or pole as passengers trade quiet words. Players can cross with strange company, bargain for a late ride, fend off river dangers, or hear the ferryman's strange tales.
- River Port: An inland dock town on a navigable river trades barges, timber, and grain, with mills, markets, and river pirates working the banks. Warehouses crowd the shore and the water is brown with traffic. Players can hire a barge, investigate smuggling, join a cargo run, or track a pirate gang to its hideout.
- Boat Landing: A simple riverbank or lakeside mooring of planks and posts serves fishermen and travelers, and it is quiet, muddy, and mildly exposed. A few skiffs bob against the posts and reeds rustle nearby. Players can rent a boat, wait for a meeting, ambush arrivals, or discover a hidden vessel tied up in the reeds.
- Lighthouse: A tall coastal tower with beacon, spiral stair, and keeper warns ships off the rocks and offers sweeping views, storms, and wreckers. The lamp turns through the night while waves boom below. Players can climb for a vantage, defend the light, uncover false-light wreckers, or investigate a keeper who has gone silent.
- Ship Lane: A marked sea route crosses open water, busy with traders and warships, and is prone to storms, pirates, and sea monsters. Days of salt and sky pass between ports, and a single sail on the horizon can mean fortune or danger. Players can escort cargo, hunt pirates, ride out a gale, or chase a legend.
### Rail & Air
- Railway Station: A busy hub of platforms, ticket halls, clocks, and steam or electric trains, crowded with passengers, porters, pickpockets, and spies. Announcements echo under iron roofs while schedules tick toward departure. Players catch trains, tail suspects, meet contacts, or lose a pursuer in the rush.
- Rail Yard: A sprawling field of tracks, sidings, locomotives, and freight cars, ideal for stealth, sabotage, chases, and hidden cargo. Switching engines shunt unpredictably and floodlights leave pools of shadow. Players sneak between boxcars, uncouple wagons, or hunt a single crate among hundreds.
- Train Tunnel: A dark rail bore through hills or beneath cities, noisy and cramped, with narrow walkways, oncoming trains, and sudden danger. Dripping walls and distant headlamps make every step tense. Players race timetables, flee along the ballast, or find maintenance alcoves hiding secret doors.
- Elevated Rail: A raised track on viaducts or pylons above streets, providing fast transit, rooftop views, and thrilling fights atop moving carriages. Wind tugs at clothing while the city blurs below. Players leap between cars, ambush from the girders, or cut the line to strand pursuers.
- Airport: A large complex of terminals, runways, hangars, and control tower, thick with security, crowds, cargo, and tense chases. Jet noise and announcements fill the air, and every gate is watched. Players smuggle goods, stow away on freighters, or race taxiing planes across the tarmac.
- Airfield: A small rough landing strip with a hangar and fuel drum, used by bush pilots, smugglers, and early aviators. Wind socks flap over patched canvas and oil-stained gravel. Players barter for flights, repair battered biplanes, or make desperate takeoffs with enemies firing from the treeline.
- Cable Car Line: Suspended gondolas hauled along cables across valleys or between peaks, offering scenic travel, swaying fights, and sabotage risks. Cabins creak and rock in the wind above dizzying drops. Players cross dangerous gaps, fight on the roofs, or cut cables to strand pursuers midair.
- Zipline Route: A pulley-slung rope descending between platforms, offering rapid traversal, daring escapes, and thrilling jungle or canopy shortcuts. The line hums as riders whip past branches and ravines. Frayed cables, ambushing predators, and mistimed landings add danger, while the speed rewards bold players and fleeing heroes.
### Markets
- Bazaar: A sprawling labyrinth of stalls, awnings, and shouting vendors selling spices, cloth, and curiosities, rife with haggling and thieves. Smells of cumin and leather hang in the heat. Players barter for rare goods, chase pickpockets through alleys, or follow whispered rumors to hidden back rooms.
- Market Square: An open town plaza filled with stalls, fountains, and a central cross, hosting trade, executions, festivals, and public announcements. Crowds shift with the hour and the news. Players buy supplies, gather rumors, witness proclamations, or stage a daring rescue before the whole town.
- Auction Hall: A grand room with raised block, rows of bidders, and discreet guards, selling treasures, slaves, or relics to high bidders. Paddles rise and whispered bids pass between masked buyers. Players bid, forge provenance, rob the vault, or rescue a person being sold.
- Night Market: Lantern-lit stalls open after dark, selling street food, trinkets, and illicit goods amid crowds, steam, and neon. Sizzling woks and haggling voices fill narrow lanes. Players sample strange wares, meet informants, hunt rare items, or disappear into the glowing throng.
- Floating Market: Boats laden with produce and goods jostling on a river or canal, bartering from deck to deck. Sellers shout over splashing oars and drifting smoke from cooking boats. Players trade by leaping between hulls, escape pursuers across moving decks, or hide contraband under fruit baskets.
- Merchant Quarter: An affluent district of trading houses, warehouses, and counting rooms, home to wealthy traders, guild politics, and guarded vaults. Fine facades hide ledgers, debts, and quiet bribes. Players broker deals, steal account books, sabotage rivals, or cultivate patrons who open doors elsewhere.
- Smuggler's Cove: A hidden sea inlet with caves, rowboats, and lookouts, where contraband changes hands away from customs and law. Tides hide the entrance and lanterns signal from the cliffs. Players buy forbidden cargo, evade revenue cutters, hide treasure, or ambush a rival crew landing goods.
- Black Market Alley: A shadowy backstreet of whispered deals, locked doors, and covert vendors selling stolen, banned, and dangerous wares. Passwords open shutters and lookouts watch every stranger. Players find rare gear, fence loot, buy forged papers, and risk raids, scams, or betrayal by informants.
- Warehouse District: A grid of large storage buildings, cranes, and loading docks, quiet at night, ideal for heists, fights, and shady meetings. Stacked crates form mazes beneath sodium lamps. Players break in, tail shipments, ambush rivals among the containers, or uncover cargo that was never supposed to arrive.
- Customs House: An official building at port or border where cargo is taxed and inspected, ripe with bribery, forgery, and bureaucratic tangles. Clerks stamp ledgers while inspectors pry open crates. Players smuggle goods past scrutiny, forge permits, bribe officials, or steal manifests that expose conspiracies.
- Mall: An enclosed shopping center of stores, food courts, escalators, and parking, offering supplies, crowds, security cameras, and surprising battle arenas. Bright signs and muzak mask long, empty corridors after hours. Players scavenge goods, hide from pursuers, fight across atriums, or defend a barricaded storefront.
## Agriculture & Pastoral
### Crop Land
- Wheat Field: A golden, rippling expanse of grain under open sky, offering cover for ambushes, scarecrows, harvest festivals, and fire risks. Wind sweeps the stalks in long waves and hides anyone crouching low. Players track movement through the grain, join harvests, or flee a blaze racing across dry fields.
- Rice Paddy: A flooded terraced field of green shoots, tended by farmers wading in mud, with water buffalo, frogs, and snakes. Reflections shimmer and footing is slow and treacherous. Players cross narrow dikes, hide among the stalks, help with planting, or ambush enemies bogged in the muck.
- Terraced Rice: Stair-stepped paddies carved into hillsides and fed by channels, glittering with reflected sky, steep, slippery, and labor-intensive. Water trickles from level to level through ancient sluices. Players climb the walls, divert irrigation to flood foes, or defend a mountain village's precious harvest.
- Vineyard: Neat rows of grapevines on sunny slopes, with press houses, cellars, pickers, and tipsy intrigue during harvest season. Warm air smells of crushed fruit and oak. Players taste vintages, spy on estate rivals, hide in cool cellars, or sabotage a family's prized barrels.
- Orchard: Ordered rows of fruit trees giving shade, seasonal blossoms, ladders, and bees, good for picnics, hiding, and foraging. Petals drift in spring and windfalls rot sweetly in autumn. Players gather fruit, climb for vantage points, meet in secret beneath the boughs, or flee through the rows.
- Olive Grove: Gnarled silver-leaved trees on dry hillsides, with stone presses, harvest nets, pressing season, and ancient wisdom. Some trees are older than the villages around them. Players harvest oil, shelter in hollow trunks, uncover buried relics among the roots, or defend the grove from raiders and wildfire.
- Hop Yard: Tall trellises of climbing bines for brewing, with drying kilns, pickers, and a sweet bitter scent in late summer. Green walls of vines make corridors that muffle sound. Players pick hops for brewers, hide in the foliage, or trace oast-house secrets and local feuds.
- Cotton Field: White-tufted plants stretching across warm flat land, with pickers, wagons, gins, and a hard history of labor. Heat shimmers over the rows and bolls catch on clothes. Players work or free laborers, trade the crop, hide among the plants, and confront the moral weight of the land.
- Sugarcane Field: Tall dense stalks swaying in tropical heat, with cane knives, mills, and cover for ambushes and fires. Leaves are razor sharp and the air is thick with sweetness and insects. Players cut paths through the cane, flee burning fields, ambush from within, or run a mill.
- Tea Plantation: Tidy green bushes on misty hillsides, tended by pickers, with drying sheds, terraces, and cool, fragrant air. Mornings arrive wrapped in cloud above the rows. Players trade fine leaves, tour estate houses, uncover planter intrigue, or hide along the steep paths between terraces.
- Coffee Hillside: Shady slopes of glossy bushes with red cherries, pickers, drying patios, and mills, rich with aroma and smuggled beans. Birdsong fills the canopy and beans dry in the sun. Players harvest or haul crops, dodge bandits on mountain tracks, and bargain with growers and exporters.
- Pumpkin Patch: An autumn field of sprawling vines and orange gourds, with scarecrows, harvest games, hay rides, and spooky seasonal events. Mist curls at dusk and carved faces glow after dark. Players pick prizes, join festivals, investigate eerie scarecrows, or battle things that wake on harvest night.
- Sunflower Field: Tall golden blooms turning toward the sun, offering cheerful vistas, hiding places, bees, and summer festival backdrops. The stalks tower over most travelers and rustle with finches. Players wander for seeds and oil, hide or ambush in the dense rows, and meet friendly farmers.
- Lavender Field: Purple rows of fragrant blooms humming with bees, ideal for calm scenes, perfume distillers, and peaceful rural settings. The scent soothes and the colors stretch to the horizon. Players gather flowers for remedies, visit distilleries, rest between adventures, or meet quiet characters with secrets.
- Tulip Field: Vivid stripes of spring flowers in neat bulb rows, drawing tourists, traders, and windmill-dotted lowland scenery. Canals frame the colored bands under flat skies. Players trade rare bulbs, meet collectors, follow speculation rumors, or run through the blossoms during a chase in a festival season.
- Vegetable Gardens: Tidy plots of cabbage, beans, and roots, with scarecrows, compost heaps, rabbits, and busy family gardeners. Neat rows give way to muddy paths and tool sheds. Players forage, help with chores for rewards, fend off pests and thieves, or discover things buried in the soil.
- Cranberry Bog: Shallow flooded beds of floating red berries, harvested by wading workers, with mud, cold water, and autumn mist. The red carpet bobs against low sunlight and chilly breezes. Players wade through harvests, slip on hidden holes, ambush from the fog, or find lost objects below.
- Fallow Fields: Resting farmland of weeds, grass, and bare soil, offering open ground, wildlife, wandering livestock, and quiet travel. Wildflowers and hares claim the ridges while the soil recovers. Players camp, cross unobserved or exposed, stage skirmishes in open country, and meet shepherds or wanderers on the move.
- Terraced Hills: Steep slopes cut into stepped growing platforms with retaining walls, stairs, and irrigation, supporting dense mountain farming communities. Stone walls ladder up the landscape, and village rooftops cling to the edges. Players climb the tiers, defend or raid crops, divert water, and scout from above.
### Controlled Growing
- Greenhouse Complex: Glass or plastic houses of humid air, hanging plants, and irrigation, producing exotic crops, medicinal herbs, and cramped hiding spots. Condensation drips from the panes and the heat is heavy. Players grow rare ingredients, fight among the planters, or find overgrown halls where something strange thrives.
- Hydroponic Farm: Stacked trays of soilless crops under artificial light and pumps, humming, sterile, and vital to futuristic food supply. Rows of glowing green climb to the ceiling in clean white corridors. Players defend nutrient tanks, sabotage supply, repair failing systems, or discover illicit crops hidden among the shelves.
- Kitchen Garden: A small household plot of herbs, vegetables, and berries beside a cottage, supplying meals, remedies, and local gossip. A low fence and a gate keep out rabbits, mostly. Players gather food and ingredients, chat with neighbors, hide a stolen item in the beds, or tend a safe home base.
- Herb Garden: Fragrant beds of culinary and medicinal plants with labels, bees, and stone paths, tended by healers and alchemists. Scents mix thyme, sage, and bitter roots. Players harvest ingredients for potions and cures, steal rare cuttings, meet wise gardeners, and avoid carefully planted poisonous specimens.
- Allotments: A patchwork of small rented plots with sheds, compost, and scarecrows, bustling with hobbyist growers, rivalries, and neighborly gossip. Each plot bears its owner's quirks and homemade fixes. Players trade produce and tips, investigate petty thefts, shelter in sheds, and uncover secrets buried beneath prize vegetables.
### Livestock & Pastoral
- Paddock: A fenced enclosure for horses or cattle with a trough and shelter, used for training, breeding, and quiet dealings. Hooves churn the ground and the rails are scuffed smooth. Players tame or buy mounts, sneak animals out at night, hold secret meetings, or survive a spooked herd.
- Pasture: An open grassy range grazed by livestock, with fences, streams, and herders, peaceful but exposed to predators and weather. Cattle drift slowly under wide skies while dogs watch the edges. Players escort herds, defend them from wolves or rustlers, rest, forage, and shelter from sudden storms.
- Cattle Ranch: Vast grazing lands, corrals, and branding pens run by cowhands, vulnerable to rustlers, stampedes, drought, and range wars. Dust rises from herds and windmills creak above stock tanks. Players ride fence lines, drive cattle to market, face outlaws, and take sides in feuds over water and land.
- Sheep Down: Rolling chalk or grass hills grazed by flocks, with shepherds, dogs, wind, low stone walls, and hungry wolves. Skylarks sing over the empty curves of the land. Players guard flocks through lambing and storms, follow ancient tracks to barrows, and face predators at dusk.
- Alpine Pasture: A high summer meadow of wildflowers, cowbells, and herder huts beneath peaks, with sudden storms and cheese-making. Thin bright air carries distant bells and snowmelt streams. Players herd animals up the slopes, trade cheese, shelter from lightning, or cross the meadow en route to passes.
- Goat Terraces: Rocky stepped slopes where agile herds graze, with climbing paths, thorny brush, and precarious shepherds. Loose stones clatter downhill with every step. Players follow goat trails to hidden places, rescue stranded animals, hide from patrols among the ledges, and trade milk, cheese, and rumors.
- Pig Farm: Muddy pens, sties, and feed troughs, noisy and smelly, with slaughter days, truffle-hunting hogs, and hungry beasts. Squeals carry across the yard and boots sink into slop. Players hunt truffles, hide in the muck, deal with escaped boars, or discover what the farmer has been feeding them.
- Chicken Yard: A fenced barnyard of coops, nesting boxes, and scratching hens, offering eggs, noisy alarms, and fox raids. Feathers drift in the dust while a rooster announces every visitor. Players collect eggs, fend off predators, sneak past the alarmed flock, or find valuables hidden in the straw.
- Beehive Meadow: A flowering field dotted with hives and buzzing foragers, producing honey and wax, with stings, beekeepers, and magical hive queens. The air hums and sweet scents drift on warm breezes. Players harvest honey, calm swarms, trade wax, or encounter strange enchanted bees guarding secrets.
- Dairy Barn: A warm stall-filled building of cows, milking pails, and churns, with hay lofts, cheese rooms, and early-morning chores. Steam rises from the animals on cold dawns and the floor is damp. Players milk, make cheese, hide in the loft, or investigate noises among the stalls.
- Haystack Field: A cut meadow stacked with golden bales and stooks, offering hiding spots, fire hazards, harvest work, and playful pranks. The sweet smell of dry grass lingers into dusk. Players hide inside stacks, help bring in the harvest, ambush from behind bales, or fight a spreading blaze.
### Aquaculture
- Fish Pond: A still freshwater pool stocked with carp or trout, ringed by reeds and nets, tended by farmers and herons. Lily pads and ripples mark where fish feed beneath the surface. Players fish for supplies, poach from the owner, wade for lost items, or discover things sunk in the muck.
- Aquaculture Pens: Netted offshore or lake enclosures raising fish and shellfish, with feeding platforms, workers, and risk of storms and predators. Boardwalks sway over churning water and gulls circle above. Players service nets, raid or defend stocks, repair storm damage, and fight predators attracted by the bait.
- Duck Marsh: Reedy shallows alive with ducks and geese, managed by decoy hunters and egg gatherers, noisy, muddy, and hidden. Mist hangs over flat water and every step sloshes. Players hunt or gather eggs, hide in blinds, follow secret punts through the channels, and ambush enemies who cannot see far.
## Industry & Resource
### Mining
- Open Pit: A vast terraced excavation spiraling downward, with haul roads, dump trucks, blasting zones, and unstable benches. Dust hangs in the air and sirens warn before each blast. Players race trucks along ramps, sabotage machinery, scavenge ore, or fight for footing on crumbling ledges high above.
- Quarry: A sheer-walled stone pit with cutting platforms, cranes, and rubble piles, offering cover, climbing routes, and falling-rock hazards. Echoes bounce off bare cliffs and pools gather in the floor. Players climb the faces, hide among blocks, hunt for fine stone, or survive a sudden rockslide.
- Salt Mine: Glittering white tunnels and chambers of crystalline rock salt, dry and echoing, with brine pools and cart rails. Lamps flash off the walls and the air tastes sharp. Players haul loads, find carved chapels or hidden caches, dodge collapses, and avoid parched creatures lurking in the dark.
- Coal Mine: Cramped black shafts with timber supports, minecarts, and lamp light, threatened by gas pockets and cave-ins. Dust coats everything and the dark presses close. Players navigate by flickering lamps, escape explosions, rescue trapped miners, and uncover what the deepest seam has been hiding.
- Gold Mine: Lantern-lit tunnels and sluice works chasing glittering veins, popular with prospectors, claim jumpers, and collapsing timbers. Rumor of a strike can empty a town overnight. Players stake and defend claims, assay ore, ambush rivals, and risk rotten supports to reach the richest seam.
- Diamond Mine: Deep shafts and floodlit pits guarded by fences and checkpoints, where gem hauls attract thieves and heist plots. Every worker is searched and every stone counted. Players plan smuggling schemes, bribe guards, crack vaults, or uncover brutal conditions among the pits and sorting rooms.
- Slag Heap: Smoldering mounds of dark industrial waste, crunchy underfoot, venting fumes and heat, and sheltering scavengers. Glowing cracks and sulfurous smoke mark unstable spots. Players scavenge salvage and metal, hide from patrols in the rubble, risk burns and collapse, and meet desperate people who live on the pile.
- Ore Processing Yard: Noisy crushers, conveyors, and ore stockpiles ringed by trucks and tanks, good for chases and sabotage. Grit blows constantly and machinery drowns out voices. Players ride conveyors, jam crushers, hide behind stockpiles, hijack haulers, and fight among spinning machinery and chemical vats.
- Underground Mine: A sprawling network of shafts, lifts, and rail tunnels with tight lighting, ventilation problems, and lurking cave dwellers. Distance and direction are hard to judge in the maze of galleries. Players manage lamps and air, ride lifts, map abandoned levels, and meet whatever dug up from below.
- Placer Gold Stream: A shallow gravel creek dotted with sluice boxes and panning prospectors, offering small gold flecks and rough frontier camps. Cold water numbs hands and claim stakes line the banks. Players pan for color, trade with camps, defend claims from jumpers, and follow the flakes upstream to their source.
- Clay Pit: A wet red or gray excavation with slick slopes, standing puddles, and brickworks nearby, slowing movement and hiding footprints. Boots stick and tools clog with sludge. Players dig for pottery or brick clay, track or evade pursuers by mud sign, and discover buried relics in the cuttings.
### Manufacturing & Energy
- Foundry: Roaring furnaces and glowing molten metal channels in a soot-blackened hall, with scalding splashes and heavy crucibles. Heat shimmers and sparks drift upward in the din. Players cast metal goods, fight on gantries over molten pours, sabotage furnaces, or race against a failing cooling system.
- Forge: A compact smithy with anvil, bellows, and coal fire, where weapons and tools are crafted, repaired, or upgraded. Hammer rhythms ring out and the glow lights soot-streaked walls. Players commission gear, learn smithing, supply rare metals, or defend a master smith whose craft rivals want.
- Steelworks: A colossal mill of blast furnaces, rolling lines, and gantries spraying sparks, intensely hot and deafening with crushing machinery. Rivers of glowing metal move through the sheds. Players brave catwalks, cut power, fight among rollers and cranes, or infiltrate a plant that arms an empire.
- Smelter: A heat-blasted plant that melts ore into metal, with slag flows, toxic fumes, and cramped catwalks. Ladles tilt and rivulets of fire run through channels. Players steal ingots, disrupt the furnaces, avoid poisonous vapors, and struggle with the harsh working conditions that breed unrest.
- Refinery: A maze of pipes, tanks, and flare stacks processing fuel, prone to explosions, leaks, and fiery set-piece battles. Fumes stink and metal groans in the heat. Players sabotage valves, race along pipe racks, hold the plant against attackers, and flee chain reactions across the complex.
- Oil Field: A flat scorched land of pumpjacks and storage tanks, slick with crude, flammable, and fiercely contested by rival crews. Nodding machines creak and black pools stain the dust. Players seize wells, tap pipelines, sabotage rivals, fight in the fumes, and risk a single spark.
- Drilling Rig: A tall steel derrick on a muddy pad, with pipe racks, noise, and blowout risk, often remote and fortified. Engines thunder as the bit chews deeper. Players defend or seize the site, climb the derrick, survive a gusher, and discover what the drill has opened below.
- Offshore Rig: A steel platform towering over open sea, linked by cranes and helipads, battered by storms and vulnerable to boarding. Waves boom against the legs and gas flares burn at night. Players fight across gantries, repel pirates, escape in lifeboats, and investigate disasters cut off from help.
- Chemical Plant: Rows of reactors and tanks laced with warning signs, leaking colored vapors, toxic spills, and volatile reactions. Pipework twists overhead and alarms sometimes ring. Players need protective gear, sabotage processes, steal formulas, escape toxic clouds, and uncover illicit experiments in locked labs.
- Power Plant: Humming turbine halls, boilers, and pylons behind fences, a strategic target where shutting down generators kills lights. Control rooms glow with gauges and switchboards. Players capture or defend the facility, cut the grid to blind a city, repair failing systems, and sneak through turbine corridors.
- Nuclear Plant: Containment domes and cooling pools behind heavy security, radiating glowing hazard zones, meltdown timers, and fierce lockdowns. Alarms and warning lights cover the long concrete corridors. Players prevent catastrophe, fight through checkpoints, handle radiation exposure, and race a countdown to shut down reactors.
- Hydroelectric Dam: A massive concrete wall holding back a reservoir, with spillways, turbine galleries, and climbable faces above deadly drops. Thundering water vibrates through the structure. Players rappel the face, sabotage turbines, hold the crest against assault, and flee a flood if the wall fails.
- Wind Farm: An open ridge dotted with giant turbines turning slowly, offering sightlines, climbable towers, and steady gusts. Blades whoosh overhead and cables hum in the wind. Players climb for lookouts, ambush beneath rotors, repair or sabotage the grid, and find shelter in the base huts.
- Solar Farm: Acres of glinting panel rows with service lanes, bright reflections, and narrow cover for sneaking or skirmishes. Glare dazzles and heat shimmers off the arrays. Players dodge sentries along the aisles, cut power links, hide under panels, and fight in a landscape of mirrors.
- Factory Floor: Loud assembly lines, robotic arms, and conveyor belts, where moving machinery makes both obstacles and weapons. Sparks and steam break the rhythm of stamping presses. Players ride conveyors, trigger machines against foes, sabotage production, rescue workers, and find the factory's hidden secrets.
- Warehouse: Towering shelves, forklifts, and shipping crates in dim halls, ideal for hide-and-seek, ambushes, and loot hunting. Narrow aisles and shadows hide every corner. Players search inventory for supplies, topple stacks on enemies, sneak between pallets, and fight across mezzanines and loading bays.
- Shipyard: Dry docks, cranes, and half-built hulls beside the water, full of scaffolding, welding sparks, and rigging. Giant ribs of ships rise over busy workers. Players climb unfinished decks, sabotage launches, steal plans, commission vessels, and fight while cranes swing heavy loads overhead.
- Textile Mill: Long halls of looms and spinning machines clattering amid lint and belts, dangerous to workers and fire-prone. Dust floats in the light and the noise is overwhelming. Players navigate tangled machinery, aid exploited workers, start or stop strikes, and escape when flames spread through the cotton.
- Brewery: Warm copper kettles, oak barrels, and malty fog in vaulted cellars, offering supplies, buffs, and drunken brawls. Sweet yeasty steam fills the air. Players sample brews, secure ingredients, hide in the cellars, protect secret recipes, and dodge fights that spill from the taproom.
- Tannery: A foul-smelling yard of lime pits, drying racks, and hides, nauseating to enter and rich in leather goods. Vats of bitter liquid stain everything. Players trade for leather armor, hold their noses to sneak through, discover bodies or secrets in the pits, and endure the reek.
- Sawmill: Riverside timber works with whirring blades, log ponds, and sawdust drifts, hazardous to limb and well stocked with planks. Logs float toward the saws on slow water. Players gather lumber, balance across logs, risk the blades in fights, and hide among stacked timber.
- Windmill: A tall sail-turned tower grinding grain on a breezy hill, with creaking gears, climbable vanes, and a lookout loft. Flour dust drifts and the great sails sweep past windows. Players use the mill as a lookout, ride the vanes, fight among the gears, or hide in the grain.
- Watermill: A wheel-driven mill beside a stream, damp and mossy, with millstones, sluice gates, and flour sacks to hide behind. The wheel splashes and the stones rumble steadily. Players control the water, fight on the wheel, meet a gossipy miller, and find hidden spaces beneath the floorboards.
- Lumber Camp: A forest clearing of bunkhouses, stumps, and log stacks, filled with axes, sleds, and hardy woodsmen. Smoke rises from the cookhouse and the sound of felling carries far. Players work for wages, ride log runs, brawl with tough crews, and uncover what lies deeper in the woods.
- Gas Works: An old industrial plant of big gasometers and pipes, hissing with leaks, ready to burst in chain explosions. A sour smell lingers and rusted valves drip. Players shut down or ignite the system, fight on catwalks above fragile tanks, and flee a blast rippling through the district.
- Cooling Towers: Giant hourglass concrete shells venting steam, echoing within and eerie to climb, suited to hideouts and set-pieces. Mist drifts from the tops and voices bounce strangely inside. Players scale ladders for views, hide fugitives or cults in the hollow bases, and stage battles in their vast shadows.
- Pylon Line: A corridor of high-voltage towers marching across open land, humming and sparking, offering climbs, shock hazards, and long sightlines. Wires crackle in rain and fog. Players climb to scout, cut lines to cause blackouts, follow the corridor across wilderness, and avoid deadly arcs.
- Sewage Works: Tanks, channels, and settling pools reeking of filth, treacherous underfoot, and home to vermin and disease. Gloom and slime coat every surface and the stench never fades. Players wade through channels, find hidden passages to the city, fight rats or mutants, and risk illness for secrets.
- Water Tower: An elevated tank on stilts above a town or rail stop, with ladders, catwalks, and a commanding view. Wood or iron groans in the wind, and water trickles from rusted seams. Players snipe or scout from the platform, cut the supply, hide beneath it, and survive a fall.
## Horror & Gothic
- Haunted House: Creaking rooms with flickering lights, slamming doors, and sudden cold spots fill a decaying home where every hallway feels watched. Poltergeists hurl objects and apparitions grow bolder as nights pass. Players search for clues, survive escalating scares, and uncover the tragedy behind a final reveal.
- Haunted Forest: Gnarled trees, drifting mist, and whispering paths that loop back on themselves make navigation maddening and unreliable. Lost spirits and unseen watchers stalk travelers, drawing them off course. Players hunt for landmarks, protective charms, and a way out before the woods claim them.
- Haunted Mansion: A sprawling decayed estate of grand halls, portraits whose eyes follow you, and hidden passages behind bookcases. Restless ghosts tied to old tragedies drift through the rooms, helping or hindering visitors. Players piece together family secrets, solve puzzles, and lay the spirits to rest.
- Haunted Asylum: Peeling corridors, rusted wheelchairs, and padded cells echo with distant moans and rattling chains. Jump scares lurk behind every door, and disturbing patient records reveal what was done here. Players explore for evidence, dodge vengeful inmates, and decide whether the truth is worth the terror.
- Cursed Village: Silent huts sit under a gloomy sky, with failing crops, marked doors, and an unnatural hush over the lanes. Villagers smile too politely and hide dark secrets about the curse. Players investigate, win trust or break it, and choose between lifting the blight and fleeing.
- Fog-Bound Moor: Sodden heath stretches beneath thick gray fog, riddled with sucking bogs and the distant howls of unseen beasts. Landmarks appear only when it is too late to avoid them. Travelers risk drowning in mire, losing the path, and meeting whatever hunts in the whiteness.
- Vampire Castle: A looming fortress of cold crypts, drafty towers, and candlelit banquet halls ruled by an aristocratic bloodsucker and his loyal servants. Hospitality masks hunger, and dawn is the only real refuge. Players uncover coffins, sabotage the lord's rituals, and escape before nightfall seals the gates.
- Werewolf Woods: A moonlit forest of claw-marked trunks and bone-strewn dens, calm and almost pleasant by day but savagely hunted at night. Tracks, howls, and torn clothing hint at who among the villagers is cursed. Players gather silver, set traps, and race the sunset to find shelter.
- Witch's Cottage: A crooked hut ringed with herbs, hanging charms, and a bubbling cauldron sits deep in the woods, smelling of smoke and strange blossoms. The witch offers brews, bargains, and treacherous hospitality. Players trade favors for cures or knowledge, and must read the fine print of every deal.
- Graveyard at Midnight: Leaning headstones, crooked iron gates, and mist-wrapped mausoleums spread under a pale moon, where lanterns barely push back the dark. The dead rise when disturbed, and fresh digging draws unwanted attention. Players hunt relics, lay ghosts to rest, or survive until the first light of dawn.
- Abandoned Hospital: Dark wards, flickering lamps, and overturned gurneys line long halls where chilling records describe experiments gone wrong. Patients vanish between glances and intercoms crackle with voices. Players scavenge medicine and keycards, dodge jump scares, and trace the disappearances to a hidden basement ward.
- Creepy Carnival: Faded tents, broken rides, and warped calliope music drift over a midway that should have closed long ago. Leering clowns appear in the wrong places and rigged games demand terrible prizes. Players win or lose bizarre bets, ride cursed attractions, and hunt for the ringmaster's secret.
- Cornfield Nightmare: Tall rustling rows hide pursuers and block sight, while scarecrows seem to stand closer each time you look. Trails vanish behind you and the wind carries whispers. Players must navigate by sound and starlight, avoid ambushes in the stalks, and find the clearing at its heart.
- Lovecraftian Sunken Temple: Slime-slick cyclopean halls with impossible carvings sit partly flooded, their proportions wrong and their silence heavy. Whispers erode the sanity of intruders who linger. Players explore for forbidden knowledge and relics, manage dwindling composure, and risk waking something that should stay beneath the water.
- Eldritch Reef: Pulsing coral of unnatural color and glowing eyes grows beneath dark water, swaying in warped currents that tug at divers. Tentacled horrors drift between the spires and sing to the unwary. Players harvest strange pearls and reagents while racing the air supply and their shrinking sanity.
- Non-Euclidean City: Streets bend at impossible angles under shifting skylines, so stairways lead up to basements and corners never meet. Geometry misleads navigation and dread builds with every wrong turn. Players map the city by feel, follow strange signs, and confront whatever architect dreamed this place into being.
- Madness Mountains: Icy peaks and howling winds hide ancient ruins carved before humankind, glittering under thin pale sunlight. The cold drains both body and sanity, and avalanches strike without warning. Players press on for expedition goals, discover forbidden things beneath the ice, and decide whether to wake them.
- Blighted Farmland: Dead fields, rotten grain, and sickly livestock sprawl under a grey sky, marking a curse or spreading infection that no one will name. Wells taste of iron and crows watch silently. Players search for the source, protect or abandon the farmers, and cleanse the land or flee.
- Black Lake: Still ink-dark water without reflection stretches beneath a hushed shoreline, concealing drowned things, sudden drop-offs, and shapes that glide below the surface. Boats drift without wind and ripples appear without cause. Players fish for relics, cross at their peril, and bargain with what dwells in the depths.
- Plague Marsh: A fetid wetland thick with flies and reeking mists, where disease saps health and pyres burn along the banks. Stagnant pools breed fever and the dying call for help from reeds. Players seek cures, ration protective herbs, and decide whom to help while contagion spreads.
- Bone Chapel: A sanctuary decorated with skulls and stacked remains, quiet and solemn, where candlelight turns every wall into patterned death. Restless dead guard cursed relics and punish desecration. Players study the ossuary's meaning, solve its ritual puzzles, and weigh taking powerful treasures against the price of waking the guardians.
- Scarecrow Field: A lonely patch of stakes and ragged effigies stands under circling crows, with straw figures that turn their heads when unobserved. They close in slowly whenever you look away. Players learn to watch them, burn or placate them, and uncover why the farmers fear the harvest.
- Flesh Garden: Wet, pulsing growths of tissue and veins sprout like plants, slippery and warm underfoot, releasing a sweet, rotting smell. Grotesque caretakers tend the beds, and some blossoms hold something moving. Players harvest strange organs for grim crafting, sneak past the gardeners, and decide whether to burn it all.
- Endless Hallway: A repeating corridor with identical doors and flickering lights disorients travelers and slowly bends reality as distance loses meaning. Some doors open to rooms that should not exist. Players hunt for patterns, mark their path, and try to find the one door that leads out.
- Backrooms: A humming fluorescent maze of damp carpet and yellow walls stretches in every direction, empty and unnerving, with no windows and no clear exit. Stalking entities respond to noise and light. Players scavenge supplies, noclip between levels, and map safe zones while sanity and almond water run low.
- Liminal Mall: A deserted shopping center is lit by dying lights and muted music, eerie and silent, its escalators stopped and fountains dry. Lurking shapes wait in dark stores and mannequins change poses. Players scavenge supplies, follow strange announcements, and search for the exit before the closing bell.
- Pool Rooms: Tiled chambers of still turquoise water and echoing drips feel calm yet deeply unsettling, with endless archways and slick floors. Drowning risks and flooded passages hide deep currents. Players swim between rooms, search for dry routes, and avoid whatever disturbs the surface when the lights flicker.
- Abandoned Subway: Dark tunnels, dead trains, and flickering signals lie under the city, with dripping ceilings and graffiti-covered platforms. Feral creatures roam the rails and speeding phantoms rush past without sound. Players restore power, scavenge old stations, and find safe routes to the surface.
- Rotting Castle: A crumbling keep of sagging floors, broken ramparts, and fungus-slick walls leans toward collapse, drafty and damp throughout. Decayed knights still patrol the halls and loose stones fall without warning. Players explore carefully for forgotten treasure, avoid collapse hazards, and face the cursed lord in his ruined throne room.
- Spider Forest: Dense woodland draped in sticky webs and cocooned prey stays dim and silent, with strands that tremble at footsteps. Giant arachnids drop from the canopy to ambush the careless. Players cut through webs, rescue victims, harvest silk and venom, and track the brood mother to her nest.
- Silent Town: Empty streets and shuttered windows lie under a hush with no birds or wind, where footsteps echo and clocks have stopped. Something watches from rooftops and follows at a distance. Players search abandoned homes for clues about the vanished residents and try to leave without breaking the quiet.
- Cabin in the Woods: An isolated timber shelter with creaking floors, a stone hearth, and a hidden cellar offers a brief refuge from the dark outside. Strange noises, old journals, and locked trapdoors suggest it was never truly safe. Players rest, barricade, and eventually uncover the secrets below before they surface.
- Rural Gothic Church: A weathered stone chapel on a lonely hill has shattered stained glass, a grave-lined yard, and a tolling bell that rings with nobody at the rope. Wind moans through the belfry. Players investigate the crypt, confront the troubled priest or spirit, and decide whether the sanctuary protects or traps.
## Genre Settings
### Wild West
- Frontier Saloon Street: A dusty main street of swinging doors, hitching posts, and false-front shops hums with piano music, gossip, and tension. Gunfights, poker games, and bar brawls erupt without warning. Players take bounties, hustle cards, make allies with the sheriff or outlaws, and settle scores at high noon.
- Desert Canyon Trail: A narrow red-rock path winds through dry gulches under blazing sun, with little shade and little water. Its walls are ideal for ambushes, rockslides, and bandit hideouts. Players escort wagons, chase outlaws, scout for water, and hunt for abandoned mines or hidden stashes along the way.
- Rail Outpost: A lonely station and water tank beside the tracks, with freight cars, telegraph lines, and a few weathered buildings. Trains bring cargo, passengers, and trouble. Players guard shipments, plan or stop train robberies, and sabotage telegraph wires to control who knows what.
- Gold Rush Camp: A muddy tent town of prospectors, claim stakes, and sluices, loud with picks and hawkers. The place is thick with greed, swindlers, and sudden violence. Players stake claims, defend them from jumpers, trade nuggets for supplies at high prices, and get rich or end up buried.
- Cattle Drive Plains: Open grassland is crossed by huge herds and chuck wagons beneath wide skies, with distant campfires at night. Stampedes, rustlers, storms, and river crossings threaten the journey. Players ride herd, manage food and morale, fend off thieves, and push the cattle to market on schedule.
- Fort Garrison: A timber-walled army post with a parade ground, watchtowers, and stables stands against the frontier, smelling of horses and gunpowder. It serves as a base, siege target, or supply stop. Players resupply, take scouting missions, defend the walls, or navigate rivalries among officers and settlers.
### Pirate
- Pirate Haven: A lawless harbor town of taverns, black markets, and crooked captains fills the air with rum, rumor, and rattling dice. Rich in rumors, recruits, and double-crosses, it rewards cunning. Players recruit crew, fence plunder, buy maps, and dodge navy spies and angry rivals.
- Treasure Island: A palm-fringed jungle isle with buried chests, cryptic maps, and hidden coves hides danger behind its beaches. Rival crews dig in the sand while traps and betrayal wait inland. Players decode clues, trek through the jungle, race rivals to the mark, and fight over the final prize.
- Ghost Ship Graveyard: Fog-shrouded waters hold wrecked hulls and tattered sails that creak with no wind, drifting in slow circles. Spectral crews haunt the decks and the wrecks seem to shift positions. Players salvage cursed gold at their peril, solve the wrecks' histories, and escape before the fog closes.
- Pirate Cove: A secluded sheltered bay with hidden docks, caves, and lookout bluffs offers calm water and good cover from the navy. Ships hide, trade, and repair here away from prying eyes. Players careen hulls, stash loot, meet smugglers, and defend the cove from discovery.
- Ship Deck: Creaking timber planks, rigging, cannons, and swaying masts form a tight arena for boarding battles, with the sea always at the edge. Players swing on ropes, man cannons, fight across rails, and try not to fall overboard, while deciding when to repair, flee, or board.
- Kraken Waters: A dark stormy ocean with whirlpools and giant tentacles rising beside ships tests nerve and seamanship alike. Waves pound the hull and lightning shows glimpses of vast shapes below. Players steer through the storm, fight sea monsters with harpoons and cannons, and decide what cargo to lose to survive.
### Noir
- Rain-Soaked City: Slick streets, flickering streetlamps, and trench coats appear beneath constant drizzle, with reflections shimmering on wet pavement. The city is bursting with secrets, informants, and shadowy crimes. Players tail suspects, trade favors with shady contacts, and unravel conspiracies that reach from the gutter up to city hall.
- Neon Alley: A narrow backstreet glows with buzzing signs and steam vents, thick with cigarette smoke and cheap perfume. Deals, chases, and muggings unfold in its shadows. Players meet informants, buy whispered secrets, evade pursuers across fire escapes, and pick which streetlight is safe to stand under.
- Docklands Fog: A foggy waterfront of cranes, crates, and mooring ropes muffles sound and hides figures only a few steps away. It is ideal for smuggling, stakeouts, and sudden betrayals. Players track cargo, shadow suspects, search warehouses, and watch their backs on the slick, creaking piers.
- Detective Office: A cramped room of venetian blinds, whiskey, and filing cabinets sits above a noisy street, with a ringing phone and a desk of unpaid bills. Clues, clients, and trouble walk through the door. Players take cases, review evidence, and decide which client to trust.
- Jazz Club Street: A smoky nightlife strip of saxophone music and velvet booths glows with warm light and cool secrets. Gangsters, singers, and whispered informant meetings fill the tables. Players gather gossip, bribe doormen, dodge hired muscle, and look for the one patron who knows too much.
### Steampunk
- Clockwork City: A brass-and-iron metropolis is driven by giant gears and steam pipes, with ticking towers and soot-stained skies over crowded streets. Automatons serve and sometimes malfunction. Players explore workshops, repair machines, navigate guild politics, and uncover who controls the great mechanism that runs the city.
- Airship Dockyard: Towering mooring masts and cargo cranes hold dirigibles as they load, repair, and depart through clouds of steam. Gangways sway in the wind and engines roar. Players hire passage, chase rival captains, sabotage vessels, and plan daring escapes by air.
- Boiler Quarter: A sweltering district of pressure tanks and pipe networks vents scalding steam through narrow alleys and deafening works. Stokers and engineers keep the machinery running, often at great risk. Players navigate hissing pipes, repair leaks, avoid explosions, and discover who profits from the district's dangerous heat.
- Gear Tower: A vertical machine of whirling cogs, pendulums, and narrow ledges rises high above the city, groaning with every turn. A perilous climb where timing platforms matter, each step can crush or save. Players leap between moving parts, time their crossings, and race to the summit mechanism.
- Brass Foundry: A copper-glowing workshop casts gears, pistons, and gadgets amid sparks and the clang of hammers. It is packed with inventors, prototype machines, and hot metal hazards. Players craft or steal inventions, test unstable prototypes, and navigate rivalries between artisans and their jealous patrons.
- Steam Canal: A waterway of paddle boats, pipe bridges, and hissing vents threads through factories along slick walkways. Mist clings to the water and whistles echo off brick. Players pilot boats, chase smugglers along the towpaths, and avoid scalding leaks, rival crews, and collapsing bridges.
### Cyberpunk
- Neon Megacity: A dense vertical sprawl of holographic ads, constant rain, and flying traffic towers over crowded streets. Corporations, gangs, and augmented street life compete for control. Players take jobs for fixers, hack systems, trade favors, and decide how much humanity to trade for chrome and credits.
- Corporate Tower: A gleaming glass skyscraper is full of security checkpoints, drones, and executive suites sealed behind biometric locks. It is a prime infiltration or heist target. Players plan routes, hack cameras, impersonate staff, and steal data from the top floors before alarms bring down the response teams.
- Undercity Slums: Crowded lower levels of cables, noodle stalls, and makeshift housing lack sunlight and breathe damp, neon-lit air. Gangs and back-alley clinics rule daily life. Players find work, buy cheap implants, earn loyalty from locals, and survive turf wars between rival crews.
- Hacker Den: A cramped lair of glowing monitors, tangled wires, and stolen hardware hums with cooling fans and cheap energy drinks. It is used for data runs and underground jobs. Players crack networks, trade exploits, upgrade rigs, and manage risk from counter-hacks and tracing corporations.
- Data Haven: A secure vault of servers and private networks stays hidden from authorities, cool and humming behind layered encryption. It is guarded by ice, rogue programs, and mercenaries. Players negotiate access, hide or retrieve forbidden data, and defend the haven when intruders trace its location.
- Chrome Market: A crowded bazaar sells cybernetic limbs, implants, and weapons under neon signs and flickering holograms. It is thick with shady dealers and bargain hunters. Players haggle for upgrades, risk dangerous black-market tech, test new augmentations, and spot scams before they cost an arm.
### Post-Apocalyptic
- Overgrown City: Collapsed skyscrapers and cracked highways are swallowed by vines and trees, turning old streets into green canyons. Scavengers, mutated beasts, and rich salvage share the ruins. Players hunt for supplies, avoid predators, climb crumbling towers for views, and uncover remnants of the world before.
- Wasteland Highway: A broken road crosses a barren expanse of wrecked cars, raiders, and dust storms, making travel dangerous and fuel precious. Heat shimmers on the cracked asphalt and ambushes wait behind rusting hulks. Players convoy goods, scavenge wrecks, upgrade vehicles, and outrun marauders to the next outpost.
- Survivor Camp: A fortified patchwork of tents, barrels, and scrap walls offers firelight, trade, and rest amid a harsh world. Constant fear of raiders keeps watchers on the walls. Players trade supplies, accept quests, recruit allies, and help the community survive shortages, disease, and attack.
- Bunker Vault: A sealed underground shelter of steel doors and dim corridors holds old supplies, strange residents, and radiation or system failures. Air is stale and lights flicker. Players restore power, unlock sealed sections, uncover secrets of the shelter's purpose, and confront whoever has survived inside.
- Raider Fortress: A spiked scrap-metal stronghold with towers and arena pits echoes with engines and cheering crowds. It is home to brutal gangs and stockpiled loot. Players infiltrate or assault it, fight in gladiator pits, rescue captives, and take down the warlord who rules.
- Flooded Metropolis: A submerged skyline with rooftops as islands and boats threading drowned streets glints under open sky. Hidden hazards lurk beneath murky water, from wires to wreckage to creatures. Players dive into sunken buildings for salvage, navigate by boat, and trade among floating settlements.
### Wuxia & Samurai
- Bamboo Duel Glade: A swaying green grove where masters trade swift sword strokes, with leaping fights, falling leaves, and wind-borne tension. The rustle of stalks masks footsteps and light flickers through gaps. Players test their skills, challenge rivals, observe honor codes, and learn from a master's technique.
- Mountain Sect: A cliffside temple compound of training yards and misty stairs, perched among clouds, rings with chanted drills. It is home to martial masters, rivalries, and secret manuals. Players train, earn rank, undertake trials, and navigate intrigue among elders, disciples, and rival schools.
- Teahouse Town: A lively village of paper lanterns, tea stalls, and wandering swordsmen bustles with chatter and steam. Rumors, duels, and fateful meetings begin here. Players gather news, meet masters and rogues, accept missions, and settle disputes with a cup of tea or a sudden blade.
- Riverside Inn: A wooden lodging by a quiet stream offers rest, warm food, and gossip to travelers and wandering fighters. The calm holds until rival fighters or assassins arrive. Players listen to stories, protect guests, defend the inn in a night fight, and decide whom to trust.
### Space Opera
- Cantina Port: A dim alien bar at a spaceport, thick with smoke, strange music, and a crowd of smugglers, pilots, and bounty hunters, buzzing with rumors and sudden brawls. Hired guns and quiet informants share the booths. Players pick up jobs, trade secrets, and risk a bar fight or a bounty.
- Trade Station: An orbital hub of docking rings, cargo bays, and bustling markets, welcoming merchants, pirates, and diplomats from many worlds. Announcements echo through crowded concourses while customs officers look the other way for a price. Players haggle for cargo, broker deals, and stumble into smuggling plots.
- Imperial Throne World: A gleaming capital planet of grand palaces, orbiting fleets, and marble boulevards, ruled by an emperor's court of intrigue and iron rule. Spies, nobles, and officers watch every move. Players can climb the court's ladder, plot against rivals, or sneak through fortified halls toward a coup.
- Smuggler Moon: A rugged, lawless satellite of hidden hangars, dusty cantinas, and cratered landing pads, outside any authority and favored by outlaws and fugitives. Patrols rarely visit, and loyalty is always for sale. Players hide from pursuers, fence stolen cargo, and sign on for risky runs past blockades.
### Other Genres
- Dieselpunk City: A grimy art-deco metropolis of smokestacks, armored trucks, and propaganda posters, driven by oil, war machines, and gritty ambition. Soot hangs over the avenues and factory sirens mark the shifts. Players work with gangs, mechanics, and officers, chasing contracts through a city on a permanent war footing.
- Solarpunk Eco-City: A bright green city of rooftop gardens, wind towers, and shared canals, balancing technology and nature in hopeful design.
